Choate's 2005 paper for Congress's China commission described the patent harvest and recommended fixes. \[16\] The Trade Representative's 2018 report catalogued the transfer methods in detail. \[11\] In January 2021 the Patent and Trademark Office published its own study of the non-market factors behind mainland filing volume. The mainland's patent office ordered its subsidies cut two weeks later. \[13\]

Each finding lived in its owner's records. The public record shows no document that tied any of them to a response owned across the seven channels.

#### Application Architecture. The Routines the Defenders Ran

The Micron dispute ran through six instruments in three legal systems.

**The Micron clock**

| Date | Instrument holder | Instrument | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| August 2017 | Taiwan prosecutors | Indictment of UMC and three engineers \[3\] | Convictions in June 2020, reduced on retrial in January 2022 |
| December 2017 | Micron | Civil suit in California \[3\] | Settled with UMC in November 2021 and with Jinhua in December 2023 \[8\] |
| January 2018 | UMC and Jinhua | Patent suits in Fuzhou \[4\] | Injunction on July 3, 2018, after 172 days |
| October 2018 | Commerce Department | Entity List designation of Jinhua \[5\] | The plant lost its American tools at once |
| November 2018 | Justice Department | Indictment unsealed \[6\] | UMC plea in October 2020, Jinhua acquitted in February 2024 \[7\] \[8\] |
| March 2023 | Cyberspace Administration | Security review of Micron \[9\] | Purchase ban on May 21, 2023, after 51 days |

The instruments that needed no trial landed in months or at once. The American criminal case ran more than five years and lost against the Party's plant. The Taiwan case reached the engineers in Taiwan and ended in reduced sentences.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms the Defenders Used

The defenders' one working layer was technology. American firms make tools that every advanced chip plant needs, and the Entity List turned that position into a control that required no trial. \[5\] The same firms sell and build on the mainland, which puts them on the Party's platforms for revenue. Mainland China supplied 12 percent of Micron's revenue in the year before the 2025 report of its exit. \[9\]

#### Principles and Governance. What Held the Defenders Together

The defenders kept their principles. Courts demanded proof, trade law demanded findings and research stayed open. Those principles cost time, and they are worth the cost. No venue held the seven channels together, and the one program built for the purpose lasted less than three and a half years.

### 4. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | Plans run on fifteen-year horizons and ten-year targets | Cases and trade reviews run on dockets and statutory cycles |
| **Entropy** | One set of plans and few handoffs | Seven owners and a handoff at every channel |
| **Topology** | A star with the plans at the hub | A mesh of firms, agencies and laboratories |
| **Asymmetry** | Absorbs what the other side publishes | Publishes worldwide and protects only where it files |
| **Agency** | Turns ordinary career choices into channels | Leaves each engineer and each firm to decide alone |
| **Resonance** | Patents, courts, capital and recruitment pointed one way | Seven instruments aimed separately at one campaign |
| **Phase** | Court in 172 days, regulator in 51 | Criminal case in more than five years |
| **Gradient** | Salaries and market access tilt each choice | Firms carry the retaliation risk alone |
| **Criticality** | Aims at memory, processors and AI infrastructure | Holds the chokepoint in tools and uses it well |
| **Coherence** | Governed by published plans under one authority | No owner of the whole |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. No Owner of the Transfer

*Architecture Governance*

**Documented.** Seven channels carried the transfer, and seven owners held the defense. The Justice Department's China Initiative came nearest to an owner of the whole. It sat inside one department, and the Department ended it after three years. The Trade Representative found in 2024 that the Party had not eliminated many of the practices its tariffs targeted. \[6\] \[11\]

**Argued.** Each owner behaved correctly inside its own remit. The patent office examined, Commerce designated, Justice prosecuted and the firm sued. Nobody held the capability map, so nobody could see seven correct actions answering one campaign on seven separate clocks. The Party never had this gap, because one authority writes its plans.

#### Gap 2. The Slowest Instrument Held the Main Line

*Architecture Principles, Phase*

**Documented.** The Entity List designation took effect on October 29, 2018, and stranded Jinhua's plant. The Justice Department unsealed the indictment and launched the China Initiative three days later. The criminal case against Jinhua ended in acquittal more than five years after that. The Party's court and regulator reached Micron in 172 days and fifty-one days. \[4\] \[5\] \[6\] \[8\] \[9\]

**Argued.** The defenders' fast instrument was administrative and their slow one was judicial. The program that framed the response put the slow one first. The Party ran its court and its regulator as administrative instruments, so both its answers landed within a year. Decide by designation where the facts support it, and keep the courts for the cases they can reach.

#### Gap 3. The Library With No Librarian

*Data Architecture. The signature gap*

Franklin built an efficient stove in 1742 and turned down a patent for it. He wrote that "as we enjoy great advantages from the inventions of others, we should be glad of an opportunity to serve others by any invention of ours." \[20\] The choice to publish rather than hoard is an American inheritance, not an oversight, and the gap that follows is its price.

**Documented.** The American Inventors Protection Act of 1999 publishes most applications eighteen months after filing. An applicant who files only in the United States may opt out. Foreign offices publish the applications of anyone who files abroad. A patent protects its owner only in the countries where it issues. The only security screen, the secrecy order, applies to single inventions judged harmful to national security. \[17\] Choate warned in 2005 that mainland firms were mining the published library. Congress moved the country to first-to-file in 2011, the change he had warned against. \[16\] \[17\]

**Argued.** The defenders' controls inspect items, and the harvest takes the aggregate. A secrecy order can stop one weapon design. Nothing watches what a million published applications teach a reader who files nothing in return. Disclosure is global and protection is territorial, so every published application with no foreign counterpart teaches the world and protects its owner only at home. The library needs no lock, because an open library is what patents exist to build. It needs a librarian who knows what the readers take.

#### Gap 4. The Firm Negotiates Alone

*Business Architecture, Economic*

**Documented.** The Trade Representative found in 2018 that the Party used joint-venture requirements and licensing approvals to pressure foreign firms into transferring technology. \[11\] Micron sued in California and saw the Party's court enjoin its products within seven months. Micron later became the first American chipmaker the Party's regulator targeted. Reuters reported in 2025 that it would leave the mainland's server-chip market. \[4\] \[9\]

**Argued.** The Party bargains as one buyer with one set of plans. The defenders send firms to the table one at a time, and each firm carries its own retaliation risk. Micron did what the defenders' architecture asked of it. It sued, it cooperated, and it absorbed the retaliation alone. The Gradient principle predicts the next firm's choice, which is silence.

#### Gap 5. The Court Reaches Only Its Own Jurisdiction

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** A jury convicted Linwei Ding in January 2026 after he took Google's files while living and working in California. \[15\] The American trial of the Jinhua case in 2024 tried the company alone, and the court acquitted it. \[8\] The Taiwan court reached the engineers in Taiwan. \[3\] The China Initiative brought more than seventy prosecutions. Critics said it drifted toward academics inside the United States charged with failing to disclose foreign ties. \[6\]

**Argued.** Prosecution works where the defendant, the evidence and the witnesses sit inside the jurisdiction. It weakens as each of them moves abroad. A routine that selects cases by what it can reach will drift toward the people nearest at hand, and the China Initiative did. Research security belongs at the level of funding and direction, never at the level of ancestry. Designation belongs to the entities abroad that no court will reach.

### 5. Target Architecture

The target keeps the openness that makes American science what it is. It changes who owns the defense and which instrument leads.

**Gap to target**

| Gap | Target element | Owner | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. No owner of the transfer | One technology-transfer map across the seven channels, with published thresholds | National Strategic Competition Council, convening only | A statute that outlives an election cycle, and agencies that share their agendas |
| 2. The slowest instrument held the main line | Designations approved in advance against published triggers, with prosecution in support | Commerce, with Justice and the Trade Representative | Errors that land on firms named wrongly, and retaliation against American firms |
| 3. The library with no librarian | A registry of what critical-technology applications publish without foreign protection, and shorter examination | Patent and Trademark Office, reporting to the council | Examiner salaries, and inventors who keep more as trade secrets |
| 4. The firm negotiates alone | A confidential channel for transfer demands, and a response package when the Party punishes a cooperating firm | Trade Representative and Commerce | Escalation, and firms that still prefer silence |
| 5. The court reaches only its own jurisdiction | Prosecution for people and firms inside, designation for entities outside, and joint case files with allies | Justice and Commerce, with allied prosecutors | Fewer headline cases, and allies' different rules of evidence |

**Pre-committed triggers already work.** The Entity List stranded Jinhua's plant without a trial. The Holding Foreign Companies Accountable Act of 2020 set an automatic trading ban for issuers whose auditors the inspection board could not examine. In December 2022 the board reported complete access to inspect audit firms on the mainland and in Hong Kong for the first time. \[18\]

**Designation pays for speed with error.** A designation needs no trial, which is why it is fast and why it can be wrong. The target buys that speed with published criteria and an appeal route.

**The firm should not stand alone.** A firm that helps American law enforcement and then suffers the Party's retaliation carries a cost the public imposed. A response agreed in advance tells the Party the price before it acts.

**The library stays open.** Every major patent office publishes at eighteen months, so closing the American library alone would change little. The librarian's work is smaller and cheaper. Know what the critical fields publish without foreign protection, shorten the window before grant, and make sure inventors who file only at home know they may opt out.

**What the target would not have done.** It would not have undone the transfer into UMC, and it would not have made the Jinhua case provable. It would have put the fast instrument first, shared the retaliation risk and shown the defenders what their library teaches. That claim remains an argument.

### What Technology IP Proves

Technology IP proves Asymmetry. The American system publishes to the world and protects its inventors only at home, and the Party built a method on that difference.

It proves Phase again. The Party's court and regulator landed in months, the American trial took more than five years, and the fast American instrument was the one that needed no trial.

It also proves something Hong Kong could not. The defenders win where they own the chokepoint. Tools gave Commerce a control the Party could not route around, and the Entity List stranded a five-billion-dollar plant. Every other layer ran on someone else's clock or someone else's platform.

Micron spent the money and the years the invention required. The country that built the memory chip can still decide whether the next one moves in only one direction. A courtroom will not make that decision for it.

### Field Card

**What happened.** Between 2016 and 2018, a state-funded mainland chip plant used a Taiwanese contractor and two engineers who had copied files from an American competitor to build technology it could not develop on its own. American and Taiwanese courts convicted the contractor, but a federal court acquitted the mainland plant itself in 2024. Commerce's Entity List had already stranded the plant in 2018, years before any court ruled.

**The gap.** The Data layer had no librarian for what a million published applications teach a reader who files nothing in return.

**The fix.** A registry that tracks what critical-technology fields publish without foreign patent protection, paired with a shorter examination window, would close the gap the courts never could.

**Where you could work on it.** Patent examination and research-security policy, export-control compliance at a technology firm, or trade law focused on Section 301 and Entity List designations.

*Sourcing notes and the full corrections record for this chapter appear in Appendix H.*

# Part II. Totality

## Total Domain War, Not Asymmetric War

*The West Filed the Method Under the Wrong Heading*

On April 15, 2014, Xi Jinping presided over the first meeting of a commission that had not existed eighteen months earlier. \[1\] The Central National Security Commission had one item on its agenda that mattered beyond the room. Xi introduced a concept he called the overall national security outlook, in Chinese *zongti guojia anquan guan*. *Zongti* can also be rendered total. \[1\]

The word choice was not incidental. The rest of this chapter reads it as a claim about method, not a slogan.

### The Western Frame

For most of the past three decades the West has filed the Party's methods under asymmetric warfare, hybrid warfare, or the gray zone. Each label assumes the same shape underneath it. A weaker side, unable to win a direct contest, reaches for tricks at the margins.

That assumption rests on real scholarship and answers a real question. Ivan Arreguín-Toft's *How the Weak Win Wars* (2005) built a rigorous account of how materially inferior actors defeat stronger ones, and the asymmetry dimension in this framework's own model owes that literature a debt. \[2\] The question the asymmetry literature answers is how the weak beat the strong, a real question with a real answer. Nothing in this chapter argues otherwise.

*Unrestricted Warfare* fits the same frame, and the frame has misled a generation of its Western readers. Qiao Liang and Wang Xiangsui published the book in 1999 as two PLA colonels answering a question their own military faced directly. Beijing had watched the Gulf War of 1991 and concluded that no force on earth could out-fight the American military machine in a straight contest. \[3\] The book that followed reads, correctly, as a weaker power's manual for winning without fighting that contest. Western reviewers filed it as an asymmetric warfare text, and for the purpose its authors wrote it, the filing was accurate.

The error is assuming the frame that fit a weaker power in 1999 still fits the party that runs the campaign today.

### The Party's Own Frame Is Totality

Four documents, read together, show the shift.

**Documented.** On April 15, 2014, Xi Jinping proposed the overall national security outlook at the first meeting of the Central National Security Commission. \[1\] The concept named political and military security, economic and cultural security, and scientific security as one connected problem. The list of named fields has grown since. \[1\] The National Security Law of July 2015 codified the outlook in statute. Article 11 assigns every citizen, every state body, every enterprise and every social organization in the country a duty to safeguard national security. \[4\] The Party's own constitution, amended at the 19th Congress in October 2017, states that the Party exercises overall leadership over every area of endeavor in every part of the country. \[5\] Freedom House's 2021 case study on transnational repression found that the Party runs the most sophisticated, global and comprehensive campaign of its kind anywhere in the world. \[6\]

**Argued.** Read apart, each document records a policy choice inside its own field. Read together, they describe one design decision applied everywhere at once. A security concept that names political and military fields, economic and cultural fields, and scientific fields in a single breath does not leave a field outside its reach to discover later. A constitution that claims leadership over every area of endeavor carves out no exception for finance, or standards, or a patent office. The Party did not arrive at totality by accumulating decisions one domain at a time, the way the record shows the West accumulating its own Enclosure. It named the totality first, in 2014, and has worked outward from the name since.

### The Framework Already Encodes It

A reader who has reached this chapter with the rest of the framework in hand has already met this argument once. Asymmetry is one dimension of ten, not the whole physics of the contest. Coherence is the tenth dimension and the master dimension, because it decides whether the other nine multiply or cancel. A total system running under one aim beats a divided system running nine separate aims, even where the divided side holds more resources in every domain measured separately. The model already says what the Party's own documents say about themselves. What this chapter corrects is the reader's frame, not the model.

### Total War Is Not New, and It Is Not Chinese

Erich Ludendorff named total war in 1935, in a book written by a general who had run Germany's own war economy two decades earlier and concluded that the next war would leave no civilian institution untouched. \[7\] The United States then ran total war for real, from 1941 to 1945. It fused industry and science, finance and information, and culture and arms into a single instrument that out-produced the Axis powers combined in munitions. Total war belongs to no single nation and no single century.

What is new is total war run in peacetime, across civilian domains, by a party that answers to no electorate and no court. Robert Jay Lifton spent years after the Korean War interviewing people who had passed through Chinese thought reform, and in 1961 he published eight criteria describing what he found in them. Milieu control. Mystical manipulation. The demand for purity. The cult of confession. Sacred science. The loading of the language. Doctrine placed over the person. The dispensing of existence itself to those the group casts out. \[8\] Lifton wrote that checklist to describe what a totalist system does to one mind at a time. The chapters ahead describe what the same design does to twelve domains at once.

Call it, once, what it is. Total totalitarian totality carries no alliteration for its own sake. It stacks three separate documented facts on top of each other, and this chapter has now supplied all three.

### The Reader's Redesign

A total system beats a divided one on first contact, and keeps beating it for as long as the divided side stays divided. Nothing about that outcome passes judgment on the divided side's people, its wealth or its ideas. The judgment falls on its wiring.

The chapters ahead trace that wiring failing in one domain at a time. The pages after that price out what rewiring it would cost. A total system defeats a divided one, and the fix available to the divided side has never been to become total in return. The fix is to stop being divided.

### Sourcing Status: Total Domain War, Not Asymmetric War

**Documented.**

**\[1\]** Xi Jinping proposed the overall national security outlook (*zongti guojia anquan guan*) at the first meeting of the Central National Security Commission on April 15, 2014. The commission had been announced at the Third Plenum of the 18th Party Congress in November 2013 and did not convene until that date. Contemporaneous and later Party sources, including CSIS's Interpret China translation series and China Daily's 2024 retrospective, confirm the date and the concept's growth from an initial set of named fields to roughly twenty by 2024.

**\[2\]** Ivan Arreguín-Toft, *How the Weak Win Wars* (Cambridge University Press, 2005).

**\[3\]** Qiao Liang and Wang Xiangsui, *Unrestricted Warfare* (PLA Literature and Arts Publishing House, 1999). Both authors held the rank of colonel in the People's Liberation Army at the time of publication.

**\[4\]** National Security Law of the People's Republic of China, adopted by the Standing Committee of the 12th National People's Congress on July 1, 2015. Article 11's text is drawn from the Stanford DigiChina and China Law Translate renderings, which agree in substance.

**\[5\]** Resolution of the 19th National Congress of the Communist Party of China on the Amendment to the Constitution of the Communist Party of China, October 24, 2017. The quoted formulation, that the Party exercises overall leadership over all areas of endeavor in every part of the country, appears in the Congress's own report and resolution. Reuters and other outlets reported it at the time.

**\[6\]** Freedom House published its China case study on transnational repression in February 2021, part of a broader report series on transnational repression worldwide.

**\[7\]** Erich Ludendorff, *Der totale Krieg* (Munich, 1935).

**\[8\]** Robert Jay Lifton, *Thought Reform and the Psychology of Totalism* (1961), chapter 22.

**Stated as inference, not finding.** That the four documents in this chapter reflect one coordinated design decision, rather than four separate agencies converging by coincidence on similar language, is the chapter's argument. The dates, the texts and the translations are documented. The claim of a single design behind them is this framework's reading, offered for the reader to weigh against the alternative that a totalist party produces totalist language by habit, in which case the outcome for the defender is the same either way.

## We Built This

*The Country That Ran the Method Best Stopped Running It*

The strongest evidence in this book is not Chinese. It comes from what the United States did between 1941 and 1945, and from what the United States did to itself in the decades after.

### The Arsenal

Read domain by domain, the American war effort is total domain war in its purest expression, and a reader is entitled to ask why a method the country once ran at the largest scale in history now reads as foreign.

**Economic.** The War Production Board converted civilian industry wholesale. Automobile plants stopped making cars in February 1942 and made tanks, aircraft and trucks instead. In April 1944, Ford's Willow Run plant produced 453 aircraft in 468 hours, one finished B-24 every sixty-three minutes. The plant accounted for roughly half of all B-24s assembled that year. \[1\] American yards drove Liberty ship construction from roughly 355 days early in the program to about 42 days by 1943, against the ten to twelve months an oceangoing ship had required in 1917. \[2\] By 1944 American munitions output exceeded that of the Axis powers combined. \[3\] This was the deliberate conversion of an economy into an arsenal, directed from the center, not the market operating on its own.

**Scientific.** Vannevar Bush directed the Office of Scientific Research and Development, which fused universities, industry and the military into one research instrument. \[4\] Radar, the proximity fuze and mass-produced penicillin each changed the war. The Manhattan Project sits at the extreme end. Peak employment reached 130,000 and total spending reached \$2.2 billion, but turnover means the true figure runs closer to half a million Americans who worked on some part of the project, near one percent of the wartime civilian labor force. \[5\] No single domain built the bomb. Physics and industry, security and logistics, and command fused into one operation.

**Financial.** War bonds pulled savings out of the population and into the war effort, absorbing purchasing power that would otherwise have driven inflation. At Bretton Woods in July 1944, before the war was won, the United States wrote the financial architecture of the world that would follow it. \[6\] This domain was not merely funding a war already underway. The same dollars were building the peace still to come.

**Information, intelligence and coalition.** The Office of War Information shaped the domestic narrative, and Frank Capra's *Why We Fight* turned the machinery of entertainment into the machinery of morale. \[7\] The Office of Strategic Services fused espionage and sabotage with analysis and unconventional warfare in one organization. \[8\] Codebreaking gave commanders the enemy's intentions in near real time. The Grand Alliance held three powers with irreconcilable long-term aims together across Tehran, Yalta and a continuous machinery of conferences, with Lend-Lease arming allies as an instrument of strategy in its own right. \[9\]

Domains are where the war was fought. Coherence is why it compounded. Pearl Harbor did in a single morning what years of argument could not. It aligned a fractious, regionally divided democracy into near-total unity, and for four years the country ran at a level of coherence it has never reached before or since. \[10\] The reversal matters more than the achievement. America did not win by staying the decentralized system it had always been. For the duration it became a centrally coordinated system, then chose to change back once the winning stopped. \[11\]

### The Enclosure

A pen is not a trap. A trap springs shut. A pen is built.

The men who take wild hogs in Texas do not chase them. Chasing scatters a sounder across a hundred square miles and teaches every animal in it to fear the open ground. The professionals build a corral instead, in ground the hogs already cross. They leave the gate wide and put out corn. The animals come. Nothing happens. They come back. Nothing happens again. Over some weeks the enclosure stops reading as a structure and becomes a feature of the landscape, no more remarkable than a creek bed. Then a man closes the gate, and it takes about a second.

**Argued.** The animals inside were not stupid. They reasoned correctly the entire time from the evidence available to them. Corn appeared and nothing bad followed. The panels were always there and never did anything. The conclusion drawn from a hundred safe visits was the wrong conclusion, not because the reasoning failed but because the relevant fact never entered the frame.

America built itself a pen in the decades after the coherence described above, six panels, each raised for a reason that was defensible at the time.

**The money power.** The capacity to create money moved decisively toward the financial sector and away from the productive one, a reversal within living memory. \[12\] New money reaches finance first.

**The multiplication.** The Federal Register ran 2,620 pages in 1936 and 80,756 pages in 2022. \[13\] Congress has passed roughly ninety-two thousand rules since 1996 and repealed sixteen. Each rule answered a real failure. The aggregate answers nothing.

**The license.** Occupational licensing covered about five percent of American workers in the 1950s and covers roughly a quarter now. \[14\] Part of the rise is mechanical, tracking the shift from making goods to providing services. The rest is not mechanical, and the measured effect includes barriers to entry that incumbents write and enforce.

**The credential.** A private accreditor, answerable to no electorate, stands between a person and a public function. The Department of Veterans Affairs requires a social worker at a Vet Center to hold a master's degree from a program accredited by one specific body, and a doctorate does not satisfy the requirement. \[15\] That provision cannot be a competence rule. It functions as a credential rule instead, and no elected official reviews what the credentialer requires.

**The curriculum.** Congress passed Goldwater-Nichols in 1986 for one reason. The armed services had specialized past the point of being able to fight together. \[16\] The law fixed jointness among the services. It left the boundary between the military and everything else exactly where it found it.

**The lane.** Work that crosses disciplinary boundaries carries a measurably lower probability of funding. A study of 18,476 proposals submitted to the Australian Research Council's Discovery Programme over five years found the penalty consistent, falling hardest on the young researcher least able to absorb it. \[17\] Nobody forbids the crossing. The slope simply runs the other way.

**Argued.** No adversary built any of these six panels. Most were built by people trying to protect the public, and many succeeded at exactly what they were built to do. This book does not manufacture a conspiracy where none exists. Nobody convened, and no document ordered the result. Several million people hold the pen in place today, each making a locally rational decision inside incentives none of them designed. Most would defend their own panel sincerely, and correctly on its own narrow terms.

That is the harder problem, not the softer one. A plot can be exposed, and exposure ends it. An accumulation has no author to expose, no meeting to reveal and no leak to publish.

### The Country Once Named This

One document shows the forgetting was not total, or not immediate.

**Documented.** George Kennan directed the State Department's Policy Planning Staff. On May 4, 1948, he sent the National Security Council a memorandum titled "The Inauguration of Organized Political Warfare." \[18\] He wrote that political warfare is "the employment of all the means at a nation's command, short of war, to achieve its national objectives." \[18\] He listed six means in one breath. Political alliances and economic measures such as the Marshall Plan. Open propaganda and clandestine support for friendly foreign movements. Covert psychological operations and encouragement of resistance inside hostile states.

**Argued.** Kennan was not describing a future adversary's playbook. He was naming an instrument for his own government to build, three years after the arsenal above had already proven the country could run every domain at once. The memorandum shows that the whole-of-government habit did not vanish the moment the shooting stopped in 1945. It survived at least until 1948, in the mind of the diplomat who had just finished explaining Soviet behavior to Washington, before the six panels described above had fully closed.

What Kennan asked for in 1948 and what the country actually built afterward are not the same thing, and the gap between the request and the result is this chapter's real subject. The men who ran the arsenal knew how to fuse every domain into one instrument. The man who asked to keep doing it on purpose, in peacetime, got a fraction of what he asked for. The rest of what he asked for is what the chapters ahead find missing.

### Sourcing Status: We Built This

**Documented.**

**\[1\]** The Henry Ford, Willow Run Bomber Plant collection, April 1944 production figures. Detroit Historical Society, *Encyclopedia of Detroit*, Willow Run entry.

**\[2\]** U.S. Maritime Commission program records. The SS *Robert E. Peary*'s four-day launch is deliberately excluded here, since it was a staged publicity competition between yards and belongs to the information domain, not the economic one.

**\[3\]** Comparative munitions tables appear in Mark Harrison's edited volume *The Economics of World War II* (Cambridge, 1998). Scoped deliberately to munitions output against the Axis combined, not total production against every combatant, a broader formulation that does not survive specification.

**\[4\]** Executive Order 8807 established the Office of Scientific Research and Development on June 28, 1941. The National Defense Research Committee came first, created June 27, 1940. Vannevar Bush directed both.

**\[5\]** The 130,000 peak and \$2.2 billion figures come from the U.S. Department of Energy's Office of Legacy Management, in its Manhattan Project background materials. Alex Wellerstein, *Restricted Data*, supplies the peak-versus-cumulative distinction.

**\[6\]** Bretton Woods Agreements Act, 1945.

**\[7\]** Office of War Information, Executive Order 9182. *Why We Fight* series produced for the War Department.

**\[8\]** Office of Strategic Services records, National Archives Record Group 226.

**\[9\]** Lend-Lease Act, Pub. L. 77-11, March 11, 1941.

**\[10\]** The attack on Pearl Harbor came December 7, 1941. The domestic political realignment that followed it is documented across standard wartime histories.

**\[11\]** Executive Order 9024, establishing the War Production Board. Executive Order 8807, establishing the OSRD. Both bodies were wound down within roughly two years of Japan's surrender.

**\[12\]** Bureau of Economic Analysis, GDP by Industry series.

**\[13\]** Office of the Federal Register, annual page and document statistics.

**\[14\]** The Treasury Department, the Council of Economic Advisers and the Labor Department jointly published the *Occupational Licensing* report in July 2015. Underlying academic work appears in Kleiner and Krueger, NBER Working Paper No. 14308.

**\[15\]** Department of Veterans Affairs social work qualification standards, published.

**\[16\]** Goldwater-Nichols Department of Defense Reorganization Act of 1986, Pub. L. 99-433. **Carried forward from the first edition.** The exact signing date is contested between secondary sources by a few days, so the text states only the year, consistent with the first edition's own deferral on this point.

**\[17\]** Bromham, Dinnage and Hua published "Interdisciplinary research has consistently lower funding success" in *Nature* 534 (2016).

**\[18\]** George F. Kennan wrote "The Inauguration of Organized Political Warfare" as a Policy Planning Staff memorandum dated May 4, 1948. National Archives and Records Administration, RG 273, Records of the National Security Council. Published in *Foreign Relations of the United States, Emergence of the Intelligence Establishment*, document 269. **New in this edition.**

**Narrowed or removed from the first edition.** The first edition's Part One also covered Grant and Sherman, Pershing, Mao and Giap in Chapters 1 through 3 and Chapter 5. That material now lives in the Prologue's Four Boxes instead of repeating here. The claim that three out of four wartime American physicists worked under the National Defense Research Committee is not repeated, since it traces to a secondary source in Jill Lepore's *These Truths* rather than a primary count.

**Stated as inference, not finding.** That six independently motivated panels produce an aggregate condition none of their builders intended is this chapter's argument, not a documented finding. That the gap between Kennan's 1948 request and what the country actually built is the book's real subject is this chapter's framing choice, offered to the reader rather than proven to the reader.

## How to Read the Machine

*A Field Card Before the Cases Start*

The Stack explained the architecture. This chapter is shorter, and it exists for one purpose. Every case chapter from here forward uses the same four vocabularies and the same five-step reading order, and a reader who has them in hand before Part III starts will not need to flip back for them.

### The Four Vocabularies, in Brief

**Ten dimensions.** Time. Entropy. Topology. Asymmetry. Agency. Resonance. Phase. Gradient. Criticality. Coherence. These are properties of a contest, not places within it, and they decide whether an actor's advantages multiply or cancel. Coherence is the master dimension. The full table, with the question each dimension asks and the principle it sets, lives in Appendix C.

**Twelve domains.** Kinetic. Economic. Financial. Information. Psychological. Legal. Association. Cyber. Cultural. AI and Cognitive. Biological. Data and Surveillance. These are the arenas where the dimensions become concrete, and every case names which domains it uses. The full table lives in Appendix C alongside the dimensions.

**Thirty-six stratagems.** A finite catalog of techniques sorted into six groups by the position an actor holds when it reaches for one, from Superiority through Desperation. A case names the stratagem it finds at work the way a security team names a technique from an attack framework, not as a literary flourish. The full catalog, with the Taiwan case mapped against it in full, lives in Appendix B.

**Five views.** Every case runs the same order. Architecture Vision states what the Party's enterprise set out to achieve. The Party's baseline records what it fielded, layer by layer. The defenders' baseline records the same layers on the side under attack, with an owner named for each. Gap analysis locates every seam the campaign used. Target architecture states what would close each gap and what it would cost. A reader who loses the thread in the middle of a case can always ask which of these five sections they are in.

### A Note for the Military Reader

This framework is not a competitor to your doctrine, and it does not ask you to set your own vocabulary aside. The ten dimensions map onto the nine principles of war and onto joint doctrine's own functions, and Appendix D lays out that crosswalk in full. One asymmetry in the doctrine itself is worth knowing before you get there. Joint doctrine added information as a seventh joint function in 2017. The Army's own warfighting functions, descended from the six that replaced the old Battlefield Operating Systems in 2008, have not followed suit and still number six. Where your own service's doctrine and the joint level disagree with each other, this framework's crosswalk in Appendix D notes the disagreement rather than picking a side.

### Where This Leaves the Reader

None of the four vocabularies above does any work on its own. A dimension without a domain is an abstraction. A domain without a stratagem is an empty arena. A stratagem without a target architecture is a description with no fix attached. The cases ahead put all four to work together, case by case, and the five-view order is what holds them in a shape a reader can follow from one chapter to the next.

Part III begins the cases proper.

# Part III. The Lifecycle

## Attract

*The Borrowed Knife*

**DOMAINS** Economic · **Financial (spine)** · Information · Legal · Association · Data and Surveillance

**DIMENSIONS** Time · Entropy · Topology · Asymmetry · Agency · Resonance · Phase · **Gradient (signature)** · Criticality · Coherence

**STRATAGEMS** Spine S1 · S3 · S9 · S11 · S35

**GAPS** Six, one at each layer. Signature gap at the Principles layer, where every gate earned a fee for opening and none for staying shut.

In 58 BC a delegation of Aedui chiefs came to Julius Caesar in tears and begged for help. Diviciacus, their leader, said the choice was stark. Unless Caesar's army intervened, the Gauls would have to abandon their homeland the way the Helvetii already had. \[22\] Caesar intervened. He broke Ariovistus and drove the Germans back across the Rhine. He did not then leave. Caesar spent the next seven years conquering the rest of Gaul, the Aedui included, and by 52 BC the same tribe that had begged him for protection stood in revolt against him at Alesia and lost.

The Aedui invited a protector because they needed one, and the protector never left. That arc, invitation to conquest, is the shape of the five chapters ahead. Part Three follows one campaign through five stages. Attract. Accommodate. Control. Extract. Consume. This chapter is the first stage, and the knife the Party borrowed was never stolen. Collaborators handed it over, one fee at a time.

On September 19, 2014, a retail investor anywhere in America could buy a hundred shares of Alibaba. The offering raised $21.8 billion, the largest initial public offering in history to that point. Six of Wall Street's largest banks stood on the cover. \[1\]

He did not buy a hundred shares of Alibaba. He bought shares in a Cayman Islands holding company that owned no part of Alibaba's Chinese operating business and never had. The holding company owned a stack of contracts. Service agreements, powers of attorney and equity pledges routed the operating company's economics offshore without transferring its ownership. The accounting rules let the shell consolidate the operating company, so the statements read as though the investor owned a piece of it. He owned a claim on a claim.

The prospectus disclosed the structure deep in its pages. Seven years later the chairman of the Securities and Exchange Commission put the buried fact on the record. On July 30, 2021, Gary Gensler stated that neither the shell nor its shareholders own equity in the operating business. He added his concern that ordinary investors did not grasp the difference. \[2\]

By March 2025 the U.S.-China Economic and Security Review Commission counted 286 Chinese companies on the three largest American exchanges, worth $1.1 trillion together. It counted 159 of them running on this contractual shell, the variable interest entity or VIE. Those issuers carried about $1 trillion, ninety-one percent of all the Chinese market value listed in the United States. \[1\]

Every gate on the way to that trillion was lawful, and every gate earned a fee for opening. The underwriters took a spread on each offering. The auditors took their fees. The exchanges took listing fees and the index providers took licensing fees. The fund managers took a fee on every dollar the index sent them. No one on that road earned a dollar for saying no.

The record documents each gate and each fee. Reading them as one architecture that the Party used without ever recruiting it is the framework's argument.

### The Terms of This Case

The case turns on one discipline. The conduct in this record falls into three categories, and blurring them destroys the case.

*Category one is wrongdoing that courts and regulators have punished.* The system worked there. Luckin Coffee paid a $180 million penalty for fabricated sales. Once the audit board gained access to the mainland, it fined the local affiliates of major audit firms. \[6\] \[7\]

*Category two is lawful conduct, however harmful.* The underwriters and auditors did lawful work on disclosed and registered offerings. So did the exchanges, the index providers, the asset managers and the regulators who registered and supervised the deals. None of them touched the mainland's operating companies. This case calls them the remote collaborators, in the plain sense of the word. They worked on the same deals the Party needed done. The record shows none of them working for the Party and none of them enlisted. The system did not need traitors. It needed incentives, and it had them.

*Category three is open questions.* The record raises questions that only government investigators can answer, and the case answers none of them. The questions appear with the target architecture, framed as grounds for investigation and never as findings.

The actor throughout is the Party. The founders of these companies are entrepreneurs, and their employees and customers are ordinary people on the mainland. The Party wrote the wall that keeps foreign owners out, and it decides which companies may list abroad. It reached into DiDi and the private tutoring sector in 2021 and cost mainland workers and families as well as American holders. \[14\]

### 1. Architecture Vision

**Documented.** The Party bars foreign ownership of companies in its internet and data sectors, and in media, education and finance. Its securities regulator has required every company that lists abroad to file for approval since March 31, 2023. VIE structures fall under the rule. The National Intelligence Law of 2017 obliges every organization on the mainland to support state intelligence work. \[3\] \[13\]

**Argued.** The design lets the Party take foreign capital while conceding no foreign control. The money crosses the wall into the operating companies. The ownership and the data stay behind it under the Party's law, and so do the licenses and the intellectual property. The capital built the technology sector that *Technology IP* shows drawing in American knowledge by other channels.

### 2. The Party's Baseline

#### Business Architecture. The Capabilities It Fielded

The Financial domain carried the operation, because the capital market itself was the knife.

**Economic.** The capital went to the sectors the Party's industrial plans name as the high ground. By the Commission's classification, more than half the VIE cohort's value sits in companies tagged as technology. E-commerce, fintech, electric vehicles and logistics fill most of the rest. \[1\]

**Financial.** An American listing created a liquid dollar currency in which these companies could pay their people. Alibaba's share-based compensation rose from RMB 1,254 million in fiscal 2012 to RMB 16,082 million in fiscal 2016, across the listing. JD.com's ran from RMB 4,250 million in its 2014 listing year to RMB 9,134 million at the 2021 peak, then fell to RMB 2,999 million in 2024. The pay climbed with American valuations and fell when American appetite withdrew. American investors supplied the standing bid that made the equity worth granting. \[11\]

**Information.** The structure hid in its own disclosures. The prospectus front sections called the Chinese business "our" business, and the qualifications sat hundreds of pages deep.

**Legal.** The Party's law built both walls. One wall bars foreign ownership of the operating companies. The other, state-secrecy law, kept the American audit board out of the mainland's audit firms for most of two decades. \[4\]

**Association.** The Party recruited no one on the American side. The fee structure of the capital market aligned thousands of separate professionals with the listing of each deal.

**Data and Surveillance.** The operating companies hold the data, and the data sits under the Party's law. When the Party's cyberspace regulator opened its review of DiDi in July 2021, it did so on data-security grounds. \[14\]

#### Data Architecture. What It Knew

Here the record documents the Party's data layer rather than leaving it to inference. The Party holds the operating companies' books, their user data and their audit papers, and its secrecy law decides who may read them. For most of two decades the American audit board could not.

The Party also knew what the audit papers of its state-owned enterprises contained. When the audit confrontation came to a head, five state-owned giants announced their exit from American exchanges on August 12, 2022. The Party signed the audit protocol two weeks later. \[4\] \[16\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines It Ran

**Stratagem 3, Kill with a Borrowed Knife**, is the master routine of the case. The Party funds its champions with the rival's capital. It bars the foreign ownership that would allow a direct listing, then approves the VIE workaround that allows a listing anyway. The record documents the design of the precondition. Whether the Party foresaw the full scale of the transfer is unknowable.

**Stratagem 1, Deceive the Heavens to Cross the Sea**, is the VIE itself. The structure crossed the sea every trading day in plain sight, normalized by repetition and hidden in the footnotes.

Stratagem 11 is **Sacrifice the Plum Tree to Save the Peach.** The state-owned enterprises whose audit papers held real secrets left American reach. The consumer internet and the technology champions stayed and kept taking American capital. The record documents the timing. Reading the exit as a deliberate sacrifice is the framework's argument.

**Stratagem 9, Watch the Fire from Across the River**, describes the rupture of 2021. The Party's regulator opened its review of DiDi two days after the company's $4.4 billion New York offering, and app stores removed DiDi's app two days after that. A decree against private tutoring gutted the education listings the same month. The losses fell on foreign holders, and the control stayed with the Party. \[14\]

**Stratagem 35, Chain the Stratagems**, links the rest. The wall creates the need and the shell answers it. The collaborators process it, and the secrecy law keeps the books closed. The record shows each link. The chain is the framework's reading.

#### A Target Audience Note

The American retail and default-fund investor fits this form, and the prospectus itself is the channel.

**Documented.** VIE prospectuses describe the Chinese operating business as "our" business in their opening pages, with the contractual qualification appearing hundreds of pages deep. Index inclusion put these listings into target-date funds and default 401(k) allocations without any individual purchase decision. \[9\]

**Argued.** A target audience analysis would read the audience as two groups rather than one. The first is the investor who reads a prospectus and stops before the qualification. The second, larger group never opens a prospectus at all, and holds the exposure only because a benchmark included it. Vulnerability in the first group runs through complexity and length. A filing that runs hundreds of pages rewards a reader who stops early. Vulnerability in the second group is structural rather than psychological. No decision of theirs is available to target, because no decision was made. Susceptibility runs highest among savers in a default plan who never chose an allocation and would not recognize the company name if they saw it. Accessibility runs through the fund's own quarterly statement, if the saver reads that far, and through nothing at all if he does not. The channel that reaches this audience most effectively is the one that requires no persuasion, because the target never has to agree to anything.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platform It Ran On

The operating company is the platform, and the Party administers it. The operating company holds the licenses without which the business cannot run. The Party's regulators grant those licenses, approve each overseas listing and can review any company on data grounds. The shareholder in New York holds a contract with a shell. The Party holds administrator rights over everything the contract describes.

#### Second-Order Effects. What the Capital Bought

The money kept working after the listing.

**Documented. Talent.** The share-based compensation in the companies' own filings rose with American valuations and fell when American buyers left. American investors underwrote the currency these companies used to hire engineers and managers. \[11\]

**Documented. Control.** The Party took special management shares, often about one percent, in the onshore units of the largest platforms. A fund set up by its cyberspace regulator took one percent of an Alibaba unit in January 2023 and one percent of a Tencent unit in October 2023. The Tencent stake cost 600,000 yuan, about $82,000. The ByteDance stake of April 2021 came with the right to name one of three directors of a key onshore company. The Party's company law requires every company to host Party organizations and to support their work. \[20\] \[21\]

**Documented. Military capital.** The index channel reached beyond the VIE companies. The bipartisan House committee found that American index and fund money put more than $5.3 billion into the Party's military companies in 2023 alone. \[18\]

**Argued. The flywheel.** The capital pays the engineers, and the engineers produce research, patents and products. The products raise the valuation that attracts the next round of capital. Each turn of the wheel builds the technology base that *Technology IP* shows the Party filling by patent, recruitment and intrusion as well.

**Argued. Gift money.** A VIE shareholder holds a contractual claim on profits, so in law the money is an investment. In control it behaves like a gift. The giver keeps no vote over the operating company, no seat on its board and no recourse the Party's courts have shown they will enforce. A one-percent state fund holds a board seat that a trillion dollars of American capital never bought.

**Argued. The tax channel.** The operating companies pay taxes into the state budget, and the state budget funds the People's Liberation Army. Every profitable VIE company contributes to that budget, as every profitable company on the mainland does.

**Not in the record.** This edition found no document that traces VIE proceeds into a Party slush fund, into bribery or into a specific theft of technology. A source that shows any of those enters the case. Until one does, the case claims none of them.

#### Principles and Governance. How It Held Together

**Asymmetry.** The American investor supplies ownership capital and receives a contract. The Party supplies nothing and keeps control.

**Phase.** The Party moved within days when it chose to. Its regulator reached DiDi two days after the offering, and its state-owned enterprises left two weeks before the audit protocol.

**Coherence.** One authority writes the ownership wall, approves each listing, controls the audit papers and can review any listed company. The record documents each of those powers. Their coordination is the framework's argument.

### 3. The Defenders' Baseline

No single enterprise defended the American saver. Public regulators held his defense with a mandate of disclosure. Security agencies held lists that do not meet, and private collaborators earned their pay from the deal.

#### Business Architecture. Who Owned What

**The remote collaborators**

| Gate | Collaborator | Paid for | Paid to refuse |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pre-listing capital | Venture firms | Fees and a share of the gains | No |
| Listing | Underwriting banks | A spread on each offering | No |
| Audit | Mainland and Hong Kong affiliates of the major audit firms | Audit fees | No |
| Exchange | NYSE and Nasdaq | Listing and trading fees | No |
| Index | MSCI and FTSE Russell | Licensing fees on each benchmark | No |
| Allocation | Asset managers and pension trustees | Fees on assets under management | No |
| Registration | Securities and Exchange Commission | A mandate to require disclosure, not to judge merit | Not its mandate |
| Audit oversight | Public Company Accounting Oversight Board | A mandate to inspect every listed issuer's auditor | Blocked for most of two decades |
| Retirement rules | Department of Labor | A mandate to police fiduciary duty | No security test until the 2025 memorandum asked for one |

Every private gate on the road earned money when a deal went through. None earned money when a deal stopped. The public gates held mandates for disclosure, inspection and prudence, and none held a mandate for security.

**The congressional finding.** The bipartisan House Select Committee on the CCP reported in April 2024 that index providers and asset managers facilitated more than $6.5 billion of investment in 63 red-flagged mainland companies in 2023. It found that MSCI indexes channeled $3.7 billion to those companies and that BlackRock invested at least $1.9 billion. More than $5.3 billion went to the Party's military companies, including makers of fighter jets and nuclear weapons for the PLA. In February 2024 the same committee reported that five American venture firms invested at least $3 billion in mainland AI and semiconductor companies. Many of those companies aid the Party's military or its surveillance state. \[18\] \[19\]

**The security owners.** The Department of Defense keeps a list of Chinese military companies under Section 1260H of the 2021 defense act. Treasury keeps a separate list, under an executive order of June 2021, that bars Americans from buying the listed securities. The two lists use different legal tests and name different companies. \[12\]

**The savings owners.** Pension trustees, the Thrift Savings Plan board, the Department of Labor under ERISA and university endowment managers each decide their own exposure. The index providers decide it automatically for anyone holding a default fund.

#### Data Architecture. What the Defenders Knew

The investor's own records described a holding he did not have. The accounting consolidated the operating company onto the shell's statements, so the numbers presented a business the investor did not own.

The government's records did not meet. A November 2020 order barred American investment in companies on the Defense list. Courts then blocked several of those designations. The June 2021 order moved the investment ban to a new Treasury list with its own test, and the Defense list published the same day carried no investment consequence. \[12\]

Hesai shows the result. The lidar maker listed on Nasdaq in February 2023. Defense added it to the 1260H list in January 2024, and a federal court upheld the designation in July 2025. The designation's statutory consequence reaches Defense contracting from 2026. American investors could still buy the stock in 2026. \[12\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines the Defenders Ran

**The audit clock**

| Date | Owner | Routine | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2002 | Congress | Sarbanes-Oxley creates the audit board with power to inspect every listed issuer's auditor | The mainland blocks inspection for most of two decades |
| December 18, 2020 | Congress | Holding Foreign Companies Accountable Act sets a delisting timer | The board finds on December 16, 2021 that it cannot inspect |
| August 12, 2022 | The Party's state-owned enterprises | Five giants announce their exit from American exchanges | They leave before any inspection |
| August 26, 2022 | Audit board and the Party's regulators | Statement of Protocol signed | Inspections begin that autumn |
| December 15, 2022 | Audit board | Reports complete access for the first time | The delisting timer stops |
| May 2023 | Audit board | First inspection findings | Deficiencies in seven of the eight audits examined |
| November 2023 | Audit board | Sanctions totaling $7.9 million | The system works where it can reach |

The delisting routine worked. A published trigger with an automatic consequence produced access the board had sought for two decades. The trigger measured access to audit papers, and the Party granted access. It kept the ownership wall, the licenses and the data.

The fraud routine worked too, within its reach. Luckin Coffee fabricated more than $300 million in sales and paid the SEC $180 million. The Party's market regulator fined the Luckin entities about $9 million. The SEC's own release records that any payment to defrauded shareholders required approval from authorities on the mainland. \[6\] \[7\]

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms the Defenders Used

The defenders' platforms are the deepest capital markets in the world, and the index is their automatic transmission. Once MSCI and FTSE Russell added Chinese listings to their global and emerging-market benchmarks, every American in a target-date fund or a default 401(k) allocation held these shells by mechanical operation. No person made that decision for the saver. The benchmark made it. \[9\]

#### Principles and Governance. What Held the Defenders Together

The defenders kept their principles. Securities law requires disclosure rather than merit review, so investors may take any risk they understand. Fiduciaries diversify. Markets stay open to foreign issuers. Those principles are worth keeping. They also left the security question with no owner inside the capital market.

The White House memorandum on investment policy of February 21, 2025 committed the administration to review VIE structures, audit adequacy and pension fiduciary standards. It named university endowments among the channels to examine. The memorandum imposed no rule itself. \[15\]

### 4. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | Took capital for two decades and recalled the secrets in two weeks | Took twenty years to open the audit papers |
| **Entropy** | One authority over the wall, the licenses and the books | Six gates and two security lists, none connected |
| **Topology** | A star around the Party's regulators | A mesh of private collaborators and public agencies |
| **Asymmetry** | Takes a board seat for one percent and hands investors a contract | Supplies ownership capital and receives no seat |
| **Agency** | Decides which companies list and which come home | Leaves each fiduciary and each index to decide alone |
| **Resonance** | Wall, approval, secrecy and data law point one way | Defense designates Hesai and investors still buy it |
| **Phase** | Reached DiDi in two days and pulled the state firms in two weeks | Delisting timer measured in years |
| **Gradient** | Needed only the fee structure the market already had | Every gate paid to open, none paid to refuse |
| **Criticality** | Aimed the capital at technology and data | Pointed default savings at the index automatically |
| **Coherence** | Governed by one authority | No owner of the security question in the capital market |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. No Owner of the Security Question

*Architecture Governance*

**Documented.** The SEC's mandate is investor protection through disclosure. Defense and Treasury keep security lists with different tests. The Labor Department governs pension fiduciaries. Congress's defense act for fiscal 2026 ordered a report on harmonizing the sanctions lists. The White House memorandum of 2025 ordered reviews and imposed no rule. \[12\] \[15\]

**Argued.** Each owner behaved correctly inside its own remit. No owner held the question of whether American savings should finance the Party's technology sector. A trillion dollars of listings ran through a capital market that had no seat for the national-security reader.

#### Gap 2. Every Gate Paid to Open

*Architecture Principles, Gradient. The signature gap*

**Documented.** Every private collaborator on the road earned fees when a VIE listing went through. The Commission's table names the underwriting syndicate for each issuer. \[1\] The House committee found in 2024 that index providers and asset managers facilitated more than $6.5 billion of investment in red-flagged mainland companies in a single year. \[18\] The Thrift Savings Plan board moved in 2020 toward a benchmark that included Chinese listings, then reversed and excluded China and Hong Kong outright. \[9\] A 2023 review counted $68 billion committed to China by seventy-two large public pension funds between 2020 and 2023. \[8\]

**Argued.** The Party needed no agent. It needed a slope, and the fee structure of the American capital market supplied one. Every collaborator acted lawfully and profited from the flow. Refusal earned nothing, and the one public fiduciary that refused did so under political pressure rather than by design. The remote collaborators are not the defect. The slope that rewarded every one of them for opening is the defect.

#### Gap 3. The Records Do Not Describe the Holding

*Data Architecture*

**Documented.** Consolidation accounting presents the operating company on the shell's statements. The SEC chairman stated in 2021 that the shareholders own no equity in the operating business. Defense's list and Treasury's list use different tests, and only Treasury's bars investment. Hesai sat on the first and remained open to American buyers. \[2\] \[12\]

**Argued.** The investor's data model says he owns a company, and the law says he holds a contract. The government's data model says a company is military, and the capital market never reads that record. Two schemas describe the same holding, and neither describes it accurately.

#### Gap 4. The Trigger Measured Access, Not Control

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** The Holding Foreign Companies Accountable Act set a timer that ran on inspection access. The Party's state-owned giants left two weeks before the protocol, and the board gained complete access that December. The first inspections found deficiencies in seven of eight audits. \[4\] \[5\] \[16\]

**Argued.** The routine worked on the variable it measured. The Party conceded access to audit papers and kept the ownership wall, the licenses and the data. A trigger built on control rather than access would have asked for what the Party would not give.

#### Gap 5. The Party Holds Root Over the Operating Company

*Technology Architecture*

**Documented.** DiDi raised $4.4 billion in New York on June 30, 2021. The Party's cyberspace regulator opened a review two days later, and app stores removed DiDi's app on July 4. DiDi completed its New York delisting in June 2022. The Party's securities regulator has approved every overseas listing since March 2023. \[3\] \[14\] A fund set up by the Party's cyberspace regulator took special management shares in onshore units of Alibaba and Tencent in 2023. \[20\]

**Argued.** The shareholder's contract describes a business that runs on the Party's licenses and the Party's data rules. The Party can reach the business in days and leave the shareholder holding the contract. A one-percent state stake buys the Party a board seat. The New York shareholder owns no seat at any price. The Hong Kong case showed controls running on a platform the adversary administers. This case shows capital doing the same.

#### Gap 6. The Index Chose for the Saver

*Business Architecture, Financial*

**Documented.** Index inclusion put Chinese listings into the default funds of American retirement savers. The Thrift Savings Plan chose to exclude them. The 2025 memorandum called for pension fiduciary standards that make adversary companies ineligible and named endowments among the channels to review. \[9\] \[15\]

**Argued.** The capability to allocate national savings with security in view had no owner. Each fiduciary decided alone, and the index provider decided for millions who never decided at all.

### 5. Target Architecture

The target keeps disclosure, open markets and the saver's freedom to take risks he understands. It gives the security question an owner and makes refusal pay.

**Gap to target**

| Gap | Target element | Owner | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. No owner of the security question | The council holds one capital-channel schema across the SEC, Treasury, Defense and Labor, with published thresholds | National Strategic Competition Council, convening only | A statute that outlives an election cycle, and agencies that share their agendas |
| 2. Every gate paid to open | Security screening as a condition of underwriting, index eligibility and default-fund inclusion, with a safe harbor for collaborators who refuse | SEC, Labor and the index providers | Lost fees, narrower benchmarks, and litigation over the screen |
| 3. The records do not describe the holding | One harmonized list of military companies that every capital-market gate reads, and a plain-language ownership label at the point of sale | Treasury and Defense, with the SEC | Designation errors that now reach markets, and more appeals |
| 4. The trigger measured access | Listing conditioned on equity ownership of the operating business, phased in, with the audit timer re-triggered automatically on any obstruction | SEC and the exchanges | Delistings, losses to current holders, and capital that moves to Hong Kong |
| 5. The Party holds root | Standard disclosure of the Party's approval and data powers over each operating company | SEC | Longer prospectuses, and issuers that list elsewhere |
| 6. The index chose for the saver | Exclusion of VIE issuers from the benchmarks that default retirement money tracks | Labor, the Thrift board model, and the index providers | Tracking error, and a smaller opportunity set |

**A published trigger already worked here.** The audit statute set an automatic consequence and produced access the board had sought for twenty years. The target moves the same design from access to ownership.

**The index exclusion does the most for the least disruption.** The Thrift Savings Plan proved a fiduciary can exclude China without forcing a disorderly sale. Extending that choice to default retirement money stops the automatic flow and leaves every investor free to buy the shares on purpose.

**The collaborators need a reason to refuse.** A safe harbor for an underwriter or index provider that declines a listing on security grounds turns refusal from a lost fee into a protected act. The slope then runs both ways.

**Open questions go to investigators.** The record raises questions only government can answer. Did specific issuers misstate the enforceability of their VIE contracts after the SEC tightened disclosure in 2021? Did any audit firm resume obstruction after 2022? Did the sale of these securities to retail and pension investors involve misrepresentation under state law? Did fund prospectuses describe the red-flag risks the House committee said they omitted? Each question belongs with the Justice Department and state attorneys general as grounds for inquiry, and none is a finding.

**The counterweights stand.** Early holders of several of these companies earned large returns, and diversification into the second-largest economy was a defensible mandate. Mainland champions would have found capital in Hong Kong or on the mainland without New York, though at smaller scale and lower valuations. The issuers disclosed and registered every offering. American holdings of mainland securities roughly halved from their 2020 peak, the Thrift Savings Plan excluded China, and outbound investment rules took effect in 2025. \[10\] \[17\] None of this dissolves the finding. It bounds it.

**What the target would not have done.** It would not have stopped mainland companies from raising money elsewhere. It would have stopped American default savings from financing them automatically, and it would have given someone in the capital market the job of asking the security question. That claim remains an argument.

### What the Borrowed Knife Proves

The Borrowed Knife proves Gradient. The Party needed no agent in New York. It needed a market in which every collaborator earned a fee for opening and none for refusing, and the market already existed.

It proves Asymmetry at the level of ownership. The American saver supplied equity and received a contract. The Party supplied nothing and kept the company, its data and its licenses.

It ties the book's money to its technology. The capital that crossed the wall financed the sectors that *Technology IP* shows drawing in American knowledge by patent, recruitment and intrusion.

For a quarter-century the United States ran the deepest capital market in the world with no way to price the difference between owning a company and holding an unenforceable contract on one. It had no seat for anyone to weigh the strategic cost of financing a rival's technology base. The transfer was lawful, systematic and largely still permitted. The knife was never stolen. It lay on the table, and the market paid the people who passed it across.

### Field Card

**What happened.** The Party barred foreign ownership of its technology companies, then approved a contractual workaround that let those companies list in New York anyway. By 2025 that workaround carried about $1 trillion of the $1.1 trillion in Chinese company value listed on American exchanges. Every American gatekeeper along the way earned a fee for letting the listing through and nothing for stopping it. Underwriters and auditors, exchanges and index providers, and fund managers each took their cut.

**The gap.** No one in the American capital market owned the question of whether a listing served national security, the signature gap at the Principles layer.

**The fix.** A published security screen at the point of underwriting, indexing and default-fund inclusion would give every collaborator a paid reason to refuse instead of only a paid reason to proceed.

**Where you could work on it.** Securities and exchange regulation, index and benchmark design, or pension and retirement-plan fiduciary policy.

*Sourcing notes and the full corrections record for this chapter appear in Appendix H.*

## Accommodate

*The War for the Mind*

**DOMAINS** Economic · Information · **Psychological (spine)** · Legal · Association · Cyber · Cultural · **AI and Cognitive (spine)** · Data and Surveillance

**DIMENSIONS** Time · Entropy · Topology · Asymmetry · **Agency (signature)** · Resonance · Phase · Gradient · Criticality · Coherence

**STRATAGEMS** Spine S3 · S7 · S10 · S20 · S33 · S35

**GAPS** Seven. Two at Governance, one at each other layer. Signature gaps sit at Governance. No owner of the objective function, and the home audience has no owner.

ByteDance runs two versions of the same application. Douyin runs on the mainland. TikTok runs everywhere else.

Since 2021 Douyin's youth mode has limited users under fourteen to forty minutes a day and shut them out between ten at night and six in the morning, enforced through real-name registration tied to a national identity system. In March 2023 TikTok gave accounts under eighteen a default limit of sixty minutes. A teenager can extend it with a passcode or switch it off entirely, and nothing on the platform verifies who holds the phone. \[1\]

The difference lies in the layer that enforces the limit, not the limit itself. On the mainland the state's identity system makes it hard to cheat. Abroad it is a default a fourteen-year-old can dismiss in under a minute. The same identity system that protects the mainland child also tracks every mainland user's every action, and the protection comes bundled with the surveillance as one design, not two.

In a recommendation system the objective function decides what the machine optimizes for, whether time on the app, a curated diet of content or anything else an owner chooses to reward. Whoever sets that parameter governs a population's attention at the level the population itself never sees. The Party sets it for its own population through a regulator that requires every recommendation algorithm to register and publishes the registry. No one on the defenders' side owns the equivalent question at all, for any platform, foreign or domestic.

That is the platform half of this case. The practitioner half sits one layer up. Once a platform can steer attention, a state can pair it with covert accounts and human operators to study a chosen audience and move it toward a specific end. This chapter reads both halves as one campaign against one target, a population's capacity to decide.

### The Terms of This Case

The actor is the Party, not China and not the Chinese people. ByteDance is a company on the mainland, and the National Intelligence Law of 2017 obliges it to assist the security organs on demand. \[2\] The Party runs cognitive control on its own population too. Douyin is not freedom. The same machine runs there at a different setting, censored and surveilled and steered toward the values the Party approves. Both populations are targets.

Two disciplines meet in this chapter, and neither is persuasion in the ordinary sense. Persuasion engages reason in the open and leaves judgment intact, whichever way it lands. The platform discipline works differently. It degrades a whole society's shared sense of what is real, not by arguing against any particular fact but by making every fact compete for attention on the same terms as everything else, until the population cannot assemble the consensus it needs to act together on anything. The practitioner discipline is narrower and sharper than the platform, and it does not need the platform to exist, though it profits when the platform has already done its work. It selects a target audience, studies its vulnerabilities and its susceptibility to a given line, and shapes that audience's emotions and behavior toward an objective it may never see or name. Democracies run the narrower discipline too, lawfully and under approval chains and doctrine, aimed abroad rather than at their own people. This chapter measures architecture on both sides of both disciplines, not the morality of the tool itself.

Two guardrails hold for everything that follows. Nothing in the target architecture polices the lawful speech of the defenders' own citizens. And where this chapter names a routine, it names the routine, not the person exposed to it. Labels and disclosure are the remedy throughout. Removal of lawful speech is never the remedy.

One boundary limits both halves. No public document shows an engineer tuning a recommendation system to degrade a rival population as distinct from tuning it for engagement with degradation as a byproduct, and no public document describes the Party's analysts at work on a given campaign. The case argues that the effect serves the Party whether or not anyone designed it to, and that operations too precise in timing to be coincidence imply analysis behind them. Readers should hold both as arguments.

A further question follows naturally from merging these two halves into one chapter, and it deserves a direct answer. No public document shows whether one office runs both the platform's objective function and the practitioner's target audience analysis, or whether they sit in separate arms that answer to the same authority without coordinating day to day. The management plane the *Military-Civil Fusion* case describes would make either structure possible under one command. This case does not depend on which one is true, because a defender facing either structure sees the same absence of an owner on its own side.

### 1. Architecture Vision

**Documented.** The Party's cyberspace regulator requires platforms to register their recommendation algorithms and publishes a registry of them. It drafted rules in 2023 to set daily limits for minors across mobile apps by age. The People's Liberation Army wrote public-opinion, psychological and legal warfare into its political-work regulations in 2003. PLA writers describe the mind itself as a domain of war in their own doctrine, cognitive domain operations. \[3\] \[4\] \[5\]

**Argued.** The Party governs attention and runs target audience analysis on whole populations, at home and abroad, faster than any defender can see the operation. At home the objective is stability, held through a registry no outside body audits.
