Abroad it is division, doubt and the belief that resistance is futile, pursued through channels the Party's own citizens cannot legally reach in return.

### 2. The Party's Baseline

#### The Platform

The AI and Cognitive domain carries this half, because the recommendation engine is both the delivery system and the aiming mechanism.

**Economic.** The advertising and creator economy funds the reach and deepens the dependence.

**Information.** Covert networks amplify, suppress and fabricate. In August 2023 Meta removed 7,704 Facebook accounts and hundreds of pages and groups in the largest covert influence operation it had found, a network researchers call Spamouflage. Meta linked it to individuals associated with the Party's law enforcement. \[6\]

**Psychological.** The engagement engine rewards short-cycle stimulation and outrage. A growing body of research links heavy short-form video use to effects on adolescent attention and emotional regulation. The literature is young and contested. \[7\]

**Legal.** The National Intelligence Law makes the platform's parent compellable. In August 2020 the Party added recommendation technology to its export-control catalog. \[2\] \[8\]

**Cultural.** The contrast between the two configurations is its own evidence of what the builders believe about their product.

**AI and Cognitive.** The Party's networks now use generative AI. OpenAI reported in May 2024 that Spamouflage had used its models to write and translate content. \[9\]

**Data and Surveillance.** TikTok told Congress it served about 170 million American users. Their behavior was the raw material of the model. \[10\]

A recommendation engine builds a predictive model of each user from behavior, and aggregated across a population the model shows which divisions carry the most charge, information no census or poll could gather at the same resolution or speed. The Party's covert networks aim at those divisions and amplify the most polarizing voices on several sides at once, rather than picking one side and pushing only it. A society under that pressure experiences its own fragmentation as an internal failure, because the grievances are real and domestic even where the amplification behind them is not, which is precisely what makes the operation hard to name while it is happening.

No public document shows the Party drawing on TikTok's American data to aim its networks abroad. The model's existence follows from how recommendation systems work anywhere. Its use by the Party for this purpose is the framework's argument, not a documented fact.

**Stratagem 10, Hide a Knife Behind a Smile**, is the platform's whole design. The degradation arrives inside delightful entertainment, never announced and never felt as an attack by the person experiencing it. **Stratagem 33, Sow Discord Among the Enemy**, amplifies a society's fault lines rather than inventing new ones. **Stratagem 7, Create Something from Nothing**, runs the synthetic personas and coordinated networks of the Spamouflage type. They manufacture apparent consensus where none exists. **Stratagem 20, Muddy the Waters to Catch the Fish**, harvests advantage from a population that can no longer tell signal from noise. The other three stratagems together help produce exactly that condition.

The platform is the recommendation engine and the cloud it runs on. The Party's export-control listing turned the algorithm into controlled technology, so any sale of TikTok's American business needed the Party's consent, granted only after five years of American effort. \[8\] \[11\]

#### The Practitioner

American doctrine runs psychological operations as a process, and the process reads cleanly as an enterprise architecture. Its core is target audience analysis. It identifies an audience and the conditions it lives under. Then it rates the audience's vulnerability, its susceptibility to a given line, and the channels that reach it. \[22\]

Target audience analysis is a data problem before it is anything else. The practitioner needs to know what an audience fears, resents and wants, which messages it will accept and which channels it trusts. The Party holds that data on its own population through its platforms, its dossiers and its real-name registration. Abroad it holds what the open record, breaches and the platforms themselves supply, including the recommendation data the platform half of this case already describes. A practitioner working from that depth of data can rate vulnerability, susceptibility and accessibility for a population segment in hours rather than months. No public document shows the Party's analysts at that work. The precision of the timing and themes in the cases below argues for the analysis behind them, without proving it.

| Process step | Architecture layer | The Party's version | The defenders' version |
|---|---|---|---|
| Target audience analysis | Data | Runs on the harvest the *Digital Twin* case describes | Runs on smaller, mostly foreign data sets |
| Product development | Application | Human teams and AI output at scale | Trained teams working product by product |
| Dissemination | Technology | Its own platforms at home, open platforms abroad | Foreign channels only, by law and policy |
| Approval | Governance | One authority, through the management plane | Approval chains that doctrine requires |
| Assessment | Feedback loop | Platform metrics read in near real time | Criteria set in advance and measured later |

**Legal.** Legal warfare is one of the three warfares by the PLA's own regulations, and the Anti-Secession Law of 2005 serves as a standing psychological instrument as well as a legal one, since its terms are read aloud to a Taiwanese audience every time tensions rise rather than left to sit unused in a statute book.

**Association.** United-front networks and trusted local voices carry messages in communities that would discount the same message from an official source, whether or not the carrier knows where the message began.

**Cyber.** Intrusion supplies private material that operations later leak or twist, turning a breach into a talking point months or years after the breach itself.

**Cultural.** Identity operations recast self-government as betrayal of a shared heritage, as the *Taiwan* case records in its own detail.

The PLA's political officers own the three warfares by regulation, not by informal practice, which means a commander who wants to run one has a chain of command to answer to rather than a norm to interpret. The Propaganda Department sets the line for what the domestic public hears. The Cyberspace Administration works as one institution with two nameplates, the office of the Party's cyberspace commission and a state agency, so the same desk that drafts an algorithm rule can draft a censorship order. Analysts identify Base 311 in Fuzhou as a PLA unit devoted to the three warfares against Taiwan specifically, a standing organization rather than an ad hoc task force stood up for one campaign and dissolved after. \[3\] \[5\]

**Stratagem 3, Kill with a Borrowed Knife**, uses the target's own users and grievances to do the damage. **Stratagem 35, Chain the Stratagems**, links both halves together. Data feeds the model, and the model builds the target audience analysis. The analysis produces the product, and the platform or the covert network disseminates it. The record documents each link. The chain is the framework's reading.

**Entropy.** The machine enforces order at home and exports disorder abroad. A regulator that can silence a rumor on the mainland in hours lets its networks seed a hundred contradictory rumors abroad and never has to clean up either mess.

**Resonance.** The practitioner half amplifies divisions the audience already carries rather than manufacturing new ones, which is why the operations below read as domestic even where the amplification is not.

**Coherence.** One authority governs both halves, the domestic feed's objective function and the operations run through it and around it. The record documents the registry, the regulations and the operations. The single design behind them is the framework's argument.

### 3. The Defenders' Baseline

No single enterprise defends the defenders' attention or their audiences. Congress and courts, the military and civilian agencies, and platforms each hold a piece.

#### The Platform

| Capability | Defender owner | Instrument |
|---|---|---|
| Foreign ownership of platforms | Congress and the President | The divest-or-ban law of 2024 |
| Children's data | Federal Trade Commission and Justice Department | The children's online privacy law |
| Platform design for minors | States, and Congress in pending bills | State design codes, many enjoined in court |
| Recommender transparency | No federal owner | None |

The European Union holds the one broad instrument among the defenders' allies. Its Digital Services Act requires the largest platforms to assess systemic risks and offer a feed that does not rely on profiling, and the European Commission opened proceedings against TikTok in February 2024 over minors' protection and addictive design. \[12\] No comparable statute exists in American law, so the comparison itself is part of the gap. The ally with the broader instrument is not the one whose citizens the operation is documented to have targeted most directly.

**The TikTok clock**

| Date | Owner | Routine | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| August 6, 2020 | President | Executive order against TikTok | Courts blocked it |
| August 28, 2020 | The Party | Recommendation technology added to export controls | A veto over any sale |
| April 24, 2024 | Congress | Divest-or-ban law | Upheld by the Supreme Court, January 17, 2025 |
| August 2, 2024 | Justice Department | Suit alleging children's privacy violations | Pending in court |
| January 22, 2026 | Investors and both governments | Joint venture closes | ByteDance keeps 19.9 percent, algorithm licensed and retrained |

The Party inserted its export-control veto twenty-two days after the first American order, a response measured in weeks against a divestiture measured in years. The joint venture that finally closed did not remove the algorithm from ByteDance's reach. It licensed the algorithm, kept ByteDance as a minority owner, and retrained the model on American data under an outside auditor's watch rather than starting the model over from nothing. \[8\] \[11\] \[13\]

The joint venture stores American data in Oracle's cloud, retrains the algorithm on American data under Oracle's audit, and licenses the underlying model from ByteDance. A seven-member board with an American majority governs it. \[11\] The First Amendment limits how far government can shape what any platform shows, and courts have enjoined several state design codes. Those limits protect a free society from its own government. They also leave the objective function with no public owner.

#### The Practitioner

| Capability | Defender owner | Limits |
|---|---|---|
| Psychological operations abroad | Military psychological operations units | Foreign audiences only, by law and policy |
| Truthful broadcasting into closed societies | Voice of America and Radio Free Asia | Cut back by executive order in 2025, litigated since |
| Exposing foreign propaganda | The Global Engagement Center | Lapsed in December 2024 |
| Detecting covert networks at home | Platforms and outside researchers | Voluntary |
| Resilience of the home audience | No owner | None |

The one operation the public record shows in detail is the Philippines campaign, and it went wrong. It ran from spring 2020 to mid-2021 through at least 300 fake accounts posing as Filipinos, aimed at undermining trust in a Chinese-made COVID vaccine in a partner nation still fighting the pandemic. Reuters reported that it began as payback for the Party's own effort to blame the United States for the pandemic's origin. The Defense Department did not deny the report and said it runs operations to counter adversary influence. \[14\] Whatever its provocation, a campaign that targets public health information during a pandemic spends a kind of credibility no later operation can buy back.

The truthful channel into the Party's own audience shrank during the period this edition covers. An executive order of March 14, 2025 directed the Agency for Global Media to cut to its statutory minimum, and staff across Voice of America and Radio Free Asia were placed on leave within days of the order. A federal judge ordered restoration in April, and an appeals court stayed parts of that order in May. The judge then blocked further cuts that September, leaving the broadcasters in a legal limbo that outlasted this edition's research. Supporters of the cuts called the broadcasters wasteful and politically biased. Press-freedom groups called the silence a gift to closed regimes that no other American institution could replace on short notice. \[15\]

At home the defenders run no counter-routine, by design. Their military may not target their own citizens, and their government may not police lawful speech. Those limits protect a free society from its own state. They also leave the home audience, where the Party's operations land, with no owner at all.

### 4. Why It Works: The Prepared Ground

Neither half of this case manufactures division. Both find division and widen it.

The weapon needs a population already losing its shared sense of what is true, sorted into distrustful camps and unsure whether its own institutions of knowledge tell it straight. Some of that erosion is organic, the fragmentation of a common media environment into private feeds that no platform designed on purpose. Some of it is self-inflicted. Serious people disagree over what schools should teach, over identity and classification in education, and over the institutions that run the schools. This framework takes no side in that argument. It observes only that the fight itself, and the distrust it breeds on every side, is the fault line both the platform and the practitioner seek.

The pandemic years deepened the distrust, as the *COVID-19* case records in detail. The Party built the platform half of this vulnerability and runs the practitioner half against it. The defenders own the ground half, and nothing in this chapter's target architecture asks them to surrender it.

Neither half needs the other to function, and each was drafted and documented separately before this edition merged them. Together they compound in a way neither half shows alone. The platform's recommendation data show which divisions in a population carry the most charge, the same divisions a target audience analysis needs to rate for vulnerability and susceptibility. Where the Party's own citizens are the audience, the platform and the practitioner can sit inside the same regulatory structure and never need to share data formally, because one authority already holds both. Where a foreign audience is the target, the platform supplies the terrain and the covert network supplies the message, and a population that has spent years inside an engagement-optimized feed arrives at any operation already sorted into the camps the operation will then amplify.

The compounding runs in the other direction too. A population trained by years of algorithmic feed to expect content tailored to what it already believes has a harder time recognizing a tailored operation as tailored. The platform does not need to coordinate with any specific campaign to make every later campaign easier. It only has to keep running.

### 5. A Target Audience Note

Both disciplines converge here, so this note carries the case's central illustration. The Taiwan electorate of 2024 serves as the example.

**Documented.** Party-linked actors used AI-generated audio, video and memes around the January 2024 vote, material realistic enough that fact-checkers had to build new verification workflows to keep pace with the volume. \[24\] The Party timed trade suspensions and military exercises to the election and the inauguration that followed it, so the message arrived through policy as well as through media. \[16\]

**Argued.** A target audience analysis would read the audience as younger and swing voters specifically, not the electorate as a whole. Their vulnerabilities would include housing costs, stagnant wages and a genuine fear of war that polling shows runs higher among younger cohorts than older ones. The line of persuasion would hold that America will not come when it matters and that resistance brings ruin without benefit. Susceptibility would run highest where economic worry meets distrust of the governing party, a combination that does not require any sympathy for the Party to produce. Accessibility would run through short-video apps, messaging groups and trusted local figures rather than through official news, which this audience already discounts. The Party's documented operations fit that analysis closely enough that the fit itself is evidence. Whether its own analysts wrote the analysis down in those terms, or simply acted on instinct built from decades of practice, is unknown.

This is not a new discipline. Soviet military theorists spent decades formalizing reflexive control, the practice of feeding an opponent selected information so that the opponent's own reasoning delivers the decision the sender wanted. Timothy Thomas's standard account of the theory, published in 2004, describes exactly the logic a modern target audience analysis automates. \[17\]

This is not a new practice either. In 1218 a Mongol trade caravan was massacred at Otrar, on the edge of the Khwarezmian Empire, and Genghis Khan's envoys carried his answer to every city that lay in his path afterward. Submit, and live under his rule. Resist, and be destroyed to the last name. \[18\] The message was tailored to each city's fear before a single soldier arrived, and it did as much of the Mongols' work as their cavalry.

### 6. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | Protects its children's formation on a generational horizon and runs operations continuously across decades | Left minors' design to defaults and pending bills, and funds counter-propaganda offices that lapse |
| **Entropy** | Order at home, disorder abroad | Absorbed the disorder, split across many owners |
| **Topology** | One registry over every domestic algorithm and every home channel | No registry, no owner, channels held by private firms |
| **Asymmetry** | Ran its apps abroad while it blocked foreign platforms at home | Kept its own market and channels open |
| **Agency** | Governs its population's feed and aims at audiences' capacity to decide | Left the feed to engagement metrics and the home audience's resilience to chance |
| **Resonance** | Covert networks amplify divisions already running hot | Divisions already running hot, unaddressed |
| **Phase** | Inserted a veto in twenty-two days, produces AI output at machine speed | Took five and a half years to divest one app, approves products one at a time |
| **Gradient** | A slope toward compulsive use abroad, fabricated consensus at home | Soft defaults a teenager can dismiss |
| **Criticality** | Aims at adolescent minds, elections and shared facts | Protected none of the three by design |
| **Coherence** | Governed by one authority through the management plane | No owner of the objective function or the home audience |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. No Owner of the Objective Function

*Architecture Governance. Signature gap, platform half*

**Documented.** The Party's regulator registers recommendation algorithms and drafted age limits for minors across apps. The United States has no federal registry of recommender objectives and no enacted federal design standard for minors, though several have been proposed. The Senate passed the Kids Online Safety Act in July 2024, and the House did not take it up. \[3\] \[4\] \[19\]

**Argued.** The objective function is a governance decision disguised as an engineering parameter, and disguising it costs nothing extra to build. The Party governs it at home through a named regulator with a public registry. In the United States the platform's revenue model governs it by default, whoever owns the platform, because no statute assigns the question to anyone. The divestiture addressed one company and left the mechanism untouched everywhere else it runs. Engagement-maximizing short-form video operates on platforms under many owners today, and the next instance of this gap need not announce its parentage the way TikTok's did.

#### Gap 2. The Home Audience Has No Owner

*Architecture Governance. Signature gap, practitioner half*

**Documented.** American psychological operations units may not target American audiences, by law and by the doctrine that trains them. The Global Engagement Center lapsed in 2024 without a statutory successor. \[23\] Platforms detect covert networks voluntarily, under no obligation to look or to report what they find. A 2022 study in *Science Advances* found that short inoculation videos shown as ads improved viewers' recognition of manipulation techniques on later exposure, and no public owner runs that kind of prebunking at any meaningful scale. \[20\]

**Argued.** The Party's operations land in the space where the defenders' state rightly declines to act, and that space has stayed empty rather than being filled by anyone else. The limits on the military and civilian agencies are not the defect. The absence of any owner for the audience's own resilience is the defect, built to work without policing a single word anyone says, and it has sat unaddressed since long before this edition's research began.

#### Gap 3. The Party Moves in Weeks, the Defenders in Years

*Architecture Principles, Phase*

**Documented.** The Party's export-control veto followed the first American order by twenty-two days. The joint venture closed five and a half years after that order. \[8\] \[11\] \[13\] Doctrine requires the defenders' psychological products to pass approval one at a time before release abroad. \[22\]

**Argued.** The Party answers in weeks on both halves of this case, with a veto and with AI output at machine speed. The defenders answer in years with a sale the Party approved, and with a chain that cannot answer an operation producing thousands of items an hour. Approval protects against error and abuse, and the Philippines campaign below shows exactly why that protection matters. But a chain that reviews every product one at a time cannot also match machine speed. Pre-approved themes and objectives, set in advance and audited afterward rather than cleared beforehand, would answer the speed without dropping the gate that keeps the defenders honest.

#### Gap 4. The Party's Data Advantage Runs Through Both Halves

*Data Architecture*

**Documented.** The TikTok joint venture moved American data into Oracle's cloud and retrains the algorithm on American data, but licenses the underlying model from ByteDance, which keeps 19.9 percent of the venture. The Party holds its own target audience data at home and draws on open and stolen data abroad. The defenders' own audience data was largely for sale until 2024. \[11\] \[21\]

**Argued.** Retraining changes weights and can leave the design underneath intact, and a licensing arrangement that keeps the original developer at the table keeps a channel open that a clean sale would have closed. Verifying either the platform's objective function or the practitioner's target model requires an audit the public cannot see, run by parties the public did not choose. The side with the richer data runs the sharper analysis on both halves of this case, and the defenders sold theirs into a market that asked no security question when it changed hands.

#### Gap 5. Detection Runs on Volunteers

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** Meta, OpenAI and outside researchers published the takedowns of the Party's covert networks that this chapter cites. The Global Engagement Center, the one federal office built to expose foreign propaganda, lapsed in December 2024 after critics charged it with funding censorship of Americans. No statutory successor replaced it. \[6\] \[9\] \[23\]

**Argued.** The defenders' detection routine depends entirely on private firms choosing to look for these networks and choosing to publish what they find, with no obligation running either way. The government's own office foundered because critics saw it policing Americans' speech, the exact risk any public routine in this space must avoid, and the fear of repeating that mistake has so far kept a successor from being built at all. A volunteer system finds what a platform is willing to find.

#### Gap 6. An Operation Without an Ethics Gate

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** The American campaign in the Philippines targeted trust in a vaccine during a pandemic, in a partner nation, through fake accounts built to look Filipino. The Defense Department did not deny the Reuters report and said it runs operations to counter adversary influence. \[14\]

**Argued.** A routine that can target public health burns credibility every later operation needs, at home and abroad. The defenders' strongest weapon against the Party's operations is the simple fact that their news is true and the Party's fabrications are not. An operation like the Philippines campaign spends that asset for a tactical gain, and the account does not reset when the campaign ends. No standing rule currently prevents a future operation from making the same trade.

#### Gap 7. The Truthful Channel Shrank, and No Identity Layer Replaced It

*Technology Architecture*

**Documented.** The 2025 order cut the broadcasters that reach the Party's own audience, and courts have heard the challenges since, with the outcome still unresolved as of this edition. Douyin's hard limits rest on real-name registration that TikTok's foreign defaults do not share and, in a free society, should not. \[1\] \[15\]

**Argued.** The Party runs operations into the defenders' open channels while closing its own, and the defenders' one channel into the Party's closed audience runs on truthful news. Silencing it, whatever its management faults, hands the Party that audience uncontested rather than contested. A free society rightly refuses the Party's identity layer as the price of protecting minors, which means any protection it builds has to work without a registry of citizens behind it, through defaults and device-level signals rather than through the state knowing who is fourteen.

### 7. Target Architecture

The target strengthens both the platform and the audience, labels rather than removes, and polices no citizen's lawful speech.

**Gap to target**

| Gap | Target element | Owner | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. No owner of the objective function | Disclosure and independent audit of recommender objectives above a size threshold | Congress and the Federal Trade Commission | Litigation under the First Amendment, trade-secret disputes |
| 2. The home audience has no owner | Public attribution of foreign operations, with labels and no removal of lawful speech | An office under statutory limits | Political suspicion, attribution errors |
| 3. The Party moves faster | A standing reciprocity rule for platforms, and pre-approved themes for operations abroad | Congress, Commerce, Defense and State | Retaliation, and risk at the edge of delegation |
| 4. The data advantage | Independent audits of training and objectives for foreign-licensed platforms, and limits on data sales | Commerce and Congress | Cost, access to proprietary code, lost broker revenue |
| 5. Detection runs on volunteers | An intelligence office restricted to attribution, with published rules | Executive branch, under statute | Political suspicion, errors of attribution |
| 6. No ethics gate | A standing rule that no operation targets public health or safety information | Defense leadership and Congress | Fewer options in a crisis |
| 7. The channel shrank, no identity layer | Statutory independence and audit for truthful broadcasting, and privacy-preserving age signals set at the device | Congress, device makers and app stores | Budget, management reform, uneven adoption |

**Labels, not removals.** The *COVID-19* case shows in detail what speech policing costs a free society, in trust as much as in law. Every element of this target discloses, audits and labels, and none of them gives any office the power to take a lawful post down.

**Truth is the defenders' advantage.** The Party's operations run on fabrication from end to end. Synthetic personas, fabricated events and manufactured consensus do the work no honest account could do. The defenders' strongest instrument abroad is accurate news the Party structurally cannot supply, because supplying it would require the Party to say things about itself it will not say. The defenders' strongest instrument at home is an audience that recognizes manipulation before it works rather than after, which is exactly what prebunking research shows can be built.

**The ground is the defenders' half.** A government can regulate the platform or force its sale, as the TikTok case shows it can. Only a society can tend the ground underneath. Rigorous schools do that work. So does a defensible baseline of shared fact, and so do institutions of knowledge that have to earn trust back one accurate report at a time. No government can do it for them.

**What the target would not do.** It would not stop the Party's networks, match their volume, or build a domestic operation against the defenders' own citizens under any name. That claim remains an argument, offered on the same terms as every other argument in this chapter.

### What Accommodate Proves

It proves the objective function is governance, on both halves of the case. The Party governs its population's feed through a registry and a regulator that doubles as a Party office, and it governs its target audiences abroad through the same management plane. The defenders let revenue govern one half and leave the other with no owner at all.

It proves Agency is the target throughout. Not a belief, not a platform, but a population's capacity to decide what is true and choose what to do about it.

It proves the discipline cuts both ways. The Philippines campaign shows the defenders can misuse the same craft the Party runs at scale, which is why the ethics gate matters as much as the technique.

It proves the brittleness of a defense aimed at one instrument. Five and a half years removed one company, with the Party's approval, and left the class of weapon standing.

It proves that treating the platform and the practitioner as separate problems was itself part of the defect. The first edition ran these as two chapters, and a reader could reasonably conclude that TikTok's algorithm and a covert influence network were different kinds of threat requiring different kinds of answer. They are the same threat at two layers of one stack, aimed at the same target, and an office that owns one without the other owns half a problem. The merge that produced this chapter is not only an editorial choice. It states the argument in miniature.

As in every case, the Chinese people are not the authors. They live under the same machine, set to a different dial. A society with a high baseline of shared fact and functioning trust is close to immune to this weapon, because the seed dies in healthy soil. The deepest counter is not a policy toward the Party, but a society that has put its own mind back in order.

### Field Card

**What happened.** A Chinese company runs two versions of one recommendation engine, hardening children's attention at home while leaving it soft abroad, and the same state runs covert networks and psychological operations through the platforms this asymmetry created. Congress spent five and a half years forcing one divestiture, which the Party had to approve, while its own operations moved in weeks. No American office owns either the objective function behind the algorithm or the resilience of the audience these operations target.

**The gap.** Governance has no owner in two places at once, the objective function and the home audience's resilience.

**The fix.** Disclosure and independent audit of recommender objectives, paired with public attribution of foreign operations that labels without removing lawful speech, would give both halves of this case an owner.

**Where you could work on it.** Platform accountability and algorithm audit policy, psychological operations or open-source analysis inside government, or media literacy and prebunking research aimed at making a population a harder target.

## Military-Civil Fusion

*The Long Reach That Conscripts Every Company, Researcher, and Citizen*

**DOMAINS** Economic · Financial · Information · Psychological · **Legal (spine)** · Association · Cyber · Cultural · AI and Cognitive · Biological · Data and Surveillance

**DIMENSIONS** Time · Entropy · Topology · **Asymmetry (signature)** · Agency · Resonance · Phase · Gradient · Criticality · **Coherence (signature)**

**STRATAGEMS** Spine S3 · S8 · S14 · S17 · S24 · S31 · S35

**GAPS** Six, one at each layer. Signature gap at the Governance layer, where the Party runs a management plane and the defenders run unconnected lists.

In October 1944, in the Vosges Mountains of eastern France, the 442nd Regimental Combat Team fought for six days to reach a Texas battalion cut off in the forest. The 442nd was a unit of Japanese Americans, and many of its men had volunteered from internment camps where their own government held their families. It took more casualties than the number of men it rescued. It ended the war as the most decorated unit of its size and length of service in the history of the United States Army. \[2\]

They could prove their loyalty because Imperial Japan could not reach them. The claim concerns capability, not restraint. To coerce a Japanese American in 1942, Tokyo would have needed a current map linking him to relatives in Japan. It would have needed a channel across a severed Pacific, a way to verify his compliance and a law obliging him to obey. It had none of them. The government's own Munson and Ringle reports had already found the population overwhelmingly loyal. \[3\]

Run the thought experiment forward. Give Imperial Japan a statute obliging every citizen to assist its intelligence organs on demand. Give it a registry of emigrants' relatives, instant encrypted channels into any American city and the means to punish a mother for her son's refusal. The Nisei's loyalty would not change. His position would, because a hostile state would hold a lever on him that he did not ask for and could not refuse.

The Chinese Communist Party holds that lever today. The variable that changed since 1942 is not the character of the people. The regime's reach changed. Military-Civil Fusion is the deliberate construction of that reach.

The experiment's hardest result stands in every section that follows. Even where the reach is real, internment would have been the wrong answer, because it punishes the person who carries the lever. The answer is to cut the lever and shield the people.

### The Terms of This Case

**Ancestry is not loyalty.** The internment of Japanese Americans was a moral catastrophe and a strategic error. The threat from Military-Civil Fusion is never a person's Chinese ancestry. Any defense that drifts toward suspecting Chinese students, researchers or Chinese Americans as a class repeats the internment error and does the Party's work for it.

The actor throughout is the Party. Sun Yat-sen, who founded the Chinese republic, studied in Honolulu at the school now called Punahou and drew on Lincoln for his Three Principles of the People. \[4\] The Party is not his heir. The Party runs a local franchise of a European ideology, and its rule produced the deadliest famine in recorded history and the Cultural Revolution. \[5\]

The Chinese people are Military-Civil Fusion's first conscripts. A graduate student in an American laboratory, legally bound to surrender what she learns if the state demands it, is far more often the Party's conscript than its agent.

### 1. Architecture Vision

**Documented.** The National Security Law of 2015 made safeguarding national security a duty of every citizen and organization. The National Intelligence Law of 2017 obliges every organization and citizen to support, assist and cooperate with state intelligence work. A central commission for integrated military and civilian development, chaired by Xi Jinping, dates from January 2017. \[1\] \[6\]

**Argued.** The aim is to erase the line between the civilian economy and the People's Liberation Army. Any advance in a laboratory, company or university on the mainland then becomes available to the army, and any access the open world grants becomes a channel home.

### 2. The Party's Baseline

#### Business Architecture. The Capabilities It Fielded

The Legal domain carried the operation, because the statute supplies the compulsion that every other channel rests on.

**Economic.** Civilian hardware from Party-controlled manufacturers entered foreign critical systems through ordinary procurement. A state-owned rail maker whose parent sits on the Defense list won American transit contracts on price. Telecommunications equipment from Huawei and ZTE ran in rural American networks. \[7\] \[15\]

**Financial.** Firms tied to military modernization raised money in Western markets and sat in the index funds that hold retirement savings. *The Borrowed Knife* carries that record.

**Information.** Western laboratories publish, and the fusion system reads.

**Psychological.** The family at home is the lever. A researcher abroad knows that his parents live within the regime's reach, and the knowledge needs no threat to work.

**Legal.** The statutes are the master key. No purely private company or apolitical researcher exists in the Party's law where its security interest is concerned. A firm on the mainland cannot credibly promise a foreign partner that its technology will stay civilian, because the promise is not the firm's to make. \[1\]

**Association.** Talent programs, led by the Thousand Talents Plan, drew established researchers with funding and prestige. The United Front Work Department cultivates overseas communities and professional associations. \[8\]

**Cyber.** Intrusion takes whatever the legal and financial channels cannot reach.

**Cultural.** Universities on the mainland carry the fusion into education. The seven universities known as the Seven Sons of National Defence answer to the industry ministry that oversees defense production. \[9\]

**AI and Cognitive.** Commercial champions route algorithms and talent toward the army's pursuit of what it calls intelligentized warfare.

**Biological.** Genomics firms and the military medical academies gather genetic data and research at global scale.

**Data and Surveillance.** Data-sharing mandates fuse commercial information into the security apparatus.

#### Data Architecture. What It Knew

The Party knows which of its own firms serve the army, because it certifies them. A private firm that wants to make weapons needs four certificates. They cover the firm's quality system, its secrecy qualification, its research and production license and its standing as an equipment supplier. Firms list the certificates in their brochures and annual reports. \[10\]

The Party's registry of its own military supply chain therefore exists, and much of it sits in public filings. Its model of the targets abroad is the least documented part of its baseline, and the precision of the talent recruitment argues that one exists.

#### Application Architecture. The Routines It Ran

**Stratagem 24, Borrow the Road to Conquer Guo**, is the signature. Civilian collaboration and market access are the road, and military capability travels down it.

**Stratagem 3, Kill with a Borrowed Knife**, lets foreign universities and companies fund and perform the research before the results go home.

Stratagem 8 is **Openly Repair the Gallery Roads, March Secretly Through Chencang.** It runs an openly civilian enterprise whose output reaches the army.

**Stratagem 14, Borrow a Corpse to Resurrect the Soul**, repurposes civilian research and dual-use products for military ends.

**Stratagem 17, Cast a Brick to Attract Jade**, offers modest grants and positions to extract research worth many times their cost.

**Stratagem 31, The Beauty Trap**, cultivates the key people whose cooperation brings a laboratory's knowledge with them.

**Stratagem 35, Chain the Stratagems**, runs the channels as one engine. Investment opens the door, talent walks through it, and intrusion takes the rest. The record shows each channel. The chain is the framework's reading.

#### A Target Audience Note

Military-Civil Fusion's talent programs fit this form, and the researcher with family still reachable by Chinese law serves as the example.

**Documented.** The Thousand Talents Plan and successor programs recruited researchers with funding, laboratory space and prestige. \[8\] The National Intelligence Law binds any citizen or organization within reach of Chinese state authority, family members included, whether or not that person has ever worked for the state.

**Argued.** A target audience analysis would read the audience narrowly, as researchers and their immediate family who hold Chinese citizenship or residency and therefore fall within the law's reach, never as an ethnic or ancestral category. A Chinese American with no citizenship, no residency and no family on the mainland sits outside this audience entirely, whatever his name or appearance. Vulnerability runs through family location, not blood. Susceptibility runs highest where a researcher's parents, siblings or children live on the mainland and the researcher has not yet been asked for anything. The line rarely needs to be a threat, because the researcher can calculate the exposure himself. Accessibility runs through the Thousand Talents Plan's own recruiters, through professional associations the United Front Work Department cultivates, and through routine visits home that carry no risk until the law decides otherwise.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platform It Ran On

The platform is the management plane, and the next section describes it.

#### Principles and Governance. How It Held Together

**Asymmetry.** Western laboratories, markets and universities stay open by design. The Party's equivalents do not reciprocate, and its law obliges its entities to carry home what they find.

**Coherence.** The Party abolished by law the line between civilian and military that the West's whole protective architecture assumes. The record documents the statutes and the institutions. The single design behind them is the framework's argument.

### 3. The Management Plane and the Banality of the Machine

In a computer network the data plane carries the traffic, and a separate management plane configures every device. An administrator on the management plane can change any router's rules without touching the traffic itself. The Party built its state the same way.

**Documented.** Every unit of the People's Liberation Army from regiment up carries a political commissar beside its commander, and every company carries a political instructor. The Party requires state enterprises to write the Party committee's role into their articles of association. The securities regulator's governance code of 2018 requires listed companies to set up Party organizations. \[11\]

The plane reaches further into private firms. The Party's Organization Department claimed Party units in 73 percent of private firms in 2017. The Party-led business federation's own survey found 48 percent in 2018. More than 92 percent of the 500 largest private firms host Party units, and so did about 106,000 foreign-invested firms by the Party's count for 2016. \[12\] In the largest internet firms the state also holds special management shares with board rights, as *The Borrowed Knife* records.

**Documented.** A Party directive of 2014 found that the four weapons certificates connected poorly, with cumbersome reviews and long approval cycles. It ordered the systems linked and the cycles shortened. In 2015 the catalog of weapons items that required a license shrank by 62 percent. \[10\]

**Argued.** Read as an architecture, the commissar and the Party committee form an out-of-band management plane with administrator rights on every node that matters. Military-Civil Fusion is the routing policy pushed through that plane. The National Intelligence Law is the root credential that every node must accept. The 2014 directive is the Party running a systems-integration project on its own bureaucracy.

Mao Zedong stated the principle in 1938, in the middle of a war for survival. "Our principle is that the Party commands the gun, and the gun must never be allowed to command the Party," he wrote. \[18\] The commissar beside every PLA commander from regiment up is that principle staffed, not merely stated.

The machine runs on forms. Hannah Arendt called the thing she saw in Jerusalem in 1961 the banality of evil, the ordinary clerk doing ordinary work inside an extraordinary system. \[13\] Military-Civil Fusion needs no conspiracy in any single office. It needs certificates issued on schedule, study sessions held in the committee room and approval cycles kept short. A clerk who shortens a review cycle for a weapons license is doing his job well. The strength of the system is that almost no one inside it has to decide anything grave.

The defenders run on bureaucracy too. Their clerks keep lists, and each list is correct. The difference is architectural. The Party's clerks sit on one management plane, and the defenders' clerks sit in separate offices with no plane above them.

### 4. The Defenders' Baseline

No single enterprise defended the open world's laboratories and networks. Each list had its own owner.

#### Business Architecture. Who Owned What

**Lists against owners**

| List or control | Owner | What it does | Year |
|---|---|---|---|
| Entity List and Military End-User List | Commerce Department | Requires licenses for exports | Entity List from 1997 |
| Section 1260H list | Department of Defense | Bars Defense contracting from 2026 | 2021 |
| Non-SDN Chinese Military-Industrial Complex list | Treasury Department | Bars Americans from buying listed securities | 2021 |
| Covered List | Federal Communications Commission | Bars federal subsidies for listed telecommunications gear | 2021 |
| Proclamation 10043 | State Department | Denies student visas tied to fusion institutions | 2020 |
| Transit rolling-stock ban | Department of Transportation | Bars federal transit funds for listed rail makers | 2019 |
| Research security rules | Universities, under NSPM-33 | Requires disclosure and security programs | 2021 |

Laid over the capability map, the lists show seven owners, seven legal tests and seven clocks.

#### Data Architecture. What the Defenders Knew

The defenders' identity model counts ownership. Commerce's affiliates rule took effect on September 29, 2025. It extended Entity List restrictions to any firm at least 50 percent owned by a listed entity. The rule counts ownership and not control. \[14\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines the Defenders Ran

Each list runs its own designation routine, and each routine answers in court on its own terms. In March 2021 a federal judge blocked the Defense designation of Xiaomi, and Defense agreed to remove it that May. The investment ban then moved to Treasury's own list with a new test. \[16\]

The fixes arrive one product line at a time. Congress barred federal transit funds for listed rail makers in December 2019, after contracts had already gone to one. \[7\] The FCC ordered carriers to rip out Huawei and ZTE equipment. Congress funded $1.9 billion of the cost in December 2020 and the $3.08 billion shortfall in December 2024. \[15\] Commerce suspended the affiliates rule on November 10, 2025 as a concession in a trade agreement, six weeks after it took effect. The suspension runs until November 9, 2026. \[14\]

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms the Defenders Used

The defenders' networks ran the adversary's hardware. Rural carriers built on Huawei and ZTE equipment because it was cheap, and subway systems bought rail cars on price.

#### Principles and Governance. What Held the Defenders Together

The defenders kept their principles. Universities stayed open to the world's students, and courts made designations prove their case. Those principles are the thing worth defending. The defenders had no plane above their lists and no policy that decided what one designation should trigger in another office.

### 5. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | Built the talent pipeline across decades | Treated talent recruitment as scattered cases for years |
| **Entropy** | One plane configures every node | Seven owners keep seven lists |
| **Topology** | A star with every civilian node one hop from the army | A mesh in which discoveries do not reach the military by default |
| **Asymmetry** | Takes open access and grants none | Stays open by design |
| **Agency** | Its law makes every entity a potential node | Leaves each laboratory and firm to decide alone |
| **Resonance** | Investment, talent, partnership and intrusion aim at one target | Each control aims at its own target |
| **Phase** | Acquires continuously | Funded a hardware replacement over four years |
| **Gradient** | Grants and market access tilt each choice | Price tilted procurement toward the adversary's hardware |
| **Criticality** | Aims at semiconductors, AI, quantum and aerospace | Controls those fields list by list |
| **Coherence** | Governed through one management plane | No plane above the lists |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. Lists Without a Policy Engine

*Architecture Governance. The signature gap*

**Documented.** Seven owners keep seven lists with seven legal tests. Defense's list bars contracting, Treasury's bars investment and Commerce's governs exports. Congress's defense act for fiscal 2026 ordered a report on harmonizing the sanctions lists. \[16\]

**Argued.** In IT terms the defenders run access-control lists without a policy engine. Each list is correct, and no policy decides what a designation in one office should trigger in the others. The Party runs one management plane that configures every node.

#### Gap 2. Fixes Take Years and Can Be Traded

*Architecture Principles, Phase*

**Documented.** The FCC's replacement program waited four years for full funding. The transit ban came after an award. The affiliates rule lasted six weeks before a trade agreement suspended it. \[7\] \[14\] \[15\]

**Argued.** A control that a trade negotiator can suspend becomes a bargaining chip. The Party's acquisition cycle runs continuously, and the defenders' controls run on appropriations and negotiations.

#### Gap 3. The Defenders Count Ownership, the Party Holds Control

*Data Architecture*

**Documented.** The affiliates rule counts ownership at 50 percent. The Party controls firms through Party units, special management shares of about one percent and the four certificates. \[10\] \[12\] \[14\]

**Argued.** The defenders' identity model looks for the owner, and the Party's control runs through the management plane. A firm can sit outside every ownership test and still answer to a Party committee. The four certificates are a public signal of military supply that no list reads by default.

#### Gap 4. The Laboratory Decides Alone

*Business Architecture*

**Documented.** NSPM-33 of January 2021 required research institutions to run security programs and require disclosure. The State Department revoked more than a thousand student visas under Proclamation 10043 in 2020. A federal jury convicted the Harvard chemist Charles Lieber in December 2021 of lying about his ties to the Thousand Talents Plan. \[8\] \[17\]

**Argued.** Each university runs its own screen against a coordinated acquisition system. The one screen the defenders run centrally sorts people by institutional affiliation. The Lieber case shows the screen must target undisclosed funding, not ancestry. The screening capability has no owner above the individual laboratory.

#### Gap 5. Designation Routines Built One Statute at a Time

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** A federal judge blocked the Xiaomi designation in 2021, and Defense withdrew it. The investment ban moved to a new list with a new test. \[16\]

**Argued.** Each list grew from its own statute and answers in court on its own terms. The weakest routine sets the pace for the rest, and the adversary tests each one separately.

#### Gap 6. The Hardware in the Walls

*Technology Architecture*

**Documented.** Huawei and ZTE equipment ran in rural American networks until the FCC ordered it out. A rail maker whose parent sits on the Defense list won American transit contracts on price. \[7\] \[15\]

**Argued.** Civil procurement had no security function, so the adversary's hardware entered the defenders' platforms by the cheapest bid. Congress then fixed it one category at a time.

### 6. Target Architecture

The target cuts the lever and shields the people. It keeps universities open and never screens anyone by ancestry.

**Gap to target**

| Gap | Target element | Owner | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Lists without a policy engine | One designation schema, with a published matrix of what each designation triggers in every other office | National Strategic Competition Council, convening only | A statute that outlives an election cycle, and agencies that give up their separate tests |
| 2. Fixes take years and can be traded | Replacement programs funded in the same act that orders them, and security controls kept out of trade concessions | Congress and the Trade Representative | Budget up front, and less room to bargain |
| 3. The defenders count ownership | An identity test that counts control, including Party units, special management shares and the four certificates | Commerce and Treasury | Heavier due diligence, and harder evidence standards |
| 4. The laboratory decides alone | A shared screening service for research funding and direction, available to every university | National Science Foundation and research agencies | Money, and friction with academic freedom |
| 5. Designation routines built one statute at a time | Published criteria and an appeal route common to every list | The council's registry | More litigation up front |
| 6. The hardware in the walls | A procurement security screen by category rather than by product | Federal procurement and grant agencies | Higher prices, and fewer bidders |

**The guardrail governs every row.** Each element aims at the Party's legal and institutional reach. None aims at Chinese students or Chinese Americans as a class, because suspicion by ancestry hands the Party the outcome it wants.

**The affiliates rule is due back.** Commerce's rule returns on November 10, 2026 unless the government acts again. The target asks that its return count control as well as ownership.

**What the target would not have done.** It would not have closed the open world's laboratories, and it would not have stopped the Party's statutes. It would have given the defenders one plane above their lists. That claim remains an argument.

### What Military-Civil Fusion Proves

Military-Civil Fusion proves Asymmetry. A free society's openness is its attack surface, and a regime that fused its civilian and military sectors by law can convert that openness into capability faster than the target can screen it.

It proves the architecture of the banal. The Party's machine runs on commissars and committees, on certificates and short approval cycles. Almost no one inside it decides anything grave. The defenders run on lists kept by conscientious clerks in separate offices. Both run on bureaucracy. Only one has a plane above it.

It proves, most of all, whom the reach conscripts first. A law none of them voted for binds the researcher, the founder and the student abroad. In 1942 the United States interned a population against which no foreign lever existed. Today the lever exists, and the Party wrote it into law. The answer does not change. Cut the lever. Shield the people.

### Field Card

**What happened.** A 2015 law made every Chinese citizen and organization legally bound to assist state intelligence work on demand, with no exceptions for private firms or individual researchers. That law converted millions of ordinary commercial and academic relationships into potential channels for military technology transfer, whether or not any individual ever intended to serve the army. Seven separate American agencies built seven separate lists in response, each testing ownership rather than control, each running on its own legal clock.

**The gap.** No American office holds a policy engine above the seven lists, the signature gap at the Governance layer.

**The fix.** A single designation schema that publishes what each list triggers in every other office would replace seven uncoordinated tests with one.

**Where you could work on it.** Export control and sanctions policy, university research-security compliance, or a career at one of the seven listing agencies pushing toward the harmonized schema this chapter argues for.

*Sourcing notes and the full corrections record for this chapter appear in Appendix H.*

## The Long Arm

*Transnational Repression and the Family at Home*

**DOMAINS** Information · Psychological · Legal · **Association (spine)** · Cyber · Data and Surveillance

**DIMENSIONS** Time · Entropy · Topology · **Asymmetry (signature)** · Agency · Resonance · Phase · Gradient · Criticality · **Coherence (master)**

**STRATAGEMS** Spine S13 · S15 · S18 · S22 · S35

**GAPS** Six, one at each layer. Signature gap at the Data layer, where the defenders see the person and the Party sees the family.

In 2018 a man knocked on the door of a house in New Jersey, circled it to look inside and taped a note to the door. The house belonged to Xu Jin, a former official from Wuhan whom the Party wanted back to face bribery allegations he denies. The note said that if Xu went home and served ten years, his "wife and children will be all right." \[1\]

The note was one step in a campaign. The Party had targeted Xu and his wife under its Fox Hunt and Sky Net programs since 2012. From 2016 to 2019 its agents hired a retired New York police sergeant as a private investigator, surveilled the family and pressed them to return. A federal jury convicted three men in June 2023. They received sentences of 16, 18 and 24 months. The retired sergeant told the court he had never thought he was working for the Party. \[1\]

In early 2022 two men opened a branch of the Ministry of Public Security on a floor of a building in Manhattan's Chinatown. It renewed Chinese driver's licenses. It also identified pro-democracy activists living in the United States. The FBI arrested both men in April 2023, and one pleaded guilty in December 2024 to conspiring to act as an agent of the People's Republic. \[2\]

In September 2020 prosecutors charged Baimadajie Angwang, a Tibetan-born New York police officer and Marine veteran, with reporting on New York's Tibetans to the Chinese consulate. He spent about six months in detention. In January 2023 the government dropped every charge in the interests of justice and never explained why. His lawyer said the consulate contacts were attempts to get visas to visit his family. \[3\]

The record documents all three cases. The same lever runs through them, a person abroad tied to a family within the Party's reach. Reading them as one architecture is the framework's argument.

### The Terms of This Case

Transnational repression is a state reaching across borders to watch, threaten, coerce or return people it considers its own. Freedom House's decade of data, from 2014 to 2024, records 1,219 direct physical incidents by 48 governments. The Party's government committed 272 of them, more than any other. Freedom House calls the Party's effort the most sophisticated, global and comprehensive campaign of its kind. \[4\]

The actor is the Party. The targets are overwhelmingly Chinese. They include dissidents and former officials, Uyghurs and Tibetans. Hong Kongers, Falun Gong practitioners and students face the same pressure. Many are citizens of the country where the pressure finds them.

Mao named the instrument that organizes this reach in 1939. "The united front, armed struggle and Party building are the Chinese Communist Party's three magic weapons," he wrote. The united front came first on his list. \[8\] The United Front Work Department that answers to that principle today does not run the campaigns this case documents. It cultivates the hometown associations, professional groups and community organizations the campaigns hide inside, work Mao named as decisive eighty years before a Manhattan police station proved it.

This case carries the book's hardest test of its guardrail. The *Military-Civil Fusion* case holds that the answer is to cut the lever and shield the people. Here the lever is the family at home, and the people are the diaspora the Party pressures. A defense that treats the diaspora with suspicion does the Party's work twice. It isolates the target and it confirms the Party's claim that overseas Chinese have no home but the People's Republic.

The Angwang case belongs in this case for that reason. The record does not say what the new information was. It does show a man who carried the lever and bore the costs of both governments' attention.

### 1. Architecture Vision

**Documented.** The Party runs Fox Hunt and Sky Net as programs to return people it accuses of corruption. Its Ministry of Public Security operates abroad without host-country consent, as the Manhattan case shows. Hong Kong's police have posted bounties of HK$1 million on activists living overseas. \[1\] \[2\] \[5\]

**Argued.** The aim is a population with no exit. The Party means that leaving the mainland does not end its reach, so that no critic abroad speaks freely and no official abroad feels safe with what he took.

### 2. The Party's Baseline

#### Business Architecture. The Capabilities It Fielded

The Association domain carried the operation, because the method works through the ties between a person abroad and people at home.

**Information.** On the same day as the Manhattan arrests in April 2023, the Justice Department charged thirty-four officers of the Ministry of Public Security's 912 Special Project Working Group. Prosecutors said they ran thousands of fake online personas to harass dissidents abroad. \[2\]

**Psychological.** The note on Xu's door needed no violence. It named his wife and children.

**Legal.** The Party frames each return as law enforcement against fugitives. It denies running police stations abroad and calls them service centers.

**Association.** Hometown and community associations give the operation a local face. The Manhattan station occupied a floor of a community building.

**Cyber.** Harassment runs through fake accounts, doxxing and coordinated reporting that gets targets' accounts suspended.

**Data and Surveillance.** The dossier joins the person abroad to relatives at home.

#### Data Architecture. What It Knew

The Party's decisive data is a join. On one side sits the person abroad, located through open records, community contacts and private investigators. On the other sit the relatives at home. The Party can visit or detain them, cost them their jobs or deny them travel. The *Digital Twin* case describes the dossiers that hold both.

**Documented.** Amnesty International and other groups have recorded Uyghurs abroad whose relatives in Xinjiang faced detention or pressure after the Uyghurs spoke out. Fox Hunt prosecutors described relatives in China pressed to plead with the target. \[6\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines It Ran

The return campaign reads as a business process. It locates the target, then maps the family and applies pressure at home. It delivers an ultimatum abroad and closes with a return or a silence.

Criminal psychology has another name for the same shape. Behavioral analysts who study predatory control describe selection, isolation and extraction as stages a coercive relationship follows regardless of its domain. \[7\] The Party's campaign selects a target, isolates him by mapping and pressuring the family that once felt like support, and extracts a return or a silence. The pattern names a method used by a small number of officials. It says nothing about the millions of people abroad who experience none of it.

**Stratagem 15, Lure the Tiger Down from the Mountain**, draws the target out of the safety of a foreign jurisdiction.

**Stratagem 22, Shut the Door to Catch the Thief**, closes the exits through bounties and pressure on the family.

**Stratagem 18, Capture the Ringleader to Disband the Bandits**, aims at the organizers of diaspora opposition.

**Stratagem 13, Stomp the Grass to Scare the Snake**, lets one visible case frighten every other target.

**Stratagem 35, Chain the Stratagems**, links the location to the family and the family to the ultimatum. The record documents each step in the Fox Hunt trial. The chain across cases is the framework's reading.

#### A Target Audience Note

The pressure campaign against Xu Jin fits this form, and his case serves as the example.

**Documented.** The Party targeted Xu and his wife under Fox Hunt and Sky Net from 2012, hired a private investigator to surveil them from 2016 to 2019, and left a note at their door naming his wife and children by implication. \[1\]

**Argued.** A target audience analysis would read the audience as former officials and dissidents with family still on the mainland, not the diaspora broadly. Their vulnerability runs through exactly the tie that once anchored them to home, a family the Party can visit, detain or deny travel. The line of persuasion holds that the family's safety depends on the target's choice, stated plainly enough that no explicit threat is legally provable. Susceptibility runs highest where the target still believes the relationship with the mainland runs two ways, family loyalty as well as state reach. Accessibility runs through the family itself, which needs no recruitment and no payment, only its own existence within the Party's borders.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platform It Ran On

The overseas stations work as edge nodes of the management plane described in *Military-Civil Fusion*. Their front end offered ordinary services such as license renewals. Behind the front end sat the targeting.

#### Principles and Governance. How It Held Together

**Asymmetry.** The Party reaches into open societies, and no open society can reach the family in Xinjiang or Fujian.

**Coherence.** The public security ministry, the discipline commission, united-front networks and local police at home act on the same target. The record documents the programs and the cases. One design across them is the framework's argument.

### 3. The Defenders' Baseline

No single enterprise protects the targeted person. The FBI investigates crimes, local police take the first call, the State Department handles diplomats and schools handle students.

#### Business Architecture. Who Owned What

| Capability | Defender owner | Limits |
|---|---|---|
| Investigating agents of foreign governments | FBI and the Justice Department | Must prove the agent acted at a foreign government's direction |
| First response to threats | Local police | Rarely trained to recognize a foreign campaign |
| Diplomats who direct operations | State Department | Expulsion is political and rare |
| Students and scholars on campus | Universities | No mandate for foreign threats |
| Records that reveal an address | States and counties | Public by default |
| The family at home | No owner | Beyond reach |

#### Data Architecture. What the Defenders Knew

The defenders see the person in front of them. They seldom see the relative in a mainland city whose job or freedom the Party is using. Xu and his family tried to keep their address out of public records, as the Justice Department later noted. Records built for openness became the location layer the campaign needed. \[1\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines the Defenders Ran

The defenders' main routine is prosecution under the law against acting as an unregistered agent of a foreign government, often paired with stalking charges. The Fox Hunt verdict and the Manhattan plea show the routine working. The Angwang dismissal shows its risk. A charge that rests on proving direction by a foreign government can reach the wrong person, and a wrong charge lands on the community the Party already pressures. \[1\] \[2\] \[3\]

The timeline runs long. The Party targeted Xu from 2012. The verdict came in 2023 and the last sentence in April 2025.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms the Defenders Used

The defenders' platforms carried the harassment. The 912 group's fake personas ran on open American platforms. Public records carried the addresses.

#### Principles and Governance. What Held the Defenders Together

The defenders keep their principles. Their courts demand proof, their prosecutors dropped a case when the evidence changed, and their communities stay open to people from anywhere. The Angwang case shows those principles correcting an error. It also shows the cost of the error to a man who had done his government's work in Afghanistan.

### 4. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | Pursues a target for years | Resolves a case years after the pressure began |
| **Entropy** | One apparatus acts on target and family | The target navigates several offices alone |
| **Topology** | A hub that joins home and abroad | Offices that each see one piece |
| **Asymmetry** | Reaches abroad and holds the family at home | Cannot reach the family |
| **Agency** | Captures the target's choices through fear | Leaves the target to decide alone whether to report |
| **Resonance** | One visible case frightens every other target | Each case handled as a single crime |
| **Phase** | Applies pressure at home and abroad together | Answers after the ultimatum arrives |
| **Gradient** | Fear tilts the target toward silence | Fear of suspicion tilts the community away from reporting |
| **Criticality** | Aims at organizers and at people who hold money or secrets | Protects no one in advance |
| **Coherence** | Governed by one authority | No owner of the targeted person |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. No Owner of the Targeted Person

*Architecture Governance*

**Documented.** The FBI, local police, the State Department and universities each hold a piece of the response. \[1\] \[2\]

**Argued.** The target meets the Party's apparatus as one force and the defenders' response as a set of doors. A frightened person who does not know which door to try often tries none.

#### Gap 2. The Pressure Runs for Years

*Architecture Principles, Time*

**Documented.** The Party targeted Xu from 2012. His pressure campaign ran from 2016 to 2019, and the court sentenced the last defendant in April 2025. \[1\]

**Argued.** The defenders' routine closes a case. The Party's campaign persists across cases and across years, and the target lives inside it the whole time.

#### Gap 3. The Defenders See the Person, the Party Sees the Family

*Data Architecture. The signature gap*

**Documented.** Uyghurs abroad report relatives detained or pressed after they spoke out. Fox Hunt pressure ran through family members. Angwang's lawyer said his consulate contacts sought visas to visit family. \[3\] \[6\]

**Argued.** The lever is a record the defenders cannot see and a person they cannot protect. The Party holds both ends of the join. The defenders hold one, and they sometimes read the target's contact with the Party as a sign of loyalty to it.

#### Gap 4. The Community Is Venue and Victim

*Business Architecture*

**Documented.** The Manhattan station operated from a community building. The Angwang case, and the China Initiative's drift recorded in *Technology IP*, left parts of the Chinese American community wary of federal attention. \[2\] \[3\]

**Argued.** The Party hides its edge nodes inside the community it pressures. A defense that treats the community as suspect loses the reports it most needs. No public owner builds the trust that would bring those reports in.

#### Gap 5. The Law Names the Agent, Not the Act

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** Prosecutors charge acting as an unregistered foreign agent, stalking and obstruction. The Angwang charges collapsed. \[1\] \[2\] \[3\]

**Argued.** A routine built on proving who directs a person must investigate the person's ties to a foreign state. That puts the diaspora's family ties at the center of every case. A law aimed at the act of coercion on a foreign government's behalf would center the harm instead of the ancestry of the harmed.

#### Gap 6. The Front End Looked Like a Service Desk

*Technology Architecture*

**Documented.** The Manhattan station renewed licenses. The 912 group used fake personas on open platforms. \[2\]

**Argued.** The Party's edge nodes present ordinary interfaces to ordinary people. The targeting runs behind the interface, on platforms and records the defenders built to be open.

### 5. Target Architecture

Every element serves the guardrail. It cuts the lever and shields the people, and it never sorts anyone by ancestry.

**Gap to target**

| Gap | Target element | Owner | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. No owner of the targeted person | One protective owner with a single point of contact in community languages | The FBI, with local police and victim services | Staff, and trust built over years |
| 2. The pressure runs for years | Standing protection for known targets that outlasts any single case | The same owner | Long-term cost |
| 3. The defenders see the person | Treat contact under family pressure as evidence of coercion, not of loyalty, and document it that way | Investigators and prosecutors | Harder cases against real agents |
| 4. The community is venue and victim | Funded trusted intermediaries, and no screening by ancestry or origin | Community groups, with federal support | Money, and patience |
| 5. The law names the agent | A statute naming transnational repression as a crime of coercion on a foreign government's behalf, with published safeguards against misuse | Congress | Drafting difficulty, and civil-liberties review |
| 6. The front end looked like a service desk | Address confidentiality for targets, action against state-run fake personas and closure of undeclared foreign police offices | States, platforms and the State Department | Administrative cost, and diplomatic friction |

**Expel the directors.** A documented campaign traced to named officials should bring a standing, pre-agreed response against those officials and the units they serve, as the *Hong Kong* case argues for pre-authorized packages.

**Address confidentiality works already.** Many states run confidential-address programs for victims of domestic violence. Extending them to targets of foreign coercion costs little and shuts the location layer the campaign used against Xu.

**What the target would not do.** It would not free the family at home. Nothing a free society does in New Jersey reaches a relative in Wuhan. It would make the target easier to protect and harder to find, and it would make the community a partner rather than a suspect. That claim remains an argument.

### What the Long Arm Proves

It proves Asymmetry at its most personal. The Party reaches into open societies through the family it holds at home, and no open society can reach back.

It proves the lifecycle's Consume stage aimed at individuals. The Party locates the target and pressures the family. It controls the target's choices and consumes his freedom, whether he returns or falls silent.

It proves the guardrail is the strategy. A defense that treats the targeted community as suspect hands the Party both the victim and the argument. A defense that shields the community and cuts the lever takes both away.

The Angwang case belongs at the center of the lesson. The same lever that the Party pulled was the one his own government read as guilt, and the courts corrected the error only after it had cost him his liberty and his career. Cut the lever. Shield the people.

### Field Card

**What happened.** A former Wuhan official living in New Jersey found a note on his door naming his wife and children, the latest step in a campaign the Party had run against him since 2012 using hired investigators and two named repatriation programs. A federal jury convicted three men in 2023, and courts later dismissed unrelated charges against a New York police officer whose own consulate contacts were never proven to be anything but an attempt to see his family. Both cases turned on the same fact, a family on the mainland the target could not protect and the defenders could not reach.

**The gap.** No one on the defenders' side can see the family the Party is pressuring, the signature gap at the Data layer, and no single office owns the targeted person either.

**The fix.** A single protective point of contact for targets, paired with treating contact made under family pressure as evidence of coercion rather than loyalty, would close both gaps without policing the diaspora.

**Where you could work on it.** Federal law enforcement focused on transnational repression, victim services for diaspora communities, or legislative work drafting a statute that names coercion on a foreign government's behalf as the crime rather than the target's foreign ties.

*Sourcing notes and the full corrections record for this chapter appear in Appendix H.*

## Protocols and Ports

*The Logical Layer and the Physical Layer of the Same Supply Chain*

**DOMAINS** Kinetic · **Economic (spine)** · Information · **Legal (spine)** · Association · Cyber · AI and Cognitive · Data and Surveillance

**DIMENSIONS** **Time (signature)** · Entropy · Topology · Asymmetry · Agency · Resonance · Phase · Gradient · **Criticality (signature)** · **Coherence (master)**

**STRATAGEMS** Spine S4 · S8 · S10 · S17 · S24 · S30 · S35

**GAPS** Six, one at each layer. Two signature gaps. Principles, where the defenders' own controls kept their engineers out of the room. Technology, where the cranes carried a channel their owners never asked for.

In November 2016 engineers from around the world met in Reno and settled the coding scheme for the control channel of 5G mobile networks. They chose polar codes, a scheme Huawei had championed. Every 5G phone and base station in the world would carry it. \[1\]
