Cut from the meeting room to the waterfront. Nearly 80 percent of the ship-to-shore cranes at American ports come from one maker, Shanghai Zhenhua Heavy Industries. Known as ZPMC, the company is owned and operated in the People's Republic. \[2\] A standard fixes how a network talks. A crane is what moves the cargo the network describes. One is the logical layer of the supply chain and the other is the physical layer, and the two get read here as the same architecture question asked twice, at different altitudes.

The record documents both events. Reading them as one contest over the supply chain's architecture, logical and physical, is the framework's argument.

### The Terms of This Case

TOGAF gives every enterprise a Technology Architecture, the platforms and infrastructure its applications run on. A telecommunications standard and a shipping crane both belong to that layer, however different they look on the surface. A protocol fixes how devices talk, which functions sit in the network and which sit at the edge. A crane fixes what physically moves and what channels run inside the machine that moves it. Engineers write the world's standards in the International Telecommunication Union and 3GPP, in the Internet Engineering Task Force, and in ISO and the IEEE. A much smaller set of manufacturers build the machines that make those standards physical at a port, and one of those manufacturers builds most of them.

Participation in standards bodies is legitimate, and Chinese engineers contribute excellent technical work. A standard that wins on merit is good engineering, and this case does not treat any contribution as suspect because of its source. The same holds at the waterfront. ZPMC's cranes are well built and sell at a price few competitors can match, and the workers who build them are not agents of anyone. The case concerns the Party's strategy for the bodies and the enterprises, never the engineers who sit at a standards table or the welders who staff a crane factory.

The actor throughout is the Party. ZPMC, the shipping giant COSCO and China Merchants are state-owned enterprises, and the standards delegations answer to a national outline the Party's Central Committee co-issued. Neither fact makes the individual engineer or the individual dockworker an instrument of the state. Both facts mean the enterprise and the delegation answer, in the end, to one authority neither layer's defenders can see clearly from where they sit.

### 1. Architecture Vision

**Documented.** In October 2021 the Party's Central Committee and the State Council jointly issued an outline for national standardization development, setting targets for converting international standards into national ones and raising the country's role in international standards bodies. The outline grew from a research program known inside the Party as China Standards 2035. \[3\]

The joint House committee report of September 2024 described ties between the People's Liberation Army and three state enterprises, ZPMC, COSCO and China Merchants. It noted a ZPMC heavy-lift ship the PLA used in an exercise, evidence that the line between a commercial fleet and a military auxiliary runs thinner than a bill of lading suggests. \[2\]

**Argued.** The aim is to write the rules the world's products must obey and to build the machines that carry those rules into every port. A protocol written to fit one country's industry or surveillance model governs every device that follows it, wherever that device sells. A crane built by one country's state enterprise carries whatever channel that enterprise designs into it, wherever the crane lifts. Neither instrument needs to be used maliciously to be useful. Both only need to exist, in enough places, for long enough.

### 2. The Party's Baseline

#### The Logical Layer. Standards

The Legal domain carried this half, because a standard works as law for machines.

**Economic.** Standard-essential patents earn royalties from every device that implements a standard, and Huawei ranks among the leading declarers of patents essential to 5G. \[4\]

**Legal.** The Party's courts have reached beyond its borders on standards patents. In September 2020 the Wuhan Intermediate People's Court barred InterDigital from pursuing its case against Xiaomi in other countries, before InterDigital had appeared in the Wuhan court. In December 2023 a court in Chongqing set the first global royalty rate by a Chinese court, for Nokia's patents in a dispute with OPPO, setting a lower rate for the mainland than for the American market. The European Union requested WTO consultations over such anti-suit injunctions on February 18, 2022. \[5\]

**Association.** A Chinese national, Zhao Houlin, served as Secretary-General of the ITU from 2015 to 2022. \[6\]

**Cyber.** In September 2019 Huawei and state-owned partners proposed a redesign of internet protocols at the ITU, known as New IP. Critics said it would move control into the network and make it easier to shut off or trace users. The proposal did not advance. \[7\]

**AI and Cognitive.** In 2019 mainland firms proposed ITU standards for facial recognition and video surveillance, drawn from systems in use at home. \[7\]

**Stratagem 30, Make the Host and the Guest Exchange Places**, is the signature routine. A newcomer to a body contributes steadily, gains seats and chairs, and becomes the host that sets the agenda. **Stratagem 8** pairs open technical contributions with attached patent positions. Its full name is Openly Repair the Gallery Roads, March Secretly Through Chencang. **Stratagem 4, Wait at Ease for the Weary Enemy**, outlasts delegations that attend only when their firms can spare the travel.

#### The Physical Layer. Ports and Cranes

The Economic domain carried this half, because the capability rests on building the world's port equipment more cheaply than anyone else.

**Kinetic.** Ports carry military cargo in war. A crane that stops, or a schedule that leaks, affects deployment.

**Economic.** ZPMC dominates the world market for ship-to-shore cranes. The Trade Representative found on January 16, 2025 that the Party had targeted the maritime, logistics and shipbuilding sectors for dominance. \[2\] \[8\]

**Cyber.** In September 2024 two House committees released a joint report finding cellular modems installed on cranes in operation at certain American ports, not needed to run the cranes, creating an obscure way to collect information and bypass the ports' firewalls. Some ports had declined remote monitoring in their contracts and found the modems anyway. ZPMC had repeatedly requested remote access and denied installing the modems. The report said the installer's identity remains unclear. \[2\]

**Data and Surveillance.** LOGINK is the Party's national transportation and logistics data platform. It tracks shipments, routing and cargo values drawn from customs forms. The Party offers it free of charge. More than twenty ports have partnered with it, including ports in Japan, South Korea and Europe. \[9\]

**Stratagem 10, Hide a Knife Behind a Smile**, offers the cheapest cranes and a free data platform alike. **Stratagem 17, Cast a Brick to Attract Jade**, gives away the platform to gain the data it carries back. **Stratagem 24, Borrow the Road to Conquer Guo**, rides commercial trade into the ports a military will use.

**Stratagem 35, Chain the Stratagems**, links both layers into one engine. A contribution earns a patent, and a patent earns a rate the Party's own courts can set for the world. The same pattern runs one level down. A cheap crane earns a contract, a contract earns a channel, and a channel earns a view of the port. The record documents each link at each layer. The chain across both is the framework's reading.

#### Governance. How Both Layers Held Together

**Time.** A standard lasts a generation of devices. Influence won in one meeting cycle pays for fifteen years, and a crane bought today runs for as long.

**Criticality.** A port is a chokepoint, and a crane is the chokepoint inside it. A protocol is a chokepoint too, quieter, sitting inside every device that implements it rather than on one waterfront.

**Coherence.** The Party's national outline coordinates delegations, champions and courts at the logical layer. State enterprises build the cranes, run the ships and operate the data platform at the physical layer. The record documents the outline, the ownership and the court cases. One design across both layers is the framework's argument.

### 3. The Defenders' Baseline

No single enterprise owns either layer, and at the logical layer that is deliberate. American standards are led by the private sector, voluntary and built on consensus. At the physical layer it is not deliberate. Port authorities are local, terminal operators are private, and federal agencies each hold a slice.

#### Business Architecture. Who Owned What

| Capability | Defender owner | Instrument |
|---|---|---|
| Coordinating federal use of standards | National Institute of Standards and Technology | Federal policy favoring voluntary consensus standards |
| Writing the standards | Companies, universities and engineers | Their own time and travel budgets |
| Export controls on standards-relevant technology | Commerce Department | The Entity List and its standards rules |
| Port operations and procurement | Local port authorities and private terminal operators | Contracts and lowest bids |
| Cyber defense of port operational technology | Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency | Guidance and advisories |
| Trade remedies at either layer | Trade Representative | Section 301 findings and fees |
| Domestic crane manufacturing | No domestic maker | None |

The export-control routine collided with the standards routine at the logical layer. Commerce placed Huawei on the Entity List in May 2019. It then answered the standards-participation question in pieces. An advisory opinion came in 2019. A rule covering Huawei alone followed in June 2020, a broader rule in September 2022, and another in July 2024. Commerce itself said the uncertainty had undermined American participation and leadership in standards work. \[10\]

The coalition routine worked when the defenders used it. On September 29, 2022 the American candidate Doreen Bogdan-Martin won the ITU's top post with 139 votes against 25 for Russia's candidate, out of 172 cast. \[6\]

At the physical layer, the defenders learned about the crane modems through a congressional investigation and port officials' own inspections. Congress acted on LOGINK in the defense act for fiscal 2024, barring the Pentagon from using any seaport that relies on the platform and barring federal funding for such ports. Allied ports in Japan, South Korea and Europe continued to partner with it regardless. \[9\] \[11\]

**The shipping clock**

| Date | Owner | Routine | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| March 2024 | Five labor unions | Petition to the Trade Representative | Investigation opened |
| January 16, 2025 | Trade Representative | Finding that the Party targeted shipbuilding and logistics | Grounds for remedies |
| October 14, 2025 | Trade Representative | Fees on Chinese-built and Chinese-operated ships | The Party imposed its own fees on American-linked ships the same day |
| November 10, 2025 | Both governments | Fees suspended for one year in a trade agreement | Due to resume November 10, 2026 |

The Party's answer went further than a mirror. Its fees reached ships owned or operated by Americans whatever their flag, and it sanctioned American units of a South Korean shipbuilder helping revive American shipbuilding. \[8\] The same trade agreement suspended Commerce's affiliates rule and the Party's rare-earth export rule, as *Military-Civil Fusion* and *The Digital Twin* record. All three instruments reach the end of their suspensions within days of each other around November 10, 2026. The record found for this edition shows none of the three extended, reversed or replaced. \[12\]

The physical layer also carries an unresolved case worth watching past this book's printing. CK Hutchison agreed in March 2025 to sell most of its global port portfolio, including the Panama Canal terminals at Balboa and Cristóbal, to a consortium led by BlackRock and MSC for $22.8 billion. The Party objected and pressed for COSCO to take a controlling stake in the buying consortium rather than the minority position first discussed. Panama's Supreme Court then ruled CK Hutchison's own concession for Balboa and Cristóbal unconstitutional, voiding it outright and removing the Panama terminals from the deal entirely. MSC's terminal arm took over Cristóbal on an interim contract while Panama prepares a fresh, competitive concession. Negotiations over the remaining forty-odd ports continued through the run-up to this edition without a signed agreement, still contested over whether a Chinese state carrier joins the buyer's side at all. \[13\]

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms the Defenders Used

At the logical layer, the open internet runs on protocols from the Internet Engineering Task Force, where individual engineers participate on the merit of their work. That model helped keep New IP from advancing. \[7\] At the physical layer, the defenders' ports run on the cranes they could buy. With no domestic maker and costlier alternatives from Europe and Japan, ports bought on price. \[2\]

#### Principles and Governance. What Held the Defenders Together

The defenders keep their principles at both layers. Standards win on merit, companies bear their own costs, and no ministry dictates a vote. Ports run locally, buy competitively and trade openly. Those principles build good standards and bring cheap cranes. They also leave national interest in the standards bodies to whoever can afford to attend, and leave the question of what else a crane might carry to nobody in particular.

### 4. A Target Audience Note

The procurement officer who buys the crane, and the small firm that cannot afford to send an engineer to Geneva, fit this form together. Both are ordinary professionals making a defensible decision inside incentives nobody designed against them.

**Documented.** American participation in standards bodies rests on company and university travel budgets, and the 2023 National Standards Strategy called for a larger standards workforce without funding individual attendance. \[14\] Port procurement runs on competitive bids that score price and lifting capacity. No line item in that process asks what communication channels a crane carries. \[2\]

**Argued.** A target audience analysis would read this audience as the professional whose job rewards exactly the choice that leaves a gap open, not as anyone acting in bad faith. The standards engineer who skips a costly trip and the port official who accepts the cheapest qualified bid are both doing their jobs correctly by every metric either of them is measured against. Vulnerability runs through the metric, not the person. Susceptibility runs highest wherever a budget line has no security question attached to it. Accessibility requires no recruitment at all, because the audience does not have to be reached. It only has to be left alone.

### 5. The Formal Version of the Acceptance Test

NIST SP 800-207's zero trust rule, named in the Stack, verifies every request rather than trusting one permanently. Executive Order 14028 of May 2021 gave that rule a physical form for exactly this problem. It requires federal software suppliers to produce a software bill of materials, a list of every component inside a piece of code, so a buyer can check what it is acquiring rather than trusting the label on the box. \[15\]

A crane is not software, but it runs on software, firmware and a set of physical communication channels no procurement contract currently itemizes. A hardware bill of materials lists every chip, modem and channel in a piece of operational technology. That list is the crane acceptance test's formal name. The 2024 congressional report found modems that no contract had ordered because no bill of materials existed to check the crane against. The fix this chapter's target table proposes for Gap 5 is a hardware bill of materials by another name, applied to a machine instead of a codebase.

### 6. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | Plans standards influence and crane dominance across decades | Attends where the next product needs it; found modems after years in service |
| **Entropy** | One outline aligns ministries, champions, courts and state enterprises | Firms, agencies, ports and diplomats each act separately |
| **Topology** | A national program behind delegations and a hub of state enterprises | Many firms and many ports, each for itself |
| **Asymmetry** | Reads every body and every port; offers free platforms abroad | Its own controls kept engineers out of rooms; barred a platform at home allies still use |
| **Agency** | Seats gained by attendance; access requested and, denied or not, obtained | Seats held only where firms pay; declined a service and got the channel anyway |
| **Resonance** | Contribution, patent, court, machine and data point one way | Standards, export, trade and procurement routines collide with each other |
| **Phase** | Contributes every cycle; answered fees the same day they were imposed | Took five years to settle one export question; suspended its own fees within four weeks |
| **Gradient** | State support tilts firms and buyers toward its standards and its cranes | Cost tilts small firms out of standards work and every port toward the cheapest bid |
| **Criticality** | Aims at protocols that fix control points and the chokepoint inside every port | Defended the open internet where engineers showed up; has no domestic crane maker |
| **Coherence** | Governed by a Party-issued outline and by state enterprises alike | A strategy with no owner of attendance, and a maritime stack with no owner at all |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. No Owner, at Either Layer

*Architecture Governance*

**Documented.** The 2023 National Standards Strategy set priorities without funding attendance. \[14\] Local authorities buy the cranes. The Coast Guard, CISA, Defense and the Trade Representative each hold a piece of the physical layer. None of the seven capabilities in the business-architecture table above answers to any of the others. \[2\] \[11\]

**Argued.** A private-sector model is the right model for standards, and it needs a public backstop it does not have. A locally run port system is the right model too, and it needs the same thing. Both gaps are the identical shape at two different altitudes, a good design principle left to fund and defend itself.

#### Gap 2. Years to Decide, Traded Away Within Weeks

*Architecture Principles, Time. Signature gap*

**Documented.** Commerce listed Huawei in 2019 and did not settle standards-participation rules until 2024. \[10\] The October 2025 port fees went into suspension by November 10, alongside two export-control rules. All three reach the same expiry date around November 10, 2026. \[8\] \[12\]

**Argued.** A control aimed at the adversary reached the defenders' own engineers for five years, and a standard fixed in those years governs a generation of devices, so the delay costs far longer than five years. A security instrument a trade negotiator can suspend in a matter of weeks becomes a bargaining chip rather than a defense, and three such chips now share one expiry date the defenders should meet with a decision already made rather than a negotiation still open.

#### Gap 3. Everything Public, Nothing Mapped

*Data Architecture*

**Documented.** Standards bodies publish officers, contributions and patent declarations. \[4\] LOGINK is free, partners with more than twenty ports and tracks cargo, value and routing. American law bars the Pentagon from ports that rely on it, and allied ports use it anyway. \[9\] \[11\]

**Argued.** Everything needed to see who holds which chairs, which patents and which data flows sits in public records at both layers. The defenders keep no national map of either one, so they notice a lost seat or an exposed port only after the fact.

#### Gap 4. The Capability Sits in the Adversary's Factories and Firms

*Business Architecture*

**Documented.** American standards participation rests on private budgets with no dedicated funding, and the 2023 strategy named the gap without closing it. \[14\] American ports depend on ZPMC for nearly 80 percent of their cranes, with no domestic maker and only costlier European and Japanese alternatives to turn to instead. \[2\]

**Argued.** Fields where American firms do not lead go unrepresented at the standards table, one missed meeting at a time, and the crane market shows what that same absence costs once it compounds for decades rather than years. Diversifying either capability costs money up front, against a competitor who built the current position over a horizon measured in plans, not quarters. The cost is the price of the chokepoint at whichever layer it sits, and the price only rises the longer the gap stays open.

#### Gap 5. No Test Before Acceptance

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** The Party's courts set global patent rates through anti-suit injunctions the WTO case now contests. \[5\] Port officials found modems during inspections, including on cranes whose contracts had declined remote services, because no bill of materials existed to check against. \[2\]

**Argued.** Neither routine asks the acceptance question before the fact. A patent enters a standard without a public rate-setting check, and a crane enters a port without a communication-channel inventory. Both gaps close with the same instrument, an acceptance test performed before the thing is accepted rather than a dispute litigated after.

#### Gap 6. The Quiet Channel

*Technology Architecture. Signature gap*

**Documented.** The facial-recognition standards New IP's failure did not stop entered ITU study groups where fewer defenders attended. \[7\] The crane modems connected to control computers, served no operational need and could bypass firewalls the port thought it controlled. \[2\]

**Argued.** The open internet's defense held where engineers crowded the room, and it thinned in the quieter study groups where they did not. The port's defense held for lifting capacity and price, and it never extended to the channel riding along with both. An unrequested channel is the risk operational-technology security teams fear most, whether it rides in a protocol or in a machine, because it sits outside the boundary the owner believes it is defending.

### 7. Target Architecture

The target keeps standards private, voluntary and won on merit. It keeps ports open, local and competitive. At both layers it makes sure the defenders show up and know what they accepted.

**Gap to target**

| Gap | Target element | Owner | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. No owner, at either layer | A funded owner that tracks standards attendance and convenes the maritime stack, without directing votes or nationalizing ports | NIST and the National Strategic Competition Council | Budget, and care to avoid a state line in private and local bodies |
| 2. Years to decide, traded in weeks | A standing rule that export controls never restrict published standards work, and security instruments kept out of trade bargains | Commerce, the White House and the Trade Representative | Scrutiny against loopholes, and less room to bargain |
| 3. Everything public, nothing mapped | A public map of standards seats and patents, and an open allied logistics data standard as an alternative to LOGINK | NIST, Transportation and allied governments | Analytic effort, and competition with a free product |
| 4. Capability sits abroad | Travel grants for standards participation, and allied or domestic crane production | Research agencies, Transportation and Defense, with allies | Money, and years to build capacity |
| 5. No test before acceptance | Allied cooperation against anti-suit injunctions, and a hardware bill of materials required before any operational-technology equipment is accepted | The Trade Representative and Port authorities, under Coast Guard rules | Diplomatic effort, and longer procurement |
| 6. The quiet channel | Expert attendance at surveillance-standard study groups, and removal or isolation of unrequested channels on operational technology | NIST, State and CISA | Time from scarce experts, and specialist staff |

**The coalition already won once.** The 2022 ITU election showed allies can hold a body's leadership when they organize. The target applies that same effort to the working groups where the architecture gets written, and to the ports where it gets built.

**What the target would not do.** It would not turn American engineers into ministry delegates or exclude a good contribution by its origin, and it would not close ports to the world's ships or treat a crane worker as an agent. Instead it would put the defenders in the room, and give every machine they accept an inventory. That claim remains an argument.

### What Protocols and Ports Proves

It proves Technology Architecture is one layer with two altitudes. A protocol and a crane look like different subjects until the same six gaps turn up in both, in the same order, for the same underlying reason.

It proves Time and Criticality together. A standard fixed once governs a generation of devices, and a chokepoint built once governs a generation of cargo. The side that shows up every cycle writes more of the future at both altitudes, and the side that shows up late inherits a design it did not choose.

It proves again that the defenders' own controls can do the adversary's work. An Entity List aimed at Huawei left American engineers unsure of their place in the room for five years, the same years a standard was being fixed for the world. Nobody in Washington intended that outcome. The architecture produced it anyway, the way a bureaucracy produces outcomes nobody in the room chose.

It proves that a chokepoint does not announce itself as one. A crane looks like a crane. A coding scheme looks like an engineering choice. Both are, right up until the day either one carries a channel or a rate or a court order that the buyer never priced into the decision to accept it.

### Field Card

**What happened.** Engineers meeting in Reno in 2016 fixed a 5G coding standard the whole world now carries, while export controls aimed at one company left American engineers unsure for five years whether they could even sit in the room where such decisions get made. At the same time, one state-owned company came to build most of the ship-to-shore cranes at American ports, some of which turned out to carry communication modems nobody had ordered. Standards and cranes are the same architecture layer, decided in a meeting room or built in a factory, and both arrived at American ports with no inventory of what exactly had been accepted.

**The gap.** No one owns attendance at the standards table, and no one owns the maritime stack, the same governance gap appearing at two different altitudes.

**The fix.** A funded standards-attendance program paired with a hardware bill of materials required before any port accepts new equipment would close both gaps with one design. Know who is in the room, and know what is in the machine.

**Where you could work on it.** Standards diplomacy at NIST, the State Department or an industry consortium. Supply-chain security for critical infrastructure. Export-control and trade-remedy law working the seam between security policy and negotiated trade deals.

## COVID-19

*Order at Home, Disorder Abroad*

**DOMAINS** Economic · Information · Psychological · Legal · Association · Cyber · Cultural · AI and Cognitive · **Biological (spine)** · Data and Surveillance

**DIMENSIONS** Time · **Entropy (signature)** · Topology · Asymmetry · Agency · Resonance · Phase · Gradient · Criticality · **Coherence (master)**

**STRATAGEMS** Spine S1 · S5 · S7 · S10 · S14 · S18 · S35

**GAPS** Six, one at each layer. Signature gap at the Data layer, where the evidence the world needed sat inside the adversary's jurisdiction.

On December 30, 2019, an ophthalmologist at Wuhan Central Hospital named Li Wenliang warned a group of medical-school classmates about a SARS-like illness. His hospital was isolating patients, and he urged his friends to protect themselves. On January 3, 2020, police made him sign a statement admitting he had made false comments that disturbed the social order. He went back to work, caught the disease and died of it on February 7, 2020. He was thirty-three. \[1\]

On January 11 the scientist Zhang Yongzhen released the viral genome to the world against orders to stay silent. \[2\] Wuhan's authorities had already told the public there was no clear evidence of human-to-human transmission. The World Health Organization relayed the same line on January 14. \[3\] \[4\] The Party locked down Wuhan's eleven million people on January 23, and the WHO declared a public-health emergency on January 30. By then the rest of the world had lost the weeks it needed. \[5\]

Two years later the Party's containment machine reached its peak. Shanghai's twenty-five million people entered lockdown in late March 2022. City health officials separated infected children from their parents until public anger forced a change. In June, depositors trying to protest frozen savings in Henan found their phone health codes turned red, and police took them to quarantine. \[12\] \[13\]

In March 2025 a federal judge in Missouri entered a default judgment of about $24.5 billion against the People's Republic, the Chinese Communist Party and seven other defendants. He found they had hoarded protective equipment while hiding what they knew. None of the defendants appeared to contest the evidence. \[9\]

The record documents each of these events. Reading them as one operation of one enterprise, and setting the Party's containment beside its own Cultural Revolution, is the framework's argument.

### The Terms of This Case

One question stays outside this case. Whether the virus came from a natural spillover or a laboratory accident remains unresolved, and the world's intelligence agencies disagree. The case needs no answer. It reads the Party's documented conduct, which stands on the record wherever the virus began.

The actor throughout is the Party, and the distinction is sharper here than anywhere. The first victims of the suppression were Chinese. Police silenced Li Wenliang, and then the disease killed him. Zhang Yongzhen defied an order to release the genome. Officials sealed the citizens of Wuhan and Shanghai into their homes. The Party took the truth from the Chinese people before it took it from anyone else.

The case also sets three seasons of pressure side by side. The Cultural Revolution was the Party's own. Zero-COVID was the Party's own. The pandemic years in Western democracies were not the Party's, and the comparison states the differences as plainly as the likenesses.

### 1. Architecture Vision

**Documented.** The Party suppressed the early warning, sealed the evidence and cornered the world's protective equipment in the first months of 2020. It then promoted its containment as proof that its system outperforms democracies. \[1\] \[7\] \[9\]

**Argued.** The operation had one aim that shifted as the crisis moved. It first protected the Party from domestic blame. It then deflected the question of origin. Finally it turned a catastrophe into a demonstration that centralized authority works and free societies fail.

### 2. The Party's Baseline

#### Business Architecture. The Capabilities It Fielded

The Biological domain carried the operation, not as a claim about the virus but as the suppression of information about it.

**Economic.** Between January and March 2020 the Party's entities bought up protective equipment on a global scale while its officials minimized the risk abroad. It then redistributed masks as diplomacy. The Missouri court found the hoarding violated federal and state antitrust law. \[9\] When Australia called for an independent inquiry into the origin, the Party struck its exports of barley and wine. \[11\]

**Information.** At home the censors scrubbed the warnings and punished the people who gave them. Abroad a foreign ministry spokesman suggested in March 2020 that the American army might have brought the virus to Wuhan. \[6\]

**Psychological.** The message to the world was constant. Centralized authority protects its people, and democracies let them die.

**Legal.** Police reprimanded Li Wenliang for rumor. The same legal machinery later enforced sealed buildings and quarantine orders without any court to hear an appeal.

**Association.** The Party's most consequential move ran through the World Health Organization. Its director-general won election in 2017 with the Party's backing. An Associated Press investigation later documented the gap between the WHO's private frustration at how little the Party shared and its public praise for the Party's transparency. \[4\] \[7\]

**Cyber.** In July 2020 the Justice Department indicted two hackers working with the Party's Ministry of State Security. Their targets included firms researching COVID-19 vaccines and treatments. \[10\]

**Cultural.** The Party's overseas network pressed to strip Wuhan's name from the disease within days of the emergency declaration.

**AI and Cognitive.** The domestic platforms steered public anger away from the center and toward local officials, then flooded feeds with praise for the leadership's response.

**Biological.** Officials ordered early samples destroyed and kept the raw patient data, the original sequences and the laboratory records from outside investigators. \[8\]

**Data and Surveillance.** Health-code apps put the whole population on a color scale. Green allowed movement, and red required quarantine.

#### Data Architecture. What It Knew

The Party held the evidence the world needed. It held the first patient records, the earliest sequences and the laboratory data in Wuhan. When the WHO's joint mission reached Wuhan more than a year later, investigators did not receive the raw data that mattered. \[8\]

The Missouri court drew its own inference from the record. On an uncontested evidentiary hearing, the judge found that the Party's pattern of conduct strongly suggested knowledge of human-to-human transmission as early as September 2019. He described a "deliberate campaign to suppress information" in support of the hoarding. \[9\] Those findings rest on evidence no defendant contested.

The health code was the Party's live model of its population. In June 2022 the city of Zhengzhou turned the codes of 1,317 bank depositors red to stop a protest over frozen savings. Of those, 446 were already in the city, and officials took many of them to quarantine hotels and sent them home. \[13\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines It Ran

**Stratagem 5, Loot a Burning House**, is the signature routine. The Party used a real catastrophe to corner the world's protective equipment and to sell its system abroad.

**Stratagem 1, Deceive the Heavens to Cross the Sea**, held the line of no clear human transmission while the evidence ran the other way.

**Stratagem 10, Hide a Knife Behind a Smile**, praised global cooperation in public while withholding the data cooperation required.

**Stratagem 14, Borrow a Corpse to Resurrect the Soul**, used the WHO's authority to carry the Party's account of events.

**Stratagem 7, Create Something from Nothing**, planted the claim that the American army brought the virus to Wuhan.

**Stratagem 18, Capture the Ringleader to Disband the Bandits**, silenced the doctors who gave the warning before the warning could organize anyone.

**Stratagem 35, Chain the Stratagems**, linked the rest. Suppression bought time, the time bought the equipment, and the equipment bought the diplomacy. The record shows each link. The chain is the framework's reading.

#### A Target Audience Note

The democratic public living through its own government's early confusion fits this form, and the spring of 2020 serves as the example.

**Documented.** A foreign ministry spokesman promoted the army-origin claim on an open American platform in March 2020. \[6\] The Party redistributed masks as diplomacy in the same months its own hoarding had helped create the shortage abroad. \[9\] \[11\] The Associated Press documented the World Health Organization's public praise for the Party's transparency running alongside its private frustration at how little the Party shared. \[7\]

**Argued.** A target audience analysis would read the audience as the citizen of a democracy watching his own hospitals run short of masks and his own officials contradict each other in real time, not the Party's domestic population, who received a different message entirely. Vulnerability runs through the visible chaos of an early emergency, when a free press reports every shortage and every reversal as it happens. The line of persuasion holds that centralized authority protects its people and that democracies let them die, a comparison that lands hardest exactly when the free society's own machinery is running at its worst and the Party's is presenting its best face. Susceptibility runs highest among audiences already inclined to distrust their own institutions. Accessibility runs through open platforms the Party's messaging could reach and its own citizens could not, through mask shipments timed for cameras, and through a captured referee whose public praise required no persuasion of its own.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platform It Ran On

The Party's platform was its own sealed information space set against the open one abroad. It could censor at home and speak freely on foreign platforms that its citizens could not reach. The health-code apps ran on the national mobile payment and messaging platforms, so the classification reached every phone.

#### Principles and Governance. How It Held Together

**Entropy.** The Party held its interior at low entropy through lockdown and surveillance. It let the disorder escape outward into open societies that had lost their weeks of warning.

**Coherence.** One authority ran the censorship, the procurement, the diplomacy and the containment. The record documents each channel. The single design behind them is the framework's argument.

### 3. The Fourth Age of the Instrument

The book traces one instrument through the Party's history. The class labels of the 1950s, the categories of the Cultural Revolution and the algorithmic classification of Xinjiang sort a population into groups. Each makes the sorting the master fact about a person. The health code is the same instrument in its fourth technological age.

**Documented.** The health code decided who could move, work, shop or travel. Officials in Zhengzhou turned the codes of 1,317 depositors red to stop a protest. Rights lawyers reported the same treatment when they traveled. Zhengzhou's discipline authorities punished five officials on June 22, 2022. One of them directed the stability-maintenance department of the municipal Party committee's political and legal committee. Depositors' codes turned red again three weeks later. \[13\]

**Argued.** The Cultural Revolution assigned a political label and let the crowd enforce it. Zero-COVID assigned a medical label and let an app and a neighborhood committee enforce it. The label changed its grammar and kept its function.

### 4. Three Seasons of Pressure

The Cultural Revolution, the Party's Zero-COVID years and the Western pandemic years each put ordinary people under pressure from authority and from their neighbors. Raw death totals mislead across populations and time spans, so the death table gives rates per 100,000 people per year.

**Mechanisms**

| | Cultural Revolution, 1966 to 1976 | Zero-COVID, 2020 to 2022 | Western pandemic years, 2020 to 2022 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Children** | Students mobilized against teachers and parents. Students beat a Beijing vice principal, Bian Zhongyun, to death in August 1966 | Shanghai separated infected children under seven from their parents until protest forced a change | Long school closures. American mathematics scores fell in 2022 by the largest margin on record |
| **The old and the sick** | Doctors and experts purged as class enemies | Residents sealed in buildings, with reported delays to emergency care | Nursing homes closed to visitors from March 2020. By June 2020 about 41 percent of American adults had delayed or avoided medical care, and hospitals postponed elective procedures |
| **Mobbing and speech** | Red Guard crowds and struggle sessions | Neighborhood committees and suited enforcers sealed buildings and ran the quarantines | Officials and peers targeted dissenting scientists. The NIH director asked for a "quick and devastating" takedown of one group's premises |
| **Labels and access** | Class labels decided a family's fate | Health codes decided who could move, and officials turned codes red to stop protesters | Vaccine status governed entry to workplaces, restaurants and venues in some cities. Air travelers faced test and vaccine rules, while the land border ran under separate rules and migrants released inland faced no vaccine requirement |
| **Work and livelihood** | Millions of urban youths sent to the countryside, and officials sent to labor schools | Factories and shops closed for weeks under lockdown | Orders closed small shops as nonessential while large retailers stayed open. Active business owners fell by about 22 percent between February and April 2020. The military discharged more than 8,000 service members for refusing the vaccine |
| **Money** | Red Guards raided homes and seized property | Depositors blocked from reaching their frozen savings | Canada froze protesters' bank accounts under emergency law |
| **The brake** | None. The decade ended with Mao's death | None in law. Protests and cost ended the policy in December 2022 | Courts struck down measures, and voters kept the power to replace governments |
| **The aftermath** | Children who denounced parents apologized decades later, in a country that still censors the history | The Party declared victory and understated the toll of its exit | A targeted scientist became director of the NIH in 2025. A Canadian court ruled the account freezes unconstitutional |

**Deaths per 100,000 people per year**

| Period and place | Deaths | Rate | What drove them |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cultural Revolution, 1966 to 1976 | One to two million by mainstream estimates, some higher | About 12 to 24 a year across the decade, concentrated in the late 1960s | Persecution, killing and suicide under pressure |
| Zero-COVID exit, December 2022 and January 2023 | About 1.87 million excess deaths over age 30 by one estimate, against an official 60,000 | About 130 in two months | The virus reaching an under-protected elderly population after an abrupt reopening |
| United States, 2020 and 2021, causes other than COVID | About 97,000 to 128,000 a year above prior trends | About 29 to 41 a year | Overdose, alcohol, heart disease, diabetes, homicide and disrupted care, with some COVID deaths misclassified |
| United States, 2020 and 2021, all causes | About one million excess deaths | About 150 a year | The virus above all |
| Sweden, 2020 to 2022, all causes | Cumulative excess of 158 per 100,000 | About 53 a year, against a European median near 117 | A light-touch strategy with heavy early losses in elder care |

\[14\] \[15\] \[16\] \[17\] \[18\] \[19\] \[21\] \[22\] \[23\] \[24\] \[25\] \[26\] \[27\]

**What the numbers show.** Per capita and per year, American deaths from causes other than COVID ran above the Cultural Revolution's decade average. The Cultural Revolution's killing concentrated in its worst years at far higher rates. Every figure in the American rows comes from American researchers working on published data.

**What the numbers do not show.** They do not show that policy caused all those deaths. The researchers count some misclassified COVID deaths among them. Overdose deaths were already climbing before 2020, and fear kept people from hospitals whatever the rules said. The share owed to the measures remains contested. The Cultural Revolution's deaths had a cause no one contests, a regime that ordered persecution.

**Per jurisdiction.** Stricter did not reliably mean fewer deaths. Sweden kept its schools and most businesses open and finished 2020 to 2022 at 37th of 42 European countries for excess mortality. It did far worse than its Nordic neighbors in 2020, and its own commission found the strategy had failed to protect the elderly. \[25\]

**What the comparison claims.** The free societies killed no one for dissent. Their measures still cost lives at a rate, per capita and per year, in the range of the Cultural Revolution's decade average. The cost fell hardest on the old, the sick, the poor and the young. Three differences stay decisive. The Party intended its persecution, and the free societies did not intend these deaths. Courts and voters reversed the free societies' measures, and nothing reversed the Party's. The free societies counted their dead in public, while the Party reported 60,000 deaths where one estimate finds 1.87 million.

**What the comparison does not claim.** It does not claim the Western measures were a Cultural Revolution. Some measures saved lives, and vaccines alone averted an estimated 14.4 million deaths worldwide in their first year. \[20\] Divorce rates in the United States fell in 2020 and 2021, a reminder that these same years carried effects the death table cannot show and that did not all run in one direction. \[28\] The Canadian judge who ruled against the government also found the protests an unacceptable breakdown of public order. The claim is narrower and harder. Every society under fear builds the machinery of mobbing and pays for its measures in lives. The difference between free and unfree societies lies in whether that machinery meets a brake and whether the dead get counted.

### 5. The Defenders' Baseline

No single enterprise defended the world. Its defense sat with a captured international referee, national health agencies, national stockpiles and state courts.

#### Business Architecture. Who Owned What

| Capability | Defender owner | Instrument |
|---|---|---|
| Outbreak verification | World Health Organization | Reports from member states and joint missions |
| Early warning | National health agencies, and Taiwan's among them | Alerts to the WHO |
| Protective equipment | National stockpiles and private supply chains | Stockpiles, purchasing and emergency production |
| Accountability | State attorneys general and federal courts | Lawsuits under the Foreign Sovereign Immunities Act |
| Rights during the emergency | Courts and legislatures | Judicial review and elections |

#### Data Architecture. What the Defenders Knew

The defenders knew what the Party let them know and what its own dissenters released. Taiwan's health authorities alerted the WHO on December 31, 2019 to atypical pneumonia patients under isolation in Wuhan. \[3\] The genome reached the world through one scientist's defiance. The raw data never reached it. \[2\] \[8\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines the Defenders Ran

The legal routine named the Party and could not collect.

| Date | Owner | Routine | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| April 21, 2020 | Missouri | Suit against nine defendants, the Party among them | Four claims filed |
| 2022 | District court | Dismissal under the Foreign Sovereign Immunities Act | All claims barred |
| January 2024 | Eighth Circuit | Appeal | The hoarding claim revived as commercial activity |
| January 2025 | District court | Evidentiary hearing | No defendant appeared |
| March 7, 2025 | District court | Default judgment of $24,488,825,457 | Damages tripled under antitrust law |
| December 2025 | Wuhan government and co-plaintiffs | Countersuit in the Wuhan Intermediate People's Court | Demands 356.4 billion yuan and public apologies |

The Party answered an American judgment the way it answered Micron in *Technology IP*, with a suit in its own court. The Wuhan filing invokes the Party's 2023 law on foreign state immunity, which permits retaliation in kind. \[9\]

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms the Defenders Used

The defenders' platforms were open, and the Party used them. Its spokesman carried the army-origin claim on an American platform that his own citizens could not legally reach. \[6\]

#### Principles and Governance. What Held the Defenders Together

The defenders kept their deepest principles, and their courts restored rights the emergencies had suspended. The brakes worked, and they worked late. Canada's account freezes lasted days, and the courts ruled on them two and four years later. \[17\]

### 6. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | Bought weeks by suppression and spent them buying equipment | Lost the weeks of warning it needed |
| **Entropy** | Low disorder at home and high disorder exported | Absorbed the exported disorder |
| **Topology** | One command over health, censorship and procurement | Separate owners for health, supply and law |
| **Asymmetry** | Sealed its own information space and used the open one | Kept its space open to the Party's claims |
| **Agency** | Worked through a referee it helped elect | Relied on a referee it did not control |
| **Resonance** | Suppression, procurement and diplomacy pointed one way | Health, supply and legal responses ran separately |
| **Phase** | Locked down a city in a day and cornered supply in weeks | Declared the emergency after the window closed |
| **Gradient** | Made supply dependence into leverage | Depended on the adversary's factories for masks |
| **Criticality** | Held the first data and the protective equipment | Needed both and held neither |
| **Coherence** | Governed by command, with no brake | Governed by law, with brakes that fired late |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. A Referee One Party Could Reach

*Architecture Governance*

**Documented.** The WHO relayed the Party's no-transmission line on January 14, 2020. Its director-general declared the emergency on January 30, after meeting the Party's leader in Beijing, and advised against travel restrictions. The Associated Press documented private frustration inside the WHO at how little data the Party shared. \[4\] \[5\] \[7\]

**Argued.** The world ran verification through one referee, and the Party had spent years cultivating that referee's leadership. No independent verification existed beside it.

#### Gap 2. The Brakes Fired Late

*Architecture Principles, Phase*

**Documented.** American courts restored worship rights in November 2020, eight months into the pandemic. Canada's courts ruled on its account freezes in 2024 and 2026. The Canadian judge faulted the absence of any objective standard for the freezes. Deaths from causes other than COVID ran at 29 to 41 per 100,000 a year in the United States during 2020 and 2021. \[17\] \[18\] \[21\]

**Argued.** Free societies hold the brake the Party lacks, and the brake is their decisive advantage. It fired after the harm. No emergency order had to weigh the measures' own death toll before officials renewed it. Emergency powers written in advance with objective standards, sunsets and a published count of their own costs would let the brake work before the damage instead of after it.

#### Gap 3. The Evidence Sat in the Adversary's Jurisdiction

*Data Architecture. The signature gap*

**Documented.** The genome reached the world through one scientist's defiance. The WHO joint mission did not receive the raw patient data, the original sequences or the laboratory records. \[2\] \[8\]

**Argued.** The world's pandemic response depended on data that sat under the Party's law. The defenders had no independent means of collecting it, so the Party's first decision became everyone's first decision.

#### Gap 4. The Masks Were Made on the Other Side

*Business Architecture, Economic*

**Documented.** The Party's entities bought up protective equipment in early 2020, and the Missouri court found the hoarding raised the state's costs by about $123 million. \[9\]

**Argued.** The defenders had moved the production of a critical capability into the adversary's jurisdiction. In the crisis the capability belonged to whoever held the factories.

#### Gap 5. The Court Can Name the Party but Not Collect

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** Sovereign immunity barred three of Missouri's four claims. The surviving claim produced a default judgment the defendants did not contest. Foreign states' property enjoys immunity from execution, and the Party answered with a countersuit worth twice the judgment. \[9\]

**Argued.** The judicial routine produced a documented record and a named defendant. It could not produce payment. The record is the value.

#### Gap 6. The Open Platforms Carried the Closed Side's Claims

*Technology Architecture*

**Documented.** A Party spokesman promoted the army-origin claim on an open American platform in March 2020, while the Party blocked that platform at home. \[6\]

**Argued.** The defenders' openness is a principle worth keeping. It also gave the Party a speaking platform it denied its own people. The asymmetry sits in the platform layer.

### 7. Target Architecture

The target keeps open science, open platforms and free courts. It gives the world verification the Party cannot capture and brakes that fire in time.

**Gap to target**

| Gap | Target element | Owner | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. A referee one party could reach | Allied verification that does not depend on one international body | Allied public-health and intelligence agencies | Duplicate effort, and friction with the WHO |
| 2. The brakes fired late | Emergency powers written in advance with objective standards, sunsets, fast judicial review and a published count of the measures' own costs at each renewal | Legislatures and courts | Less flexibility for officials in the first days |
| 3. The evidence sat in the adversary's jurisdiction | Independent genomic and wastewater surveillance shared among allies | Allied health agencies | Money, and privacy safeguards to build |
| 4. The masks were made on the other side | Stockpiles and allied production for critical protective equipment | Health departments and industry | Warehousing costs, and higher unit prices |
| 5. The court can name the Party but not collect | A documented record of the Party's commercial conduct, pursued where its commercial assets sit | State attorneys general and Congress | Countersuits under the Party's reciprocity law |
| 6. The open platforms carried the closed side's claims | Attribution and labels on state-run accounts, with no removal of lawful speech | Platforms, under published rules | Disputes over attribution |

**The brake is the advantage.** Nothing in the target asks a free society to act like the Party. The comparison shows where the free society won, and the target makes that win arrive sooner.

**Labels, not removals.** The pandemic years showed what speech policing costs a free society. The target labels the Party's accounts and removes nothing lawful.

**What the target would not have done.** It would not have stopped the virus or the Party's suppression. It would have given the world its own evidence, its own masks and its own brakes. That claim remains an argument.

### What COVID-19 Proves

COVID-19 proves Entropy. The Party held order at home by force and let the disorder escape into societies that had lost their warning.

It proves the instrument endures. The class label, the Cultural Revolution's category, Xinjiang's algorithm and the health code sort people the same way in four technological ages.

It proves the difference that matters most. Every society under fear builds the machinery of mobbing and pays for its measures in lives. The Party's machinery has no brake and hides its count. A free society counts its dead in public and holds a brake that fires late. Its task is to make the brake fire on time.

### Field Card

**What happened.** A doctor who warned his colleagues about a new illness was forced to sign a confession, then died of the disease he had tried to name. The Party suppressed the warning, cornered the world's protective equipment and captured the one international body meant to sound the alarm. At home the same government ran a health-code system that could turn a bank depositor's phone red to stop a protest, and a federal court later found the Party liable for billions in hoarding damages it never contested.

**The gap.** The evidence the world needed sat inside the adversary's jurisdiction, the signature gap at the Data layer, and no independent means existed to collect it.

**The fix.** Independent genomic and wastewater surveillance shared among allies would give the world its own evidence instead of depending on the evidence one government chooses to release.

**Where you could work on it.** Public health surveillance and biosecurity policy, international law focused on sovereign immunity and cross-border accountability, or emergency-powers legislation that builds in the sunsets and cost-reporting this chapter argues were missing.

*Sourcing notes and the full corrections record for this chapter appear in Appendix H.*

## Taiwan: The Republic of China

*The One Campaign That Runs Every Domain, Every Dimension, and Every Stratagem at Once*

**DOMAINS** Kinetic · Economic · Financial · Information · Psychological · Legal · Association · Cyber · Cultural · AI and Cognitive · Biological · Data and Surveillance

**DIMENSIONS** Time · Entropy · Topology · Asymmetry · Agency · Resonance · **Phase (signature)** · Gradient · Criticality · **Coherence (master)**

**STRATAGEMS** All thirty-six. Spine S1 · S3 · S4 · S19 · S22 · S23 · S33 · S35 · S36. Complete mapping in the Application Architecture.

**GAPS** Six, one at each layer. Signature gap at the Principles layer, where the Party already runs the architecture the defenders lack.

Between October 1 and October 4, 2021, the air force of the People's Liberation Army flew 149 sorties into the Republic of China's air-defense identification zone. The count set a record at the time. \[1\] October 1 is the founding anniversary of the People's Republic, and the Party chose the date. The flights crossed no legal threshold and drew no military response. The following week the planes flew again.

Since then the Party has fitted a named exercise to each political event it wished to answer. It launched Joint Sword-2024A three days after President Lai Ching-te took office in May 2024. Joint Sword-2024B came four days after his National Day speech that October. Strait Thunder-2025A followed his speech of March 13, 2025. Justice Mission-2025 began on December 29, twelve days after Washington notified Congress of an $11 billion arms package. \[6\] \[18\]

The defenders run a different clock. The first new F-16 Block 70 for Taiwan, sold in 2019, left the assembly line in March 2025 about two years late. Guided bombs notified to Congress in 2017 now look set to arrive in 2027 or 2028. \[18\]

The Party answers in days, and the defenders deliver in years. The record documents both clocks. Reading them as one architecture against another is the framework's argument.

Taiwan is the one place where all twelve domains, all ten dimensions and nearly the whole stratagem catalog run at once against one target. The campaign runs in the open, and its openness belongs to the design.

### The Terms of This Case

Taiwan is the Republic of China, a free and self-governing Chinese society of roughly twenty-three million people. It has its own elected government, military and currency. The People's Republic has never governed it for a single day. The Party calls its aim reunification, but no one can reunify what was never one polity under the regime now claiming it. What the Party proposes is annexation.

The aggressor is the Chinese Communist Party, never China and never the Chinese people. The Party has ruled the mainland since 1949 and holds its 1.4 billion people under its authority. The People's Liberation Army proves the distinction. Its soldiers swear loyalty to the Party, and the Party's own doctrine gives it absolute leadership over the armed forces. \[2\]

The ideology driving the campaign is not Chinese tradition. Marx and Engels wrote the doctrine in nineteenth-century Europe, and Lenin turned it into a method of total state control in Russia. The Party runs a local franchise of a European import. The campaign against Taiwan heals no ancient wound. A Leninist movement seeks to extinguish the most successful free Chinese society in history.

That explains why the Party cannot negotiate the campaign away. The Republic of China threatens the Party's legitimacy, not its territory. The Party claims the Chinese people need it for order and dignity. Taiwan refutes that claim every day it governs itself. The war is one of regime survival dressed as national restoration.

### 1. Architecture Vision

**Documented.** The Anti-Secession Law of 2005 authorizes non-peaceful means if Taiwan declares independence, if a major incident threatens unification, or if the Party judges the possibilities for peaceful unification exhausted. The Party alone decides when any trigger has fired. Its congress report of 2022 called complete reunification an essential requirement of national rejuvenation. \[4\]

**Argued.** The aim runs wider than conquest. The Party seeks absorption without a war it might lose, by making resistance look irrational and absorption inevitable. Every domain serves that one judgment in the minds of twenty-three million people.

### 2. The Party's Baseline

#### Business Architecture. The Capabilities It Fielded

All twelve domains carry weight in this case, and every one points at the same judgment.

**Kinetic.** The Party fights below the threshold that would justify a kinetic answer. The PLA flew more than a thousand incursions into Taiwan's identification zone in 2023 alone. \[5\] Named exercises now follow named political events within days. The Party's coast guard joined them for the first time in May 2024. \[6\]

**Economic.** The Economic Cooperation Framework Agreement of 2010 bound the two economies together. \[3\] The Party used it as a lever in the open. It suspended tariff concessions on 12 products three weeks before Taiwan's January 2024 election, and on 134 more eleven days after President Lai took office. \[7\]

**Financial.** Taiwanese firms with mainland operations depend on approvals and capital the Party controls. The Party can raise the cost of capital for anyone whose politics it wishes to discipline, and it needs no headline to do it.

**Information.** The information operation aims less at winning affection than at teaching futility. Aligned media and coordinated networks carry one message into Taiwan's own press, that absorption is coming and only its manner remains open.

**Psychological.** Three frequencies run together. Defeatism says Taiwan cannot win and America will not come. Identity pressure recasts self-government as cultural betrayal. Cost amplification turns every exercise into a preview of the price.

**Legal.** The Anti-Secession Law is the Party's self-issued license, and its triggers are elastic by design. \[4\] The One China framing the Party presses on governments and companies worldwide carries the same instrument abroad. The Party also recasts United Nations Resolution 2758 as authority for a claim the resolution never makes. \[8\]

**Association.** The United Front Work Department cultivates business leaders and local officials inside Taiwan, along with temple associations and media figures. Accommodation then arrives in Taiwanese voices. In June 2025 Taiwan's prosecutors charged four former staff members of the governing party with spying for the mainland, including former aides to the president's office and a former foreign minister. A district court convicted and sentenced all four that September, with terms from four to ten years. On appeal in June 2026 the High Court reduced three of the sentences and acquitted the fourth outright. \[9\]

**Cyber.** Taiwan's government networks absorb continuous intrusion and pre-positioning. The logic matches the Volt Typhoon operation documented against American infrastructure. \[10\]

**Cultural.** Subsidized tourism, temple ties, youth exchanges and entertainment access deepen a shared identity the Party can then convert into a political claim.

**AI and Cognitive.** Around the January 2024 presidential election, Taiwan faced a documented surge of deepfaked audio and video, fabricated documents and amplified narratives. \[11\]

**Biological.** The Party uses sanitary pretexts as trade weapons. It banned Taiwanese pineapples in March 2021 and grouper in June 2022, each at a moment of political friction. \[12\]

**Data and Surveillance.** Platform data, intrusion and the Party's wider collection assemble a picture of Taiwan's leaders. The picture covers their networks, their finances and their vulnerabilities.

#### Data Architecture. What It Knew

The data layer is again the least documented part of the Party's baseline. The precision of the timing shows it at work. Each exercise names its trigger, each tariff suspension lands on a sector and a date, and each agricultural ban falls on a constituency the Party wants to teach.

That precision argues for a working model of Taiwan's politics, this case's Digital Twin. No public document describes it.

#### Application Architecture. The Routines It Ran

**Stratagem 4, Wait at Ease for the Weary Enemy**, sets the patience. The Party lets Taipei and Washington tire across electoral cycles.

**Stratagem 19, Remove the Firewood from Under the Pot**, strips the foundations one ally at a time. Taiwan's formal allies fell from about thirty in the 1970s to twelve. \[13\]

**Stratagem 22, Shut the Door to Catch the Thief**, rehearses the blockade. Every named exercise since 2024 has practiced encirclement. \[6\]

**Stratagem 23, Befriend a Distant State While Attacking a Neighbor**, courts distant powers so the near target stands alone.

**Stratagem 3, Kill with a Borrowed Knife**, lets Taiwan's own mainland-dependent businesses and cultivated media carry the argument.

**Stratagem 1, Deceive the Heavens to Cross the Sea**, turns the extraordinary into routine. Daily incursions hide real preparation inside the familiar.

**Stratagem 33, Sow Discord Among the Enemy**, amplifies Taiwan's divisions and the friction between Taipei and Washington.

**Stratagem 35, Chain the Stratagems**, links the rest. An election brings a tariff suspension, an inauguration brings an exercise, and each move feeds the message of inevitability.

Stratagem 36 is **If All Else Fails, Retreat.** Each exercise ends short of a red line, which preserves the option for the next cycle.

The record shows each routine. Reading them as one chain is the framework's argument.

#### The Complete Mapping

Taiwan runs nearly the whole catalog, so the full mapping appears here and nowhere else. Group placement follows *The Code*. The group an actor reaches for shows how it rates its own position.

**Group I. Superiority.** *Deployed from strength, to win cheaply.*

| 2   | Besiege Wei to Rescue Zhao            | Pressure applied to Taiwan’s trading partners and diplomatic allies rather than to the island directly        |

Groups I and II dominate the table. The Party operates from a position it believes already favors it. It reaches for misdirection and patience rather than desperation, and it holds the Group VI entries in reserve. Any analyst who learns the matrix can read that position in advance.

#### A Target Audience Note

The Taiwanese electorate of January 2024 fits this form, and the case carries this book's fullest illustration of the discipline because every other case builds toward it.

**Documented.** Around the January 2024 presidential election, Taiwan faced a documented surge of deepfaked audio and video, fabricated documents and amplified narratives. \[11\] The Party timed a tariff suspension on twelve products to land twenty-three days before the vote. \[7\]

**Argued.** A target audience analysis would read the whole electorate as the audience, with three sub-audiences rated differently. Younger and economically anxious voters carry the highest susceptibility to the message that resistance costs more than accommodation. Voters with mainland business ties carry the highest accessibility, reached directly through the same commercial channels that carry ordinary trade. Voters already skeptical of Washington's commitment carry the deepest vulnerability. The line of persuasion needs no fabrication to land there, only the ambiguity America has chosen to keep. The Party's economic, information and psychological domains are not separate lines of effort against this audience. They are one target audience analysis, executed through three channels that reach the same voter from three directions in the weeks before he decides.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms It Ran On

The Party ran the campaign on platforms it commands outright. The PLA and the coast guard answer to the Central Military Commission, which the Party controls. The Taiwan Affairs Office, the customs service and the tariff commission set the economic terms. The United Front Work Department sits inside the Party itself.

#### Principles and Governance. How It Held Together

**Time.** The Party measures the contest in generations, and it stripped Taiwan's allies across fifty years. Taipei and Washington live it as two- and four-year windows.

**Phase.** The Party answers each trigger with a package built in advance. Joint Sword-2024A came three days after the inauguration, and the second tariff suspension eight days after that.

**Coherence.** The PLA swears loyalty to the Party, and one authority directs the exercises, the tariffs and the United Front. The record documents each channel. The single design behind them is the framework's argument.

Coherence also explains the hostility itself. The Party manufactures it from the top and polices the name of the Republic of China on the mainland. When a state must police a name, the enmity belongs to the state and not to the nation. The 1.4 billion people of the mainland are the campaign's subjects, as the people of Taiwan are its targets.

### 3. The Defenders' Baseline

No single enterprise defends Taiwan. Its defense sits with owners in Taipei and Washington and with the firms between them.

#### Business Architecture. Who Owned What

**Capabilities against owners**

| Capability | Defender owner | Instrument |
|---|---|---|
| Deterrence | Taiwan's defense ministry, with American arms | Forces, and arms bought under the Taiwan Relations Act |
| Arms delivery | State Department, Defense Security Cooperation Agency, industry, and Taiwan's legislature | Approval, notification, production and budget |
| Economic resilience | Taiwan's government | Support for targeted sectors and new markets |
| Military-company designation | Department of Defense | The 1260H list |
| Capital-market exposure | SEC and Treasury | Disclosure and sanctions |
| Undersea infrastructure | Taiwan's coast guard and prosecutors, and the cable owners | Interception, prosecution and repair |
| Leading-edge chip capacity | TSMC and the Commerce Department | Fabs abroad and subsidies |

Laid over the capability map, the owners divide a campaign the Party runs as one.

**The American commitment.** The Taiwan Relations Act of 1979 commits the United States to provide arms of a defensive character. It leaves open whether the United States would fight, and successive administrations have kept that question open by choice. \[14\]

**Taiwan's own government.** Taipei owns its forces, its coast guard and its courts. Its legislature had not passed the government's special defense budget as of January 2026. \[15\]

#### Data Architecture. What the Defenders Knew

The defenders see a great deal. Taiwan's defense ministry publishes daily counts of the Party's aircraft and ships. \[5\] Its reports to the legislature track every arms case, and independent monitors track them too. \[15\]

The records do not always travel. The Department of Defense added the lidar maker Hesai to its list of Chinese military companies on January 31, 2024. Hesai had listed on Nasdaq in February 2023, a year earlier. A federal court upheld the designation in July 2025. The list's built-in consequence bars Defense contracts from 2026, and Hesai remained listed on Nasdaq in 2026. \[16\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines the Defenders Ran

The Party's routine is one trigger and one package.

**Trigger and answer**

| Trigger | Date | The Party's answer | Days |
|---|---|---|---|
| Taiwan's presidential election | January 13, 2024 | Tariff concessions suspended on 12 products | 23 before |
| President Lai's inauguration | May 20, 2024 | Joint Sword-2024A | 3 |
| The same inauguration | May 20, 2024 | Tariff concessions suspended on 134 products | 11 |
| Lai's National Day speech | October 10, 2024 | Joint Sword-2024B | 4 |
| Lai's speech on infiltration | March 13, 2025 | Strait Thunder-2025A | 19 |
| American arms notification | December 17, 2025 | Justice Mission-2025 | 12 |

The defenders' main routine runs through a production line.

**The defenders' deliveries**

| Case | Notified | Status |
|---|---|---|
| AGM-154C guided bombs | 2017 | Delivery expected in 2027 or 2028 |
| 66 F-16 Block 70/72 fighters | 2019 | First aircraft off the line in March 2025, about two years late |
| All approved and undelivered sales | Through December 2025 | About $32 billion outstanding in January 2026 |

Taiwan's courts moved fast where they could reach. The Hong Tai 58 severed the cable between Taiwan and Penghu on February 25, 2025. A court convicted its captain 107 days later. \[20\]

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms the Defenders Used

The defenders' largest platform is also their largest exposure. TSMC makes the overwhelming majority of the world's most advanced logic chips, and it makes them on one island. Its first Arizona plant began volume production in late 2024. The island still holds most of the leading edge. \[17\]

Taiwan also runs on undersea cables that a single dragged anchor can cut.

#### Principles and Governance. What Held the Defenders Together

The defenders keep their principles. Taiwan's courts demand proof, its legislature debates its budget, and Washington keeps its commitment ambiguous by design. Ambiguity at the top of the ladder aims to deter a war. It leaves the bottom rungs empty. No venue owns the whole defense of Taiwan.

### 4. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | A generational horizon, with allies stripped across fifty years | Two- and four-year windows, and arms delivered across a decade |
| **Entropy** | One authority imposes friction cheaply | Every sortie met costs pilots, airframes and budget |
| **Topology** | A star with one command point | A mesh of two governments, a legislature and industry |
| **Asymmetry** | Chooses the time, the place and the threshold | Must answer in force what it cannot answer in law |
| **Agency** | Cultivated voices inside Taiwan carry its case | Open institutions cannot refuse a lawful voice |
| **Resonance** | Exercises, tariffs and narrative point one way | Defense designates Hesai and Nasdaq still lists it |
| **Phase** | A named package within days of each trigger | Arms deliveries measured in years |
| **Gradient** | Trade dependence tilts business toward accommodation | Taiwan absorbs each coercive ban alone |
| **Criticality** | Aims at the island that holds the leading edge | Leading-edge capacity still concentrated on that island |
| **Coherence** | Governed by command | No owner of the whole |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. No Owner of the Clock

*Architecture Governance*

**Documented.** The State Department approves arms sales and Congress receives the notifications. The Defense Security Cooperation Agency and industry execute them, and Taiwan's legislature funds them. The backlog stood at about $32 billion in January 2026. Guided bombs notified in 2017 slipped to 2027 or 2028, and the first new F-16 ran about two years late. \[15\] \[18\]

**Argued.** Each owner behaved correctly inside its own remit. Nobody owned the time between a notification and a delivery. The Party's exercise has one owner and a clock measured in days. The defenders' answer has five owners and a clock measured in years.

#### Gap 2. The Party Already Runs the Target Architecture

*Architecture Principles, Phase. The signature gap*

**Documented.** The trigger table records six answers timed to six events. The Party's statements or its state media named the political trigger for each exercise. For the tariff suspensions it cited Taiwan's trade restrictions, and the timing is the record's own. Every answer landed within twenty-three days of its trigger, and one landed before the election it aimed at. \[6\] \[7\]

**Argued.** A named trigger, a package built in advance and a published justification make up the architecture the Hong Kong case asks the defenders to build. The Party runs it already, against Taiwan, in public. No defender instrument in the public record fires on any of these triggers. The defenders need not share the Party's aims to copy its timing.

#### Gap 3. A List Only One Owner Reads

*Data Architecture*

**Documented.** Defense designated Hesai a Chinese military company in January 2024, a year after its Nasdaq listing. A federal court upheld the designation in July 2025. The designation's statutory consequence reaches Defense contracting. Three senators asked the SEC and Treasury in September 2025 to delist the company, and it remained listed in 2026. \[16\]

**Argued.** The designation lives in one owner's records and binds one owner's contracts. Capital markets never read it. The defect sits in the schema, which has one reader where the campaign needs four.

#### Gap 4. The Routine Reaches the Captain

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** Taiwan's court convicted the captain of the Hong Tai 58 and sentenced him to three years. An appeal failed, and a later ruling awarded the cable's owner about $570,000 in damages. Prosecutors cited the ship's changing names and flags. They charged the captain alone and sent the rest of the crew home. The two cables to the Matsu Islands cut in February 2023 still have no formal attribution. \[19\] \[20\]

**Argued.** Criminal law reaches the person on the deck. It stops at the owner, the registry and whoever sent the ship. The Party builds its gray-zone acts to land in that space.

#### Gap 5. Taiwan Absorbs Coercion Alone

*Business Architecture, Economic*
