Three examples show the range. A foreign area officer. A case officer at a listing agency. A Hill staffer who understands both a statute and the gap it is meant to close. None of these jobs pay what a comparable skill set earns in the private sector, and every one of them sits closer to the actual seam this book has spent its length describing than most private-sector work ever will.

### Open-Source Intelligence

Several of this book's own footnotes cite work done by outside researchers and small nonprofits, not by any government office. *Protocols and Ports*, *Technology IP* and *The Pen From the Inside* all depend on a detection routine that runs today on volunteers rather than on any funded office. The Global Engagement Center's lapse and the absence of any statutory successor is a documented gap, not a hypothetical one.

This is a field a determined person can enter without waiting for an institution to build a pipeline for it. The tools are largely public. The skill is patience with public records, satellite imagery and court filings, plus the judgment to know what a pattern shows and what it does not.

### Supply-Chain Analysis

*Protocols and Ports* and *Financial Control* both trace a single thread. A component, a listing, an index fund runs through more hands than any one of them tracks. Tracing where a critical part is really made takes one skill. Following a listed company's ownership back through a shell structure takes a related one. Checking what an index fund holds, constituent by constituent, takes a third. All three sit in genuine and growing demand, in government and well outside it.

### What This Chapter Does Not Ask

It does not ask a reader to choose one of these paths over an ordinary life, a family, or a career that has nothing to do with any gap this book has named. Most readers will do something else entirely, and that is not a failure of the argument. It does not ask any reader to treat a colleague, a classmate or a neighbor of Chinese descent as a subject of suspicion. Every chapter that touches ancestry has said the opposite, and this one does not reopen that question to make a recruiting point land harder.

What it asks is narrower. If a reader was already weighing one of these fields against another, this book has tried to say plainly what the work involves and why it matters. The choice, if it comes, gets made with the facts rather than without them.

The architecture that produced the gaps in this book took a century to build, one defensible decision at a time, by people who were mostly trying to do their jobs correctly. It will not close in one career, or in one generation's worth of careers. It closes the same way it opened, one decision at a time, made by someone who happened to be the person standing at that particular gate when the decision came due.

That person could be the reader of this page. It could just as easily be someone else. The book's job ends here. What comes next belongs to whoever picks it up.

# Appendices

## Appendix A. The Stack in Full

*The Reference Architecture*

Enterprise architecture is the discipline that makes a large organization act as one. It lines up the strategy, the functions, the data and the technology so every part pulls toward a single aim.

That makes it the engineering of coherence, and Coherence is the dimension this book keeps returning to, the one the Party holds and the West lacks. Set the framework down as an architecture and it becomes something a person can build rather than only argue for. The Stack in the front matter introduced this briefly. This appendix gives it in full.

Total Domain War is an enterprise, not by metaphor but by structure. It pursues one aim across many functions, on a shared base of technology and data. The Party runs it as a single coherent system. The West runs the same functions as a scatter of projects owned by rival institutions that answer to none of the others.

The gap between them is not a gap in money or talent. Architecture gaps close with architecture, and this appendix specifies the one this book proposes.

### The Unifying Stack

The three catalogs at the center of this book are not three subjects. They are three views of one architecture, mapped onto the layers an architect already works in.

The ten **Dimensions** are the Architecture Principles, the physics every design must obey. The twelve **Domains** are the Business Architecture, the functions the enterprise performs. The thirty-six **Stratagems** are the Application Architecture, the patterns those functions run on. Full detail on the Stratagems, including the complete Taiwan mapping, lives in Appendix B. Full detail on the Dimensions and Domains lives in Appendix C.

Beneath these sit the technology layers this book calls the Digital Twin and the orchestration layer, treated at length in that case chapter. The Digital Twin is the Data Architecture, the working model of a target assembled from the harvest. Agentic AI is the orchestration layer, running the technique catalog across domains faster than a human staff can answer. Compute, network and cyber infrastructure sit beneath both.

Above every layer sits governance, which is Coherence doing the architect's work, holding every layer to one aim. *Coherence Without Command* argues that governance at the scale this contest requires must run on architecture contracts and an architecture board rather than on command, and this appendix does not repeat that argument. It only notes where governance sits in the stack.

Read the stack top to bottom and it forms a sentence. Principles govern functions. Functions run on techniques. Techniques run on data through orchestration. Governance holds the whole in line.

The Party's enterprise reads straight down that sentence. The West's breaks at the last line. It holds the functions, most of the technology and the finest individual talent in the world. It fields no architect with authority over the whole, so its layers do not line up. The failure the architecture reveals is not a missing capability. A missing governance layer, precisely specified, is the actual defect.

### The Ontology

A separate enterprise-architecture framework, the Zachman Framework, tests whether a model is complete by asking six questions of it. What. How. Where. Who. When. Why. \[1\] This book's framework answers each one without strain, which is evidence the model is complete rather than an arbitrary list.

**What** is the data, the Digital Twin. **How** is the Stratagem catalog. **Where** is Topology, the shape power flows through. **Who** is Agency and Association, the people and the organizations. **When** is Time and Phase, the horizon and the beat. **Why** is Coherence, binding every part to one aim.

Two of those six answers are whole Dimensions rather than borrowed concepts, Topology and Time. An architecture built for a contest fought across geography and across decades puts the where and the when in the foundation rather than in the trim.

### The Capability Maturity Model

Engineering grades not only what an organization does but how maturely it does it. The Capability Maturity Model Integration, a standard developed at Carnegie Mellon, runs five levels. Initial. Managed. Defined. Quantitatively Managed. Optimizing. \[2\] Laid against Total Domain War, the same five levels grade an actor's ability to wage or withstand the contest. The abstract Coherence gap becomes something an engineer can track rather than only argue.

The grade is uncomfortable to state plainly. In single technical domains, cyber and finance and advanced computing alike, the West runs at or near the top of the scale. Per-domain skill was never the problem this book documents.

In cross-domain integration, the capability that decides outcomes, the West runs near the bottom. It fields no architect to define the higher-order processes. Assessments get produced but are not comparable across the agencies that produce them. Responses get executed but are not sequenced against each other. Nothing is repeatable across agencies, because nothing is specified across agencies. The Party runs near the top of the integration scale, not because its domains are individually stronger but because they answer to one enterprise.

The maturity gap is the Coherence gap, made legible on a scale an engineer already knows how to read. That low integration cancels a capability lead rather than merely discounting it is this book's model, argued throughout its case studies, not a measurement this appendix reports as settled.

### The Technique Matrix, in Brief

Cyber defense changed in practice once it stopped logging incidents one at a time and started mapping adversary behavior to a shared matrix of tactics and techniques, the MITRE ATT&CK framework. \[3\] A common vocabulary let one organization's observation become another's warning.

The thirty-six Stratagems are that kind of artifact, roughly two thousand years older. They sort into six groups keyed to the actor's position. Advantage when ahead. Confrontation in a balanced fight. Offense against a weaker foe. Exploiting chaos among several parties. Combination to gain ground. Retreat when behind. Appendix B lays out all thirty-six in that order, with the full mapping against the Taiwan case.

Set out as a matrix rather than read as a classical curiosity, the thirty-six become an operational map instead. Every case in this book's third and fourth parts is a path through that matrix, a sequence of techniques across domains, the same way an intrusion is a path through the cyber matrix. A reader who knows the matrix can name the technique in play in a live case and anticipate the next one, because the library is finite and the six groups run in a known sequence.

### The Military Viewpoints

Defense staffs already own architecture frameworks built to model an enterprise of exactly this kind, through a set of standard viewpoints, including the Department of Defense Architecture Framework. \[4\] This book's framework fills them without strain.

The **capability view** takes the Dimensions and the maturity grade above. The **operational view** takes the twelve Domains. The **systems view** takes the AI, data, network and cyber layers. The **data view** takes the Digital Twin.

This is the plain answer to whether military planners could use this framework starting tomorrow. They could, because the tools, the viewpoints and the staffs trained on them are already theirs. The obstacle this book documents throughout is not capability. Authority is the obstacle, the subject *Coherence Without Command* takes up directly.

### The Defensive Architecture

The architecture organizes the defense as well as the threat it describes. NIST's Cybersecurity Framework, updated to version 2.0 in February 2024, now runs six functions instead of the five its first version specified. The sixth is Governance, added because practitioners found the same gap this book finds. \[5\] The counters scattered through this book's case studies assemble into that same six-function shape, and each is discharged by a dimensional move already named in this appendix.

**Identify.** Know what you hold and where it is exposed. This is Criticality applied inward, the chokepoint audit that finds the rare-earth dependency, the single-source input and the utility whose failure cascades. The counters in the Economic and Financial cases are largely Identify functions.

**Protect.** Reduce the exposure the audit finds. Second sources and stockpiles, ownership requirements and research-security standards, design rules that protect minors. This is Gradient run in reverse, flattening a slope the adversary would otherwise ride down.

**Detect.** See the operation while it runs. This is a Phase and Topology problem, and it is where the West is weakest. Detection across domains requires that a securities filing, a patent volume, a recruitment offer and a network intrusion be describable in one schema. At present they are not.

**Respond.** Act inside the adversary's decision cycle. This is Phase again, and it requires the pre-authorization argued for across several cases, because a response designed during a crisis arrives after the crisis has already been decided.

**Recover.** Restore function and, harder, restore trust. This is Coherence, and the domestic cases show why recovery is the least tractable of the six. A department can remove thousands of people by order and cannot bring their trust back by the same order, because trust is not a switch any authority holds.

**Govern.** Set the strategy, the roles and the risk tolerance the other five run inside. NIST added this function because an organization can run the other five well at the working level and still fail without one layer above all five holding them to a single aim. This is Coherence again, named for a second time because the same gap shows up at every scale this book examines, from a single firm's cybersecurity program to a free society's whole contest with the Party.

Assembled, the six are a defensive architecture in their own right, and they show the same truth from the protective side that the offense showed from the other. Every function but the narrowly technical depends on a coordinating authority the West does not currently field.

### The Principles Table

TOGAF documents architecture principles in a fixed form. A name, a statement, a rationale and the implications that follow. The ten Dimensions have been documented informally throughout this book. They receive that formal treatment here for the first time, in one place.

#### Time

**Statement.** The actor holding the longer strategic horizon accumulates a decisive advantage against an actor constrained to shorter political or fiscal cycles.

**Rationale.** *We Built This* documents an Enclosure accumulated over roughly a century, one defensible decision at a time, against a Party outlook stated in terms of a 2049 aim. A century-long process and a four-year electoral cycle are not different speeds on the same clock. They are different clocks.

**Implications.** Institutions that reset their priorities with each electoral or budget cycle cede this Dimension by default, regardless of any single cycle's resourcing. A defense that only ever plans to the next cycle has already lost the Dimension before the contest inside that cycle begins.

#### Entropy

**Statement.** An actor that exports disorder while preserving order at home gains at the rate the disorder it exports spreads.

**Rationale.** *Accommodate* documents a domestic feed governed by a registry and a regulator, set against covert networks abroad that amplify division on several sides of a question at once. The order and the disorder are the same design, aimed in two directions.

**Implications.** Entropy must be measured bidirectionally. The question is never only whether disorder exists in a society, since some disorder is normal in any free one. The question is who controls its direction and its rate, and whether the same actor that benefits from disorder abroad tolerates none of it at home.

#### Topology

**Statement.** A centralized structure concentrates the coordination that a distributed structure disperses, and the two cannot be judged against the same standard of success.

**Rationale.** The Party's enterprise runs as a hub, one center connecting every agency and enterprise this book documents. The West's institutions run as a mesh, independent nodes answering to no shared center. *Coherence Without Command* argues that the mesh's resilience is inseparable from its coordination cost.

**Implications.** A fix imported from a hub-shaped system, a single command authority, will not survive contact with a mesh. It should not be attempted. The correct fix works with the mesh's own Topology, an architecture contract that coordinates without commanding, rather than against it.

#### Asymmetry

**Statement.** An actor unable to win a contest at one altitude will move the contest to an altitude where the balance favors it instead.

**Rationale.** *Total Domain War, Not Asymmetric War* documents the literature on how weaker actors defeat stronger ones. American supremacy is total in the kinetic domain. This book's cases show the same actor moving the contest instead into financial, legal and informational domains, where that supremacy does not reach as cleanly.

**Implications.** Overwhelming strength in one domain does not deter action in another, and a defender who aggregates strength across all domains into one confidence score will miss exactly the domain an adversary has chosen to fight in. Assessment has to run domain by domain.

#### Agency

**Statement.** The contest's actual target is a population's or an individual's capacity to decide, not that population's belief in any single fact.

**Rationale.** *Accommodate* documents target audience analysis aimed at shaping a decision rather than correcting or replacing a specific belief. A population that believes every fact correctly but cannot act coherently on those facts has still lost the Dimension.

**Implications.** A defense built only to correct false claims, a fact-check or a correction notice, treats the symptom and misses the target. Resilience has to address the capacity to decide, which is a different capacity than the capacity to know.

#### Resonance

**Statement.** An intervention that amplifies an existing, organic division compounds faster and lasts longer than one that manufactures a division from nothing.

**Rationale.** *Accommodate* argues that the platform and the practitioner both find division rather than create it, and that the *COVID-19* years deepened a distrust the Party did not originate.

**Implications.** A society's own baseline of cohesion is a defense-relevant variable in its own right, not a separate question from national security. The seed this Dimension describes dies in soil that was never prepared for it.

#### Phase

**Statement.** The actor that completes its decision cycle faster inside a fixed window captures the advantage regardless of which actor holds the greater resources.

**Rationale.** *Protocols and Ports* documents a Party veto inserted twenty-two days after an American order, against a divestiture that took five and a half years. *Military-Civil Fusion* and *Taiwan* both show the same gap between an adversary's weeks and a defender's years.

**Implications.** An approval chain built to prevent error becomes, unmodified, a vulnerability once an adversary's cycle time is measured in weeks. Pre-authorization, deciding the response before the crisis rather than during it, is the only countermeasure this book documents that does not simply abandon deliberation.

#### Gradient

**Statement.** Power flows toward the actor holding the steepest incentive slope, and the flow requires no coercion once the slope exists.

**Rationale.** *Financial Control* and *Attract* both document capture that required no persuasion of any individual. An ordinary professional incentive did the whole job, already pointed in one direction by a fee, a bonus or an index inclusion.

**Implications.** A defense built only against explicit coercion, a threat or a bribe, misses the majority of the cases this book documents. These cases ran entirely on incentive rather than on any threat. The fix has to change the slope itself, through disclosure or a screen at the point of decision, not merely police the threats that never had to be made.

#### Criticality

**Statement.** A chokepoint's value is set by what depends on it, not by its own size, cost or visibility.

**Rationale.** *Protocols and Ports* and *Taiwan* both document small, unglamorous chokepoints. A crane, a coding standard, a fabrication process each carry leverage entirely disproportionate to their footprint.

**Implications.** An inventory of a society's own dependencies matters at least as much as an inventory of an adversary's capabilities, and ordinarily gets far less attention, because a dependency is boring in a way a capability is not.

#### Coherence

**Statement.** A system that aligns every domain under one aim multiplies its component advantages. A system that runs each domain separately cancels those same advantages before an adversary has to intervene.

**Rationale.** This is the master Dimension, and *Coherence Without Command* is this book's fullest argument for it. Every case in this book documents a domain where the West holds individual strength that its own lack of coordination cancels before the contest is even joined.

**Implications.** Coordination itself, not any single domain's resourcing, is the primary lever available to the West. The fix is architectural, an architecture contract and an architecture board. Nothing additive works instead, since no quantity of additional per-domain resourcing substitutes for the governance layer this book finds missing throughout.

### Where Governance Sits

Every architecture needs an architect, a function with authority across the whole enterprise to set the principles, hold the standards and keep the layers aligned. Without that authority, an architecture is a document rather than a system.

This book's own Topology predicts the difficulty precisely. The West is, by its structure, a mesh of independent nodes. Separated powers. Rival services. Autonomous agencies. Sovereign allies. None holds authority over the rest. The structure that keeps a free society free is the same structure that denies it a commanding architect, and *Coherence Without Command* argues at length that this is not a flaw to be engineered away but the design constraint any workable answer has to respect.

This appendix does not repeat that argument. It records only where governance sits in the stack. Above every layer, exercised through an architecture board with authority to convene and to publish, and no authority to command.

### Sourcing Status: Appendix A

**\[1\]** John Zachman published the framework bearing his name in an IBM Systems Journal article in 1987, organized around six interrogatives applied across multiple perspectives.

**\[2\]** The Capability Maturity Model Integration is maintained by the CMMI Institute, a subsidiary of ISACA, building on a model originally developed at Carnegie Mellon University's Software Engineering Institute.

**\[3\]** The MITRE ATT&CK framework is maintained by the MITRE Corporation and is in wide use across the cybersecurity industry as a shared taxonomy of adversary tactics and techniques.

**\[4\]** The Department of Defense Architecture Framework models a defense enterprise through capability and operational viewpoints, systems and data viewpoints, and several others.

**\[5\]** *New.* The National Institute of Standards and Technology published Cybersecurity Framework 2.0 on February 26, 2024. It added Govern as a sixth core function. The other five stayed as they were. Identify. Protect. Detect. Respond. Recover. The first edition of this book, and the source material behind this appendix, predates that update and cited only five functions. This appendix corrects the count and treats the addition of Govern as independent confirmation of this book's own argument, made without reference to it.

**Corrections from the first edition.** The maturity-model level names are corrected to CMMI's current terminology. Initial. Managed. Defined. Quantitatively Managed. Optimizing. These replace the first edition's use of the older Capability Maturity Model's terms. The defensive-architecture section is expanded from five functions to six to match NIST's 2024 update. The count of intelligence organizations, where it recurs in this appendix's source material, follows the correction made throughout this book. Eighteen replaces seventeen.

**Stated as inference, not finding.** The claim that low cross-domain integration cancels a capability lead, rather than merely discounting it, is this book's model. This book's case studies examine that model throughout. No cited source reports it as a measurement.

## Appendix B. The Thirty-Six Stratagems in Full

*With the Complete Taiwan Mapping*

For two thousand years, Chinese statecraft has carried a codified set of thirty-six stratagems. \[1\] Western analysts often file them under philosophy or folklore. That filing is a category error. The stratagems are an engineered taxonomy of leverage, thirty-six tested methods for reaching a strategic objective through deception, indirect force and the exploitation of an opponent's own structure.

Through most of history, human friction capped their reach. Running one deception across the economic, informational and diplomatic domains at once took a bureaucracy that never tired and a network that never leaked. People tire. Networks leak. The stratagems worked, but the human mind set their speed limit. Artificial intelligence erases that limit. Fed into a system that processes a planet's data in real time, the thirty-six stop being history and become the operating software this book calls Total Domain War, running across all twelve domains at once.

Six of the thirty-six get a full profile below, one from each group, because they carry the most weight across this book's cases. The rest get a line apiece. Each entry carries its classical principle, a documented modern application and a Western wartime parallel. The point of this appendix is not that the stratagems are Chinese. These entries name the grammar of strategy, and the West has spoken that grammar fluently whenever it had to.

### The Architecture of Leverage: Six Groups

The thirty-six are not a flat list. They divide into six groups of six, and the division is not decorative. Each group fits a different position on the board. Reading which group an actor reaches for reads how that actor rates its own strength.

Groups I and II belong to the actor already holding the advantage. The work is Superiority and Confrontation, and the goal is to win cheaply, by misdirection rather than force. Groups III and IV belong to the contest itself. The work is Attack and Confusion, degrading an opponent's capability and fouling its decision loops before any decisive clash. Groups V and VI belong to the actor operating inside a stronger host, or under real pressure. The work is Control and Desperation, capturing a host's institutions from within, or retreating in good order to rebuild for the next round.

A Group I move says an actor believes it already holds the high ground. A Group VI move, including the instruction to retreat, says the actor is buying time. Naming the group names the phase of the campaign.

### Group I. Stratagems of Superiority

Deploy from strength. Win without unnecessary cost by weaponizing the advantage through misdirection rather than direct force.

**Stratagem 4. Wait at Ease for the Weary Enemy.** This is the group's anchor and the Time dimension made operational. The classical text holds the high ground, keeps fresh water and lets the enemy climb. The Party holds a centennial horizon, planning in decades and building in pipelines, while the American system plans in two-year electoral cycles and annual budgets. The Party does not need to defeat that system in the field. It waits while the system spends itself on cycles that reverse every few years. Admiral Nimitz ran the Western version of the same patience across the Pacific from 1943 onward, bypassing the strongest Japanese garrisons at Rabaul and Truk, cutting their supply and leaving tens of thousands of defenders to wait on islands the war had already passed by. \[2\]

The rest of the group. **1. Deceive the Heavens to Cross the Sea.** Hides the decisive act inside routine until repetition makes the observer stop looking. **2. Besiege Wei to Rescue Zhao.** Threatens what the enemy cannot afford to lose rather than striking its strength, the logic behind the Doolittle Raid of April 1942. \[3\] **3. Kill with a Borrowed Knife.** Lets a proxy deliver the strike and absorb the blame. **5. Loot a Burning House.** Advances while the adversary is consumed by its own crisis, the same logic behind the Destroyers-for-Bases deal of September 1940. \[4\] **6. Make a Sound in the East, Strike in the West.** Manufactures a loud threat in one place to strip the guard from another.

### Group II. Stratagems of Confrontation

Outmaneuver a stronger adversary through misdirection, manufactured reality and the exploitation of the adversary's own defensive logic.

**Stratagem 8. Openly Repair the Gallery Roads, March Secretly Through Chencang.** This is the anchor. Han Xin let the enemy watch his soldiers rebuild the mountain roads while his real force slipped through a pass hundreds of miles off. Two tracks run at once, one built to look real and one built to win. *Military-Civil Fusion* is the modern version, a visible commercial firm paired with a hidden legal duty that hands its technology to the state on demand. Montgomery built the Western equivalent at El Alamein in October 1942 with a dummy pipeline timed to suggest a southern attack while real armor massed disguised in the north. \[5\]

The rest of the group. **7. Create Something from Nothing.** Repeats a fiction at enough volume that it reads as fact, the same device the Ghost Army ran against German reconnaissance in 1944. \[6\] **9. Watch the Fire from Across the River.** Lets rivals destroy each other and advances into the ruin, the logic behind the Two-Ocean Navy Act of July 1940. \[7\] **10. Hide a Knife Behind a Smile.** Projects warmth until a structural trap closes. **11. Sacrifice the Plum Tree to Preserve the Peach.** Surrenders a lesser asset to shield the critical one. **12. Take the Opportunity to Pilfer a Goat.** Pockets every incidental gain until the aggregate decides the outcome, the same logic behind the Alsos Mission's trailing collection of scientists and equipment. \[9\]

### Group III. Stratagems of Attack

Use deception, sabotage and indirect force to degrade the adversary's capability before any decisive engagement.

**Stratagem 18. Capture the Ringleader to Disband the Bandits.** This is the anchor. The actor does not fight the marching mass. It finds the node that gives the network its coherence and takes that instead. Hong Kong in 2020 ran this to the letter, jailing a publisher, freezing a newspaper's payroll and detaining the organizers rather than arresting a million marchers. American codebreakers ran the identical logic against Admiral Yamamoto in April 1943, intercepting his aircraft rather than attempting to degrade the fleet he commanded. \[10\]

The rest of the group. **13. Stomp the Grass to Scare the Snake.** Pressures a visible node so a hidden network moves and reveals its shape. **14. Borrow a Corpse to Resurrect the Soul.** Inhabits a hollow institution and runs new objectives on its residual legitimacy, the device behind Operation Mincemeat's fictional officer in 1943. \[11\] **15. Lure the Tiger Down the Mountain.** Draws the adversary onto terrain the actor controls. **16. Let the Enemy Off in Order to Catch Them.** Eases pressure to induce overextension, then tightens. Eisenhower ran this logic against the German counteroffensive in the Ardennes in December 1944. **17. Cast a Brick to Attract Jade.** Offers something of modest value to extract something far greater, the same trade Britain's Tizard Mission made in 1940. \[12\]

### Group IV. Stratagems of Confusion

Destabilize the adversary's operating environment through manufactured chaos and the removal of the foundations its power depends on.

**Stratagem 24. Borrow the Road to Conquer Guo.** This is the anchor. The classical actor asks passage through a neighbor's land under a neutral purpose, then uses the road for a second purpose the neighbor never granted. Telecommunications equipment sold as ordinary commerce carries an embedded duty, owed to the state under fusion law, to enable access the buyer never agreed to host. BGI's prenatal testing business, developed with the People's Liberation Army and marketed under a purely medical banner, runs the identical logic through a different door. \[8\] Britain and the Soviet Union ran the same logic against Iran in August 1941, opening a supply corridor that became an occupation for the war's duration and after. \[13\]

The rest of the group. **19. Remove the Firewood from Under the Pot.** Cuts the fuel that sustains a capability rather than attacking the capability directly. **20. Muddy the Waters to Catch the Fish.** Floods the field with noise until threat cannot be told from clutter, the same effect RAF chaff had over Hamburg in July 1943. \[14\] **21. Slough Off the Cicada's Golden Shell.** Leaves a convincing decoy in place while the real actor relocates undetected. **22. Shut the Door to Catch the Thief.** Encircles completely before engaging, the logic behind closing the Falaise Gap in August 1944. **23. Befriend a Distant State While Attacking a Neighbor.** Buys distant alignment to strip the target of a coalition it would otherwise call on.

### Group V. Stratagems of Control

From growing strength, infiltrate, replace and redirect the adversary's power structures until they serve the actor's own objectives.

**Stratagem 30. Make the Host and the Guest Exchange Places.** This is the anchor and the Agency dimension in practice. The move is one a target least expects, precisely because it is slow. The guest arrives deferential and dependent, and over years gathers relationships and institutional knowledge until it becomes indispensable, at which point it sets the agenda. Two decades inside the international standards bodies is the macro version this book's *Protocols and Ports* documents in full, delegates entering as observers and departing as drafters. \[15\] The United States played the identical role inside the Anglo-American alliance. It arrived in 1942 as the newer, deferential partner. By 1944 it was setting Overlord's terms over British planners' own preferences.

The rest of the group. **25. Replace the Beams with Rotten Timbers.** Swaps the load-bearing supports of an institution for compromised ones that hold until real weight applies. **26. Point at the Mulberry While Cursing the Locust.** Punishes a secondary target so the real audience reads the warning unaddressed. **27. Feign Madness Without Losing Balance.** Projects weakness so the adversary underestimates real capability. **28. Lure the Enemy onto the Roof, Then Remove the Ladder.** Induces dependency, then cuts the way back, the same sequence that deepened Japan's oil dependence before the 1941 cutoff. **29. Deck the Tree with False Blossoms.** Projects more strength or coalition than the actor holds, the device behind the fictional Fortitude divisions of 1944.

### Group VI. Stratagems of Desperate Situations

From disadvantage, use layered deception and the adversary's own assumptions to survive and reconstitute for the next engagement.

**Stratagem 36. If All Else Fails, Retreat.** This is the anchor. Alone among the thirty-six it names withdrawal as doctrine rather than failure. A disciplined retreat preserves the force for the next cycle. When the Party pulls back from a market or abandons a deal, the correct reading is repositioning, not defeat. American forces learned the same doctrine at Kasserine Pass in February 1943. \[16\] Command did not score the retreat as the campaign's end. It reorganized and returned weeks later to defeat the same army on the same ground.

The rest of the group. **31. The Beauty Trap.** Compromises a decision-maker through whatever desire makes that person susceptible. **32. The Empty Fortress.** Projects such confidence from real weakness that the adversary assumes a trap and declines to press, the posture Britain held after Dunkirk. **33. Sow Discord Among the Enemy.** Finds the existing fractures in an adversary's coalition and widens them. **34. Inflict Injury on Yourself to Win Trust.** Accepts visible damage to establish credibility and gain access otherwise unavailable, the method behind the Double-Cross System's controlled losses. \[17\] **35. Chain the Stratagems.** Links every other entry into one sequence, and gets its own section next.

### The Meta-Stratagem: Chaining

Stratagem 35 is the most important of the thirty-six, because no single stratagem alone produces the campaigns this book documents. Each case chapter names a chain, a sequence of these techniques run across domains where one entry's success is the condition the next one needs. The Allies chained the same way to open the decisive front in Europe. Stratagem 1 hid the invasion's true preparation inside routine movement orders. Stratagem 29 decked the northern flank with a fictional army threatening Norway. Stratagem 14 had already misdirected German reinforcement toward Sardinia and Greece over a year earlier. Stratagem 22 closed the door at Falaise only once the landing the earlier links had protected had succeeded. No single deception won Normandy. The chain did, exactly as this stratagem describes.

### The Full Taiwan Mapping

*Taiwan* runs nearly the entire repertoire at once, the only case in this book where all thirty-six appear. The table below carries the complete mapping, reproduced from that chapter.

### Sourcing Status: Appendix B

**\[1\]** *Thirty-Six Stratagems* (三十六計), traditional. The received text dates to the Ming or late Qing period, with individual stratagems drawn from far older sources including the *Book of Qi* and the *Romance of the Three Kingdoms*.

**\[2\]** The bypassing of the Japanese garrisons at Rabaul and Truk, 1943 to 1944, is documented across standard U.S. Navy operational histories of the Pacific campaign.

**\[3\]** The Doolittle Raid of April 18, 1942 and the Battle of Midway of June 4 to 7, 1942 are both extensively documented in the standard military histories of the Pacific War.

**\[4\]** The Destroyers-for-Bases Agreement was concluded on September 2, 1940.

**\[5\]** Operation Bertram, the deception plan supporting the Second Battle of El Alamein, ran in October 1942.

**\[6\]** The 23rd Headquarters Special Troops, the unit known as the Ghost Army, operated from 1944 to 1945. Its records were declassified in 1996.

**\[7\]** The Two-Ocean Navy Act is Public Law 76-757. Congress passed it on July 19, 1940.

**\[8\]** BGI Group's NIFTY prenatal test and its documented collaboration with the People's Liberation Army are covered in published research co-authorship and in public statements from the U.S. National Counterintelligence and Security Center. The precise count of women tested and the program's exact start date vary across sources, so this appendix states the scale qualitatively rather than with a specific figure.

**\[9\]** The Alsos Mission operated from 1943 to 1945, documented in National Archives Record Group 165.

**\[10\]** Operation Vengeance intercepted Admiral Yamamoto's aircraft on April 18, 1943.

**\[11\]** Operation Mincemeat ran from April to July 1943.

**\[12\]** The Tizard Mission traveled to the United States in September 1940.

**\[13\]** Operation Countenance was the Anglo-Soviet invasion of Iran. It ran from August 25 to September 17, 1941.

**\[14\]** Operation Gomorrah was the July 1943 bombing campaign over Hamburg. It saw the first operational use of Window, the codename for radar-jamming chaff.

**\[15\]** The full documentation of two decades of positional movement inside the ITU, ISO and 3GPP standards bodies appears in *Protocols and Ports*.

**\[16\]** The Battle of Kasserine Pass ran from February 19 to 24, 1943. Patton assumed command of II Corps on March 6, 1943.

**\[17\]** The Double-Cross System's official history has been released in full through the National Archives.

**Naming decisions applied throughout this book.** *Stratagem 9* is rendered Watch the Fire from Across the River. This is the closer rendering of 隔岸觀火, to observe a fire from the opposite bank, and it is standardized against an earlier variant. *Stratagem 14* is rendered Borrow a Corpse to Resurrect the Soul, standardized against an earlier variant that read Raise the Spirit. *Stratagem 30* is rendered Make the Host and the Guest Exchange Places. An earlier variant read turn the host into the guest, which inverts the actor, since in 反客為主 it is the guest who rises. All three corrections apply everywhere the stratagem appears in this book.

**Group structure, for reference.** Group I holds Stratagems 1 through 6, anchored on 4. Group II holds 7 through 12, anchored on 8. Group III holds 13 through 18, anchored on 18. Group IV holds 19 through 24, anchored on 24. Group V holds 25 through 30, anchored on 30. Group VI holds 31 through 36, anchored on 36.

**Stated as inference, not finding.** The claim that Huawei and ZTE base stations carry access capability owed to the state under fusion law is an argument from the 2017 National Intelligence Law's documented legal obligation, not from a demonstrated technical finding in any specific deployed equipment. What a particular base station does in practice is not publicly documented, and this appendix does not assert otherwise.

**On the Western parallels.** The historical episodes cited alongside each stratagem are standard, widely documented military history. They are drawn from operational histories and official records rather than from specialized or contested scholarship. None were independently re-verified against primary sources for this edition beyond the citations above, consistent with their status as settled history rather than live reporting.

## Appendix C. The Ten Dimensions and Twelve Domains in Full

*The Physics and the Battlefields*

Appendix A gave the ten Dimensions their formal TOGAF treatment. A name, a statement, a rationale and the implications that follow. This appendix gives both catalogs, Dimensions and Domains, their full reference detail instead. The working metaphor, a documented Party application and a Western historical parallel come first, followed by the measure and the fix this book proposes for each.

None of the numbered estimates below are measurements. Each is this book's own analytical judgment, stated as such at first use here and everywhere it recurs. A reader who thinks the true ratio runs at half the stated figure still faces the same argument, because nothing in this book turns on the exact number.

### The Ten Dimensions

**1. Time.** The size of the board, not the timing of a single blow. The Party plans in five-year blocks toward 2049 and took two decades to dismantle Hong Kong's autonomy, one step too small each time to trigger a hard answer. \[1\] American power runs on an eighteen-month planning window between elections. The 2015 breach of the Office of Personnel Management, which took the security files of 21.5 million people, drew no lasting penalty across three administrations. \[2\] Congress has reached for a longer horizon before, at Bretton Woods and at Los Alamos, and let it collapse back within days of each crisis passing. **Measure and fix.** The Party holds roughly a sevenfold edge in working horizon. The fix is structural. Competition strategy written into law for a decade or more, and accounts an annual budget cannot raid.

**2. Entropy.** How much force a system loses to friction on the way from decision to result. The Party locked down Wuhan, eleven million people, inside a day. \[3\] The fentanyl trade, run through a Chinese-to-cartel supply chain, killed 48,422 Americans in 2024. That is down from 76,282 the year before, while the supplier keeps a full layer of deniability. \[4\] Eighteen American intelligence organizations arguing with one another is the mirror image, a system that cannot move at the speed of one order. \[5\] The Civil War proves entropy is nobody's national trait. Confederate currency inflated to a fraction of face value inside three years, for want of a workable tax system, while the Union held one stable currency for the duration under the same pressure. **Measure and fix.** The Party moves roughly five times faster. The fix is not central control. One binding assessment a year that every agency answers to, plus real enforcement power in one body across all sixteen infrastructure sectors, would close the gap instead.

**3. Topology.** The shape power flows through, star or mesh. The Party runs a star. One hub reaches every province, state firm, university and citizen in a single hop. The physical version runs through Belt and Road. The influence version runs through more than six hundred United Front-linked overseas groups. \[6\] The West is a mesh by design, resilient to any single failure and slow to coordinate because every node answers to no one above it. Lincoln built the American demonstration first, running the telegraph office himself to collapse a chain of command that had taken weeks into an exchange that took hours. **Measure and fix.** The star coordinates roughly two and a half times faster. The fix is not to become a star. A thin layer of star-speed coordination grafted onto the mesh, for the single job of strategic assessment alone, answers better. Back it up wherever the hubs an adversary would target first happen to sit.

**4. Asymmetry.** Fighting where you are strong against where the rival is weak, and the edges compound rather than add. The Party stacks at least five. An open society against a closed one. Years of Western process against days of Party action. Cheap living-off-the-land intrusion against expensive total defense. An open society's visibility against a closed one's opacity. The attacker's choice of time and place against a defender who must guard everywhere. \[7\] Scott's naval blockade in 1861 and Mao's 1938 doctrine of concentrating against weakness while declining the enemy's chosen ground are the same refusal, arrived at independently, seventy-six years and one ocean apart. \[8\] **Measure and fix.** Stacked, the illustrative ceiling runs past ten thousand to one, a shape rather than a quantity. The fix is reciprocity as a rule. Price every asymmetric access, and tie the access a Chinese entity gets in an American sector to the access an American entity gets in return.

**5. Agency.** Whether a target was ever free to choose, beneath both Gradient and Resonance. The Party has captured real ground inside the institutions Americans trust most. Roughly a quarter of surveyed China scholars report self-censoring, and no order need ever be given once tuition and grant money have done the work. \[9\] Lend-Lease is the American demonstration in reverse, dependence bought rather than captured, arming allies with material they could not repay and watching their options narrow without a single directive. **Measure and fix.** The Party's pull runs roughly twelve to one against near-zero access flowing the other way. The fix is disclosure. Real-time public reporting of foreign funding above a set line for universities, think tanks and media, and cooling-off periods of a decade or more for officials moving into China-linked work.

**6. Resonance.** Whether every domain points one way, so effort multiplies instead of cancelling. Eleven of twelve domains aligned against Taiwan, from trade dependence to the collapse of its diplomatic allies to a message of inevitable defeat, so the twelfth domain lands with the weight of the rest. \[10\] The Gettysburg Address did the same work for the Union in two hundred seventy-two words, a message pitched at a frequency the audience was already tuned to receive. **Measure and fix.** The Party runs close to ninety-five percent domain alignment against a Western thirty. The fix is one coordinating authority with the standing to stop a Defense designation and a Treasury listing from pointing opposite ways.

**7. Phase.** Whether the blows land together or arrive in a sequence the target can answer one at a time. Hong Kong's National Security Law took effect at a single hour on June 30, 2020. The arrests, the frozen accounts, the pulled apps and the dismissals landed together. No window opened to regroup. \[11\] Grant's simultaneous 1864 offensives and the five Normandy beaches fixed to one dawn prove the same principle needs no exotic technology, only the refusal to run a plan as a sequence when running it at once is available. **Measure and fix.** The Party's synchronization runs near eighty-five percent against a Western forty. The fix is pre-authorization. Design and approve the response before the trigger, so the deliberation happens before the event rather than during it.

**8. Gradient.** The slope that moves behavior with no order given. Access to a market of 1.4 billion buyers, revocable overnight, makes compliance the easy path. A studio rewrites a film before Beijing asks, and the deniability is total because nothing was ordered. \[12\] The Liberty Loan drives of 1917 and 1918 cut the same incline in the other direction, making bond purchase the socially visible default until purchase rates climbed past what any tax could have compelled. **Fix.** Flatten the slope rather than chase the order that was never given. Build second sources in the most exposed sectors, and end the post-government payday with real cooling-off periods.

**9. Criticality.** The tipping point where a small push starts a fall far larger than the push. The Party's near-monopoly on rare-earth processing forced a Japanese climbdown within weeks in 2010 after a single administrative halt, and Taiwan's concentration of advanced chip fabrication makes a single strait the hinge of the global economy. \[13\] The Allied bombing campaign against German oil in 1944 crippled the Wehrmacht's fuel within months. An earlier campaign against ball-bearing plants destroyed real capacity but produced no comparable collapse, because production dispersed and substitutes appeared faster than the campaign could close every avenue. **Fix.** A critical chokepoint yields roughly a tenfold multiplier. Map the West's own chokepoints first, stockpile and second-source the worst of them, and hold whatever chokepoints the West already occupies inside the rival's own supply chains as deterrents rather than trading them away for short-term profit.

**10. Coherence.** The master Dimension, deciding whether the other nine add or cancel. Democratic centralism and a personnel system that controls every appointment from governors to editors gave the Party's Zero-COVID policy total reversal, nationwide, in seventy-two hours. \[14\] Pearl Harbor produced the American demonstration without any command structure forcing it. The same alignment built the arsenal and, in the same years, authorized the internment the Supreme Court upheld in *Korematsu* and Congress later redressed. \[15\] Neither precedent, a shock or a purge machine, is a model worth wanting. **Measure and fix.** The Party runs close to ninety-five percent institutional alignment against a Western twenty-five. The compound edge across all ten Dimensions runs, illustratively, near eight hundred to one. The fix comes first, before any other repair, because every other investment gets partly cancelled without it. A standing council built to convene rather than command.

### The Accelerant: Why AI Is Not an Eleventh Dimension

Artificial intelligence does not join the ten Dimensions as an eleventh. It removes the human friction that once rationed all ten at once. Coordinating one stratagem across three domains used to take a bureaucracy that never tired and a network that never leaked, a constraint of patience rather than secrecy. AI erases the constraint. It does not select a favorite Dimension. It lifts the ceiling on Time, Entropy, Phase and every other term at once. Millions of simulations run against a model of a target's economy and politics faster than the target can finish observing the first move.

The Party was first to the engine because it already held every precondition. A single chain of command with almost no internal friction left for the engine to remove, a population it could model without consent, and no institutional check that would object on principle to turning the whole apparatus on a civilian population. \[16\] China is the proof of concept, not the patent holder, and the disposition the engine converts into capability is not a nationality. A posture toward the world travels to any actor, state or otherwise, that reaches the same threshold of will and access.

The engine cuts both ways for whoever holds it. A system coherent enough to act as one and fast enough to act before anyone can object has built a machine for executing mistakes flawlessly as well as strategies. The Party's own policy whiplashes are a preview. The durable answer is not a faster machine. A society coherent and clear-eyed enough that an injected signal does not shatter it, when the machine that injects it arrives in more than one capital, is what actually lasts.

### The Twelve Domains

**1. Kinetic.** The domain every reader already understands, and the one where non-use does the work. The People's Liberation Army's missile inventory and near-daily incursions into Taiwan's air defense identification zone shape Taipei's politics without a shot fired, each incursion banked as evidence for the next warning. \[17\] The German battleship *Tirpitz* tied down a disproportionate share of the Royal Navy for three years while firing at almost nothing, sunk only in November 1944. A fleet in being is a fleet employed. **Fix.** Price the standing threat honestly, invest in mine countermeasures and distributed basing that lower a first strike's payoff, and stop scoring restraint as proof the threat was empty.

**2. Economic.** The flow of goods a country needs to live and fight, turned into leverage. The Party processes the large majority of the world's rare earths and, in 2023, restricted gallium and germanium exports to remind the West the valve exists. \[18\] Washington froze Japan's oil supply in 1941 after Japan occupied southern Indochina, holding the chokepoint the United States then controlled. Chokepoint control is proven American doctrine, not a Chinese invention. **Fix.** Lower criticality with stockpiles and funded second sources, treating the added cost as an insurance premium rather than a market failure.

**3. Financial.** Money and the rails that carry it. The variable interest entity routes American retirement money into flagged firms through a Caribbean shell, while one arm of government names the same firm a security risk and another keeps it listed. \[19\] Executive Order 8389 in 1940 and the freeze on Japanese assets in July 1941 prove the freeze is an American wartime instrument with its own pedigree. **Fix.** Reconcile the security-designation lists with the investment indices so one government stops funding what another forbids.

**4. Information.** The fight over a shared set of facts. Coordinated inauthentic networks push synthetic consensus in identical phrasing across supposedly independent accounts inside fifteen-minute windows. \[20\] The Office of War Information proved in 1942 that a democracy could run coordinated messaging at national scale without shutting its own free press, the same instrument the Party now runs without that restraint. **Fix.** Treat the recommendation algorithm as the weapon it is, subject to security review and independent audit, not only the data it runs on.

**5. Psychological.** The war on the will to keep resisting. Transnational repression holds a relative hostage to a diaspora member's silence abroad, an operation this book's *Long Arm* documents in full. \[21\] SHAEF's Psychological Warfare Division proved in 1944 and 1945 that leaflets and radio, aimed at the will rather than the equipment, could produce tens of thousands of surrenders combat alone would have cost far more to achieve. **Fix.** Name transnational repression a national-security offense rather than a private dispute, and protect the diaspora as its target rather than treating it as a suspect population.

**6. Legal.** Law written as a weapon and worn as a shield. The Anti-Secession Law of 2005 pre-licensed force against Taiwan years in advance, so the Party can call any future move lawful from its first hour. \[22\] The Foreign Agents Registration Act of 1938 ran the opposite instinct first, exposing an actor already in the room rather than licensing an action in advance. **Fix.** Build pre-authorized fast tracks for review in genuinely strategic cases, so the law can move in weeks without weakening the ordinary process everyone else relies on.

**7. Association.** Coalitions bought and coalitions fractured. The United Front Work Department runs a star of influence through hundreds of overseas organizations, and Belt and Road debt buys the silent abstention of the borrower at bodies like the UN Human Rights Council. \[23\] The Atlantic Charter and the Declaration by United Nations built a twenty-six-nation coalition on shared purpose in 1941 and 1942. The Party's version buys the same alignment with debt instead. **Fix.** Compete with better terms in the most contested states, and expose front groups so a borrowed voice stops passing for a native one.

**8. Cyber.** The fight inside the networks everything else runs on, decided by pre-positioning rather than theft. Volt Typhoon's implants lived inside American water, power and telecommunications systems for months using the target's own tools, taking nothing and waiting for the hour a Taiwan crisis would need them to fail together. \[24\] The Special Operations Executive and the Office of Strategic Services ran the identical architecture with human cells across occupied France, activated on a single coded broadcast the night before D-Day. **Fix.** Hunt for the implant on the assumption it is already there, and speed the handoffs between the agencies and the private owners who hold the infrastructure.

**9. Cultural.** What a society admires and teaches, moved without an order. Market access to one of the largest box offices on earth gets studios to censor their own scripts before Beijing has to ask. \[25\] The Office of War Information's Bureau of Motion Pictures ran a milder, four-year version of the identical leverage, trading favorable film-stock allocations for cooperative scripts. **Fix.** Require disclosure of any editorial condition tied to foreign-market access, and reduce dependence on the single market that makes the slope so steep.

**10. AI and Cognitive.** The race to see, decide and act first, compressed toward zero. The Party's Digital Twin, a high-fidelity population model built from harvested data, aims every other domain. Its sharpest synthetic media are procedural forgeries, not sensational fakes. \[26\] Colossus at Bletchley Park proved the principle in 1944, reading enciphered German orders before their own recipients had finished acting on them. **Fix.** Keep a human at every high-consequence node, and slow the automation pipeline just enough at decisive points to let judgment intervene.

**11. Biological.** Degradation kept below the threshold of acknowledged war. Chinese-sourced fentanyl precursors, cooked by Mexican cartels, killed 48,422 Americans in 2024. That is a real decline from 76,282 the year before, and it reflects enforcement rather than any change in supply. \[27\] Malaria hospitalized more Marines on Guadalcanal in 1942 than combat wounds did, proving a domain does not need an enemy attack to prove itself decisive. **Fix.** Attack the proxy chain at the broker and the precursor rather than policing only the street, and answer the trade as statecraft rather than ordinary crime.

**12. Data, Surveillance and Intelligence.** Knowing more about the target than the target knows about you, at population scale and at committee scale together. The Ministry of State Security runs collection abroad and policing at home as one function, while the National Security Act of 1947 walls the CIA out of the FBI's own domestic lane. \[28\] The Census Bureau's supply of block-level population data used against Japanese Americans in 1942 is this domain's darkest American precedent, and Operation Overlord's fusion of Ultra decryption with the Fortitude deception is its clearest demonstration done right. **Fix.** Stand up a genomic-data authority with review standing equal to a port or a chip plant, and build one small fusion cell cleared to trade leads across the wall before a case is already filed.
