## Appendix D. The Doctrine Crosswalk

*Mapping the Ten Dimensions Against Established Doctrine*

A military reader arrives at this book already owning a vocabulary. This appendix maps the ten Dimensions against three doctrine systems that reader already knows. The nine Principles of War, the Army's six warfighting functions, and joint doctrine's seven joint functions. Some Dimensions translate cleanly. Others do not, and the gap is itself a finding.

### The Three Systems, Briefly

**The nine Principles of War** are these. Objective. Offensive. Mass. Economy of Force. Maneuver. Unity of Command. Security. Surprise. Simplicity. \[1\] The list reached its current form in 1949 and has held since, the closest thing American doctrine has to a constitution for tactical and operational thought.

**The Army's six warfighting functions** are these. Command and Control. Movement and Maneuver. Intelligence. Fires. Sustainment. Protection. \[2\] The Army replaced an older set of eight Battlefield Operating Systems with this six-function structure in 2008. Treat the Battlefield Operating Systems as history. Nothing in this book's framework maps against them, and no current doctrine does either. One naming note matters for a reader working from an older manual. The first function was called Mission Command from 2008 until July 2019, when the Army restored the term Command and Control for the function itself, keeping mission command as the philosophy a commander applies through it. \[3\]

**Joint doctrine's seven joint functions** are these. Command and Control. Intelligence. Fires. Movement and Maneuver. Protection. Sustainment. Information. \[4\] Joint doctrine ran six functions, without Information, until 2017. A reader working from a source written before that date will find six, not seven, and should update the count rather than assume an error.

That 2017 addition is worth pausing on. The joint force concluded, independently of this book and years before it existed, that information had to stand as its own function rather than as a supporting task inside the other six. This book's own argument, that Agency and Resonance deserve standing as Dimensions in their own right rather than as footnotes to Fires or Intelligence, reached the same conclusion by a different road.

### The Crosswalk

| Dimension | Nearest Principle of War | Nearest Warfighting or Joint Function | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|
| Time | None directly | Sustainment | Loose |
| Entropy | Simplicity | Command and Control | Close |
| Topology | Unity of Command | Command and Control | Close |
| Asymmetry | Economy of Force, Maneuver | Movement and Maneuver | Close |
| Agency | Security | Information | Loose |
| Resonance | Unity of Command | Command and Control | Close |
| Phase | Surprise, Mass | Fires | Close |
| Gradient | None directly | Information | Loose |
| Criticality | Objective, Mass | Fires, Intelligence | Close |
| Coherence | Unity of Command | Command and Control | Closest |

Six of the ten sit close to doctrine already in place. Coherence maps almost exactly onto Unity of Command, restated at civilizational scale rather than at the scale of a single operation. Resonance is the same principle applied across domains instead of across units. Phase restates Surprise and Mass together, the timing half of each. Criticality restates Objective and Mass from the standpoint of the target rather than the attacker. Entropy and Topology both live inside what doctrine already calls Command and Control, read as friction and as shape respectively.

### Where the Fit Is Loose, and Why

Three Dimensions resist a clean mapping, and each resistance teaches something.

**Time** has no principle of war to answer to, because the principles assume two forces fighting on comparable horizons. A campaign has a beginning and an end, and the principles govern conduct inside that span. Time as this book uses it describes a mismatch between horizons themselves, a rival planning in decades against a rival planning in election cycles, and doctrine built for a bounded campaign has nothing to say about an unbounded one. Sustainment comes closest only because it is the function concerned with duration, not because it was built for this purpose.

**Agency** has no principle of war to answer to either, because the principles assume both sides know a contest is underway. A captured institution does not know it has been captured, and doctrine written to guide a commander who knows he is fighting has no native vocabulary for an actor who does not know it yet. Security comes closest, since Security is defined as denying the enemy an unexpected advantage, and institutional capture is exactly an advantage taken before the target notices the taking. The fit stays loose because Security, in its classical sense, still assumes an adversary relationship both parties recognize.

**Gradient** is the newest gap of the three. Doctrine built around force, the application or threat of it, has no principle for a slope built entirely from incentive. The target never has to be threatened at all, because compliance is the easier path on its own. The 2017 addition of Information as a joint function is the closest doctrine has come to naming this territory. Information is where an incentive-based influence operation would have to sit if joint doctrine tried to house it, but Information as currently defined is built around messaging and deception, not around the design of markets and institutions that Gradient describes.

None of these gaps is a defect in the Principles of War. The principles were never meant to answer a contest fought below the threshold of open war. Such a contest runs across financial, legal and cultural terrain in peacetime, waged by a party that treats every domain as one campaign. They are a defect only in the assumption that nine principles built for a bounded battle between uniformed forces would still cover a contest that has moved outside those bounds. The three loose fits mark exactly where the contest has moved.

### Reading a Case Chapter Against the Crosswalk

A military reader working through this book's case studies can use the table above as a translation key. Where a case chapter names Coherence as the signature gap, read Unity of Command, applied across departments rather than across units in the field. A case naming Phase calls for the Surprise and Mass a commander already knows, compressed from a battlefield timescale to a policy timescale. Agency or Gradient named in a case should signal no clean doctrinal analogue at all, since these two Dimensions are where this book's argument and existing doctrine most clearly diverge, and where a reader's own doctrinal instincts will least prepare him for what the case describes.

### Sourcing Status: Appendix D

**\[1\]** The nine Principles of War reached their current form in U.S. Army Field Service Regulations in 1949 and have been restated in every subsequent capstone doctrine publication since. The nine, in the order this book uses, are Objective. Offensive. Mass. Economy of Force. Maneuver. Unity of Command. Security. Surprise. Simplicity.

**\[2\]** Army Doctrine Publication 3-0 defines the six warfighting functions. The Army replaced the earlier eight Battlefield Operating Systems with this structure in 2008.

**\[3\]** *New.* Army Doctrine Publication 6-0 was published July 31, 2019. Its new title reads *Mission Command, Command and Control of Army Forces*. The revision restored Command and Control as the name of the warfighting function itself and retained mission command as the Army's philosophy for exercising it. The Army's own account of the change states plainly that the earlier single term had created ambiguity between the philosophy, the warfighting function and the supporting systems.

**\[4\]** *New.* Joint Publication 1 and Joint Publication 3-0 define the joint functions. Information was added as the seventh joint function in 2017, following a doctrine revision process that ran from 2016 into 2017. The six functions before that addition were these. Command and Control. Intelligence. Fires. Movement and Maneuver. Protection. Sustainment.

**Stated as inference, not finding.** Every entry in the crosswalk table is this book's own analytical judgment about which existing doctrinal category sits nearest a given Dimension. No cited military publication endorses this mapping, and a reader with deeper doctrinal training may reasonably draw some lines differently. The claim that Agency and Gradient mark genuine gaps in the Principles of War, rather than gaps in this book's own reading of them, is likewise an argument offered to the reader rather than a settled finding.

## Appendix E. The Digital Twin

*A Working Model of Everything the Party Means to Aim*

**DOMAINS** Economic · Financial · Information · Psychological · Legal · Association · Cyber · AI and Cognitive · Biological · **Data and Surveillance (spine)**

**DIMENSIONS** Time · Entropy · Topology · Asymmetry · Agency · Resonance · Phase · Gradient · **Criticality (signature)** · **Coherence (master)**

**STRATAGEMS** Spine S9 · S12 · S13 · S20 · S35

**GAPS** Six, one at each layer. Signature gap at the Data layer, where the defenders' own data is for sale.

In September 2020 researchers published a leaked database from Zhenhua Data, a company in Shenzhen that described its clients as including the Party's military. The database held profiles of about 2.4 million people around the world. The profiles covered politicians and officers, executives and academics, and their families. Zhenhua assembled them largely from open sources and sorted people into categories such as politically exposed persons and their relatives and close associates. Analysts found that about a fifth of the data did not come from open sources. Assessments of the database's value conflict, from an aspirational commercial venture to a real tool of influence. \[1\]

The harvest behind such files is older and larger. In 2015 the Office of Personnel Management lost the background-investigation records of about 21.5 million Americans, including the forms that list a cleared person's relatives, debts and foreign contacts. The health insurer Anthem lost records on about 78.8 million people that year. Equifax lost the financial records of about 147 million Americans in 2017. The Justice Department indicted four officers of the People's Liberation Army for the Equifax breach in 2020. It charged a Chinese national in the Anthem breach in 2019. \[2\] \[3\]

In November 2024 the FBI and the Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency reported that Party-linked hackers had broken into multiple American telecommunications carriers. They stole call records and reached the systems carriers use to fulfill court-ordered wiretaps. Investigators named the operation Salt Typhoon. \[4\]

The Party keeps files on its own people too. It has kept a personnel dossier on every urban worker, student and official since the 1950s. In Xinjiang a police platform flags people for travel, prayer and phone use and sends officers to investigate. \[5\]

This case calls the assembly a Digital Twin. The twin is a working model of people and companies and of the assets, minerals and supply chains they depend on. It also models spectrum and psychology. It runs across time and learns from events. The term is the framework's own. The record documents every harvest named here. No public document shows the Party running one unified twin. The assembly is the framework's argument.

### The Terms of This Case

A digital twin, in engineering, is a live software model of a physical system. Sensors feed it, and engineers use it to forecast failure and test changes before touching the real machine. The architecture carries over to a population. Data feeds the model, and the model forecasts behavior and tests pressure before anyone applies it.

The actor is the Party. The Chinese people are the twin's first and most completely modeled population. Their dossiers, their health codes and their real-name registrations built the method before it faced outward.

The case keeps one line that must survive any edit. It never treats a person's ancestry or nationality as evidence. It describes a method of collection and modeling. The defense it proposes models dependencies, never people.

### 1. Architecture Vision

**Documented.** The State Council's plan for artificial intelligence of July 2017 set the goal of world leadership in the field by 2030. The National Intelligence Law obliges every organization on the mainland to support state intelligence work. The Party's rules on human genetic resources, in force since July 2019, restrict the export of Chinese genetic data. \[6\] \[7\]

**Argued.** The aim is a model complete enough to aim every other domain. Such a model shows whom the Party can recruit, pressure or ignore. It marks the firm, mineral or cable whose loss would cause the most harm, and the division in a society that carries the most charge.

### 2. The Party's Baseline

#### Business Architecture. The Capabilities It Fielded

The Data and Surveillance domain carries the operation, because the twin is the capability that lets every other capability aim.

**Economic.** The Party models supply chains well enough to govern them abroad. On October 9, 2025 its Ministry of Commerce claimed export-control jurisdiction over foreign-made products containing as little as 0.1 percent Chinese-origin rare-earth value. The rule copied America's foreign direct product rule and added a 50 percent affiliates rule. The Party suspended it on November 7, 2025 for one year, in exchange for the American suspension of Commerce's affiliates rule. Controls on gallium and germanium date from August 2023. \[8\]

**Financial.** The capital flows in *The Borrowed Knife* feed the model with the ownership and funding of foreign firms.

**Information.** The published record of the open world feeds the model directly. The Patent and Trademark Office publishes new applications every Thursday and offers the full corpus as bulk data. \[9\]

**Psychological.** The recommendation data in *The Cognitive-Industrial Complex* map a society's moods and divisions.

**Legal.** The intelligence law makes every mainland company a potential sensor. The genetic-resources rules keep Chinese data at home while foreign data flows in.

**Association.** Talent programs and united-front networks add who knows whom.

**Cyber.** Intrusion supplies what the open record does not. The OPM, Anthem, Equifax and Salt Typhoon breaches each added a layer.

**AI and Cognitive.** Machine learning turns the harvest into forecasts at a speed no human staff could match.

**Biological.** BGI, the Party-favored genomics firm, sold a prenatal test used by millions of women in dozens of countries. Reuters reported in 2021 that BGI stored genetic data from the test in a national gene bank and developed the test with the PLA. The National Counterintelligence and Security Center warned in February 2021 that the Party was collecting American health and genomic data. Commerce added BGI units to the Entity List in 2023. \[10\]

**Data and Surveillance.** The domain assembles the rest.

#### Data Architecture. The Twin's Schema

An architect describes a data model by its entities. The record documents a harvest for each.

| Entity | What the harvest supplies | Documented source |
|---|---|---|
| The mainland population | Dossiers, real-name registration, health codes, police flags | Party practice since the 1950s. Human Rights Watch on Xinjiang, 2019 |
| Americans | Background forms, health records, credit files | OPM 2015, Anthem 2015, Equifax 2017 |
| People worldwide | Profiles of about 2.4 million influential people | The Zhenhua Data leak, 2020 |
| Communications | Call records and wiretap systems | Salt Typhoon, 2024 |
| Genomes | Prenatal test data held in a national gene bank | Reuters, 2021 |
| Companies and technology | Weekly patent publications, filings and certificates | *Technology IP* |
| Minerals and supply chains | Enough visibility to write a 0.1 percent rule | Ministry of Commerce, October 2025 |
| Capital | Ownership and funding of foreign firms | *The Borrowed Knife* |
| Psychology | Behavior, mood and division | *The Cognitive-Industrial Complex* |

The model spans time. The dossiers run back to the 1950s, and the stolen background forms of 2015 describe people who will still hold clearances in the 2040s. A background form lists relatives and debts, which change slowly. Stolen once, it stays useful for a career.

**Documented.** American military doctrine for psychological operations runs target audience analysis. The analysis rates a group's vulnerabilities, its susceptibility to a message and the accessibility of channels that reach it. \[11\]

**Argued.** A twin of this breadth can run the same analysis on a whole population. It can score vulnerability, susceptibility and accessibility person by person, and keep the result as a dossier. The *Psychological Operations* case takes up that use.

#### Application Architecture. The Discovery Loop

The twin's routine is a loop. It gathers and models, then forecasts and acts, then measures and retrains.

**Documented.** Human Rights Watch reverse-engineered the app behind Xinjiang's Integrated Joint Operations Platform in 2019. The platform collects data on phone use, travel, electricity use and religious practice. It flags people whose patterns it deems suspicious and sends officers to investigate, and the officers' reports feed back into the platform. \[5\]

**Argued.** The same loop runs outward. The patent corpus arrives weekly and machine reading can process it the same day. A model can tell which foreign inventions have no mainland counterpart and which inventors to recruit. It can see which suppliers to watch. Each probe, each recruitment and each export-control announcement becomes an experiment whose result retrains the model. Checking forecasts against events is what turns a database into a twin.

**Stratagem 13, Stomp the Grass to Scare the Snake**, probes to see how the target responds and records the response.

**Stratagem 12, Take the Opportunity to Pilfer a Goat**, takes every record a breach exposes whether it helps now or later.

**Stratagem 9, Watch the Fire from Across the River**, lets the open world publish and reads.

**Stratagem 20, Muddy the Waters to Catch the Fish**, uses the model to pick the divisions worth stirring.

**Stratagem 35, Chain the Stratagems**, runs the loop across every domain. The record documents each harvest and the domestic loop. The outward loop is the framework's reading.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platform It Ran On

The twin runs on the mainland's compute, its cloud and its platforms, and on the management plane of *Military-Civil Fusion*. Its most useful foreign platforms are the defenders' own. In Salt Typhoon the intruders reached the systems American carriers built for court-ordered wiretaps. The access point built for lawful use served whoever held it. \[4\]

#### Principles and Governance. How It Held Together

**Criticality.** The twin exists to find chokepoints, the node where a small push causes a large fall.

**Coherence.** One authority can join what its harvest collects. The record documents the harvests. The joining is the framework's argument.

### 3. The Defenders' Baseline

No single enterprise defended the defenders' data. Each breach met its own owner.

#### Business Architecture. Who Owned What

| Layer | Defender owner | Instrument |
|---|---|---|
| Federal personnel records | Office of Personnel Management | Its own security, breached in 2015 |
| Health and credit records | Private firms, under sector regulators | Breach notification and fines |
| Commercial data brokers | Federal Trade Commission | A 2024 law barring sales to foreign adversaries |
| Bulk sensitive data | Justice Department | A 2025 rule on transfers to countries of concern |
| Telecommunications | Carriers, the FCC and CISA | Carrier security and advisories |
| Genomic data and biotech | Commerce, and federal procurement | Entity List and the 2026 procurement bar |
| Critical minerals | Geological Survey, Energy and Defense | Lists of critical minerals |

#### Data Architecture. What the Defenders Knew

The defenders' own data stayed on sale for years. Commercial brokers bought and sold Americans' location, health and financial data with few limits. Congress barred brokers from selling Americans' sensitive data to foreign adversaries in April 2024. The Justice Department's rule on bulk data took effect in April 2025. It restricts transfers of genomic and biometric data to countries of concern, along with health, location and financial data. \[12\]

The flow of genetic data ran one way. The Party restricted the export of Chinese genetic data from 2019. American genetic data reached mainland firms until the controls of 2023 and later. \[7\] \[10\]

#### Application Architecture. The Routines the Defenders Ran

The defenders treated each breach as an incident. OPM, Anthem, Equifax and the carriers each ran their own response. Prosecutors indicted hackers, and regulators fined firms. Treasury sanctioned a mainland company in January 2025 for its role in Salt Typhoon. \[3\] \[4\]

The defenders ran no discovery loop across the harvests. No public office assessed what the combined breaches let an adversary model.

#### Technology Architecture. The Platforms the Defenders Used

The defenders built lawful access into their own networks. The carriers' wiretap systems exist because the law requires them. In Salt Typhoon the adversary used them.

#### Principles and Governance. What Held the Defenders Together

The defenders kept their principles. A free society does not keep a dossier on every citizen, and it should not. That principle is the reason the target twins things and never people.

### 4. Gap Analysis

**The principles audit**

| Principle | The Party's design | The defenders' design |
|---|---|---|
| **Time** | Keeps what it takes for decades | Treats each breach as a closed incident |
| **Entropy** | One authority can join every harvest | Each breach has its own owner |
| **Topology** | A hub that reads every layer | A mesh of firms and agencies |
| **Asymmetry** | Bars export of its genetic data | Let its own flow out for years |
| **Agency** | Every mainland firm is a potential sensor | Brokers sold data to anyone until 2024 |
| **Resonance** | Harvests feed one model | Harvests are handled one at a time |
| **Phase** | Reads the patent corpus weekly | Wrote data-transfer rules in 2024 and 2025 |
| **Gradient** | Collection rides on commerce and convenience | Data sales earned revenue with no security price |
| **Criticality** | Models chokepoints well enough to write a 0.1 percent rule | Lists critical minerals without a model of the flows |
| **Coherence** | Governed by one authority | No owner of the aggregate exposure |

The cells summarize documented acts. The audit itself is the framework's reading.

#### Gap 1. No Owner of the Aggregate Exposure

*Architecture Governance*

**Documented.** OPM, Anthem, Equifax and the carriers each handled their own breach. Prosecutors and regulators acted case by case. \[2\] \[3\] \[4\]

**Argued.** Each breach was an incident to its owner and a layer to the adversary. No one assessed what the layers add up to once joined.

#### Gap 2. The Harvest Is Permanent

*Architecture Principles, Time*

**Documented.** The stolen background forms of 2015 describe people who still hold or seek clearances. \[2\]

**Argued.** Data that changes slowly stays useful for decades. The defenders close an incident in months, and the adversary's model keeps the record for a career.

#### Gap 3. The Data Layer Was for Sale

*Data Architecture. The signature gap*

**Documented.** Brokers sold Americans' sensitive data with few limits until the 2024 law. The Justice Department's bulk-data rule took effect in 2025. The Party restricted the export of its own genetic data from 2019. \[7\] \[12\]

**Argued.** The adversary did not need to steal what it could buy. The defenders' data architecture had a market where it needed a control, and the controls arrived years after the Party closed its own door.

#### Gap 4. Dependencies Without a Model

*Business Architecture*

**Documented.** The Party wrote a rule that traces Chinese content through foreign products at a threshold of 0.1 percent. The defenders keep lists of critical minerals. \[8\] \[13\]

**Argued.** A list names what matters. A twin shows how it flows, where it chokes and what fails when it stops. The Party showed it holds the second, and the defenders mostly hold the first.

#### Gap 5. No Discovery Loop

*Application Architecture*

**Documented.** The Party runs a gather, flag, act and feedback loop at home. The defenders' analysis runs by agency and by request. \[5\]

**Argued.** The side with the faster loop learns faster from every contact. The defenders publish their inventions weekly and read their own corpus slower than the adversary reads it.

#### Gap 6. The Lawful Door Opened for the Wrong Hand

*Technology Architecture*

**Documented.** Salt Typhoon reached the wiretap systems American carriers built for court orders. \[4\]

**Argued.** An access point built into a platform serves whoever holds it. The defenders built a door for their own courts, and the adversary walked through it.

### 5. Target Architecture

The target rests on one principle. Twin things, not people. The Party models its citizens and foreigners. The defenders should model minerals and supply chains, spectrum and infrastructure, and their own exposure. They should never keep a dossier on their citizens.

**Gap to target**

| Gap | Target element | Owner | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. No owner of the aggregate exposure | A standing assessment of what all known breaches let an adversary model, joined across owners | National Strategic Competition Council, with the intelligence agencies | Classified effort, and uncomfortable findings |
| 2. The harvest is permanent | Security practices that assume stolen data stays live, such as rotating what can be rotated for exposed people | OPM and the clearance agencies | Cost, and burden on cleared personnel |
| 3. The data layer was for sale | Minimum collection and firm limits on broker sales, extended to allies | Congress, the FTC and the Justice Department | Revenue lost by brokers, and friction for research |
| 4. Dependencies without a model | A dependency twin of minerals, drugs and components that shows flows and chokepoints | Commerce, Energy, Defense and Health, with allies | Data from private firms, and protection of their secrets |
| 5. No discovery loop | An allied loop that reads the open corpus, including patents, as fast as any adversary | Research agencies and the Patent and Trademark Office | Compute, and analysts |
| 6. The lawful door opened | Wiretap systems built to security standards, with the access points audited | Congress, the FCC and the carriers | Cost to carriers, and debate over lawful access |

**Drugs belong in the dependency twin.** The Drug Enforcement Administration names mainland suppliers as the main source of fentanyl precursor chemicals, and the Justice Department charged eight mainland chemical companies in October 2023. American generic medicines depend on foreign active ingredients whose exact origins the Food and Drug Administration has told Congress it cannot fully trace. A dependency twin shows both flows without modeling a single person. \[14\]

**The twin of things protects liberty.** A dependency model needs data about shipments, factories and ores. It needs no data about any citizen's beliefs, and the target keeps it that way.

**What the target would not do.** It would not match the Party's twin. A free society that built a dossier on every citizen would become the thing it defends against. That claim remains an argument.

### What the Digital Twin Proves

It proves Criticality. The twin exists to find the node where a small push causes a large fall, and the Party showed in October 2025 that it can see supply chains well enough to govern them abroad.

It proves the dossier is old and only the scale is new. The personnel files of the 1950s, the health codes of 2022 and the stolen background forms of 2015 serve the same purpose. The Chinese people were the first file.

It proves the defenders' weakness sits in their own data architecture. They sold what the adversary would otherwise have had to steal. They built doors the adversary used, and they published their inventions faster than they read them.

The answer is not a Western twin of the Western citizen. The answer is a twin of things. It covers the ores and pills, the chips and cables and the flows a free society depends on, and someone who looks at the whole owns it.

This appendix has sketched the architecture in outline. The twin itself deserves the fuller treatment a single case chapter cannot give it. Its data model, its discovery loop and the gap it leaves open all belong to work beyond this book's scope. What stays fixed regardless of how far that future work goes is the one line this appendix opened with. Twin things, not people.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The twin itself.* The record documents each harvest and the domestic loop. That one authority joins them into a single model is the framework's argument.

*The outward loop.* The Xinjiang platform documents a loop at home. Its use abroad is the framework's argument.

*Target audience analysis at scale.* The doctrine appears in published manuals. Its application to a population through a twin is the framework's argument.

*Drugs.* The record documents the flows of precursors and ingredients. The case claims no intent behind them.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**The line that must survive any edit.** The case never treats ancestry or nationality as evidence, and its defense models dependencies, never people.

## Appendix F. The Consolidated Roadmap

*Every Fix, Sequenced*

Every case chapter in this book closes with a target table, one gap and one fix at a time. Read alone, each fix looks reasonable. Read together, seventy-two of them would overwhelm any legislature, any agency and any budget cycle presented all at once. *The Roadmap* makes exactly that argument. This appendix does the sequencing that chapter only sketched.

TOGAF calls the discipline migration planning, and it sorts change into three transition architectures on the way to a target rather than the target itself. **Now** needs no new statute, because existing authority already covers it. **Next** needs one statute or one rule, not a program of them. **Later** needs years and money before it exists at all, regardless of who is willing.

Each row below carries the chapter it came from, so a reader can return to the full argument behind any single line.

### Now: No New Statute Needed

| Element | Owner | Cost | From |
|---|---|---|---|

| Financial continuity for independent institutions, held outside the adversary's jurisdiction | Treasury and allied finance ministries, with willing banks | Bank revenue, and staff who remain within reach | Hong Kong |
| Designations built on evidence gathered outside the target jurisdiction, protection for the diaspora | Intelligence and sanctions staffs | Analytic cost, and fewer local voices in the record | Hong Kong |

### Next: A Single Statute or Rule

| Element | Owner | Cost | From |
|---|---|---|---|

| Statutory independence and audit for truthful broadcasting, and privacy-preserving age signals set at the device | Congress, device makers and app stores | Budget, management reform, uneven adoption | Accommodate |

| Resolution rules that impose losses on insiders before any public money | Congress and bank regulators | Higher funding costs for large banks | Financial Control |
| Emergency financial powers with objective standards, sunsets and fast judicial review | Legislatures | Less flexibility in the first days | Financial Control |

### Later: Built Capacity, Years Regardless of Will

| Element | Owner | Cost | From |
|---|---|---|---|
| Treaty negotiators securing enforcement and verification behind any future two-systems guarantee | Treaty negotiators and legislatures | Agreements that never get signed | Hong Kong |
| A public map of standards seats and patents, and an open allied logistics data standard as an alternative to LOGINK | NIST, Transportation and allied governments | Analytic effort, and competition with a free product | Protocols and Ports |
| Travel grants for standards participation, and allied or domestic crane production | Research agencies, Transportation and Defense, with allies | Money, and years to build capacity | Protocols and Ports |
| Allied verification that does not depend on one international body | Allied public-health and intelligence agencies | Duplicate effort, and friction with the WHO | COVID-19 |
| Independent genomic and wastewater surveillance shared among allies | Allied health agencies | Money, and privacy safeguards to build | COVID-19 |
| Stockpiles and allied production for critical protective equipment | Health departments and industry | Warehousing costs, and higher unit prices | COVID-19 |
| A standing allied answer that buys what the Party bans for political reasons | Allied trade ministries | Money, and trade-law disputes | Taiwan |
| Leading-edge semiconductor capacity built across allied soil | Commerce and the chipmakers | Higher unit costs, and a thinner silicon shield for Taiwan | Taiwan |
| A dependency twin of minerals, drugs and components that shows flows and chokepoints | Commerce, Energy, Defense and Health, with allies | Data from private firms, and protection of their secrets | The Digital Twin |
| An allied discovery loop that reads the open patent corpus as fast as any adversary | Research agencies and the Patent and Trademark Office | Compute, and analysts | The Digital Twin |

### Reading the Roadmap

Seventy-two elements sort three ways. Thirty-one need no new law at all, only a decision inside an agency that already holds the authority to make it. Thirty-one need one statute or one rule apiece, not a program of them. Ten need years of capacity no statute can shortcut.

The pattern rewards a particular kind of patience. Most of the Now column costs nothing but the will to act, which is why it belongs first, before any legislature convenes. Most of the Next column repeats the same owner, the National Strategic Competition Council, across a dozen different lines. This is *Coherence Without Command*'s argument made visible in a table rather than in prose. Build that one convening body and roughly a third of the Next column becomes easier to pass, because the venue for agreeing on it already exists. The Later column carries the items no administration can rush, and the honest answer to each of them is to start now, because a decade from now is the earliest any of them arrive regardless of when the work begins.

### Sourcing Status: Appendix F

**Primary documents.** Every element in this appendix is drawn directly from the target table of the chapter named in its row. No new documented fact is asserted here that the source chapter does not already carry, and no citation number is repeated, since the source chapter's own Appendix H entry carries it.

**Stated as inference, not finding.** The sorting of each element into Now, Next or Later is this book's own judgment. It weighs what existing authority covers, what a single new rule could reach, and what requires years of capacity regardless of statute. A reader with deeper expertise in a given agency's actual authority may reasonably move some rows between columns. The count of seventy-two, and its rough three-way split, offers a picture of the whole roadmap's shape. No precise inventory that permits no revision is on offer here.

## Appendix G. The Shelf

*Twenty-Five Works, One Line Each*

**Qiao Liang and Wang Xiangsui, *Unrestricted Warfare* (1999).** Names most of this book's twelve domains a generation early, from the perspective of a weaker power. It does not ask what let the stronger power's own architecture go unguarded.

**Robert Spalding, *War Without Rules* (2022).** Reads the adversary's playbook in detail, chapter by chapter. It does not locate the defect inside the defender's own architecture, which is this book's project instead.

**Pat Choate, *A Great Wall of Patents* (2005).** Documents the mining of published patent applications years before anyone else noticed and recommends fixes that were never adopted. It stops at one domain, patents, and does not connect the pattern to the other eleven this book maps.

**Robert Jay Lifton, *Thought Reform and the Psychology of Totalism* (1961).** Names eight criteria of a totalist system from interviews with people who lived through Chinese thought reform. It describes what the system does to one mind, not what the same design does to twelve domains at once.

**Hannah Arendt, *Eichmann in Jerusalem* (1963).** Names the banality of evil, the ordinary clerk doing ordinary work inside an extraordinary system. It studies one office in one totalitarian bureaucracy, not a whole architecture run across a rival civilization.

**George F. Kennan, "The Inauguration of Organized Political Warfare" (1948).** Proves the United States once named and organized around exactly the whole-of-government instrument this book describes, three years after the war that built it. It never says what became of the instrument, or why the country stopped building it.

**Douglas Pike, *Viet Cong* (1966).** Documents a coherent political organization running every domain of a war a materially weaker side went on to win. It answers how. It does not ask whether the same coherence, run today, would look different with a machine behind it.

**Andrew Mertha, *Brothers in Arms* (2014).** Shows Hanoi's Politburo running a single coordinated struggle against a divided, better-resourced rival bureaucracy. It treats the coordination gap as a historical curiosity rather than a general property any divided system carries.

**Sun Tzu, *The Art of War*, the Giles translation of 1910.** Public domain. States the oldest form of nearly every stratagem this book catalogs, including the case for human sources over documents. It offers no architecture for running the whole catalog across twelve domains at machine speed.

**Niccolò Machiavelli, *The Prince*, the Marriott translation of 1908.** Public domain. States plainly that a ruler keeps faith only while it serves him. It has nothing to say about a guarantee kept by a government answerable to courts and elections instead of a prince.

**The Open Group, *TOGAF Standard*, 10th Edition (2022).** Supplies the whole vocabulary this book borrows. Architecture principles and architecture contracts, migration planning and an architecture board. The standard was built to align one enterprise's own systems, not to read an adversary's campaign against a rival civilization.

**National Institute of Standards and Technology, *Special Publication 800-207*, Zero Trust Architecture (2020).** States the rule that no access is trusted permanently and every request is verified. The publication was written for a computer network, and this book is the argument that the same rule governs a defense built across twelve domains.

**Ivan Arreguín-Toft, *How the Weak Win Wars* (2005).** Built the rigorous modern account of how a materially inferior actor defeats a stronger one, the foundation the asymmetry literature still stands on. It answers how the weak beat the strong, a different question from how a stronger side loses to its own uncoordinated strength.

**Timothy L. Thomas, "Russia's Reflexive Control Theory and the Military" (2004).** Names the Soviet doctrine of feeding an opponent selected information so the opponent's own reasoning delivers the decision the sender wanted. It describes the doctrine in the abstract and leaves its automation, at population scale, to whoever builds the machine.

**Robert D. Hare, *Without Conscience* (1993).** Catalogs the pattern of predatory control, selection and isolation followed by extraction, in an individual psychopath. The pattern transfers cleanly to a state's pressure campaign against a person abroad. The clinical claim never should, and this book borrows the shape only.

**Mao Zedong, "Problems of War and Strategy" (1938).** States in one line that the Party commands the gun and the gun must never command the Party, the whole logic of the commissar system compressed to a sentence. The line states a rule for a revolutionary party seizing power, not a rule any other kind of institution can adopt.

**Mao Zedong, "Introducing *The Communist*" (1939).** Names the united front as the first of the Party's three magic weapons, decades before the same instrument reached a diaspora abroad. It says nothing about what the instrument looks like once artificial intelligence runs its targeting.

**Frank Dikötter, *Mao's Great Famine* (2010).** Documents the deadliest famine in recorded history as the direct product of a coherent system with no channel for bad news to travel upward. The book stands as the clearest single proof that this book's master dimension has a body count when it runs unchecked.

**Frank Dikötter, *The Cultural Revolution* (2016).** Documents a civilization turned against its own teachers by the same instrument later aimed outward. It closes the case, argued throughout this book, that the Chinese people were this method's first subjects rather than its authors.

**Jonathan Phillips, *The Fourth Crusade and the Sack of Constantinople* (2004).** Traces the full Venice-Byzantium arc this book uses as its lifecycle anchor, from the 1082 trading privileges to the 1204 sack. It reads the collapse as one empire's failure of judgment, not as a five-stage pattern a modern institution might recognize in its own arrangements.

**Jack Weatherford, *Genghis Khan and the Making of the Modern World* (2004).** Recovers the *yam* relay network and the Mongols' use of merchants as intelligence, alongside the terror of the submit-or-be-destroyed ultimatum. It reads the empire as a story of conquest, not as an early demonstration of topology and psychological operations run as one instrument.

**National Institute of Standards and Technology, *Cybersecurity Framework 2.0* (2024).** Adds Governance as a sixth function beside the original five. Identify. Protect. Detect. Respond. Recover. The addition arrives at this book's own argument about coordination from a different direction entirely. The framework was built for a single organization's cyber posture, not for a civilization's whole contest.

**Department of Defense Architecture Framework, version 2.02.** Supplies the capability, operational, systems and data viewpoints this book borrows to show a military planner the framework in a vocabulary already his own. No one built it with a whole-of-society, peacetime, twelve-domain contest in view.

**MITRE Corporation, *ATT&CK* framework.** Proved that a shared taxonomy of adversary techniques turns one organization's observation into every other organization's warning, the model this book's own thirty-six stratagems follow. It catalogs techniques inside one domain, cyber, and this book is the argument for building the same catalog across twelve.

**Carnegie Mellon Software Engineering Institute, *Capability Maturity Model Integration*.** Supplies the five-level scale, Initial through Optimizing, this book borrows to grade a nation's cross-domain integration rather than a single organization's software process. No one ever built it to compare a free society against a totalitarian one, and this book's use of it is an adaptation, not an endorsement of the fit.

### A Note on This List

Twenty-five works cannot cover a subject this wide, and this appendix does not try. It gathers the sources this book leans on hardest, in one line each, so a reader who wants to go deeper on any single point knows exactly where to start. No entry ranks another. Several of these authors would disagree with each other, and a few would disagree with this book. The list is a shelf, not a verdict.

## Appendix H. Notes and Sourcing, by Chapter

*The Complete Record*

This appendix gathers the sourcing notes for every case chapter in Parts I through IV, in the order those chapters appear in the book. Each chapter's footnote numbers are local to that chapter alone. Chapter Hong Kong's citation 1 and Chapter Technology IP's citation 1 are different sources. The section headers below tell a reader which chapter's numbering is in view.

Within each chapter's notes, sources marked **Documented** are supported by the citation given. Passages marked as inference, argument or reading are this book's own analytical conclusions, stated as such rather than as demonstrated fact. Every classical anchor used anywhere in this book was verified against a live source before use, and no anchor is presented as a genuine quotation without that verification.

The front matter, the Prologue, Part II and Part V of this book carry their sourcing inline rather than here. Those chapters argue rather than document a single case, and their notes stay closer to the argument they support. Appendices A through D, F and G each carry their own sourcing within the appendix itself, for the same reason.

### Hong Kong

*This chapter's Documented and Argued labels stay inline in the chapter text. Only the citations move here.*

**Primary documents.** Notes marked *New* enter in this edition. The rest carry forward from the first edition.

**\[1\]** Law of the People's Republic of China on Safeguarding National Security in the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region. The law took effect at 11 p.m. local time on June 30, 2020. Article numbers appear in the text at each use. **Verified.** The full text appeared only at commencement, and the Legislative Council never voted on it.

**\[2\]** *New.* Hong Kong Free Press and the South China Morning Post reported Demosisto's dissolution on June 30, 2020. Studentlocalism and the Hong Kong National Front ended their Hong Kong operations the same day.

**\[3\]** *New.* The Associated Press and CNN reported the Hong Kong Police Force statements of July 1, 2020. The first arrest came shortly after midday. Police reported about 370 arrests that day, ten under the new law.

**\[4\]** *New.* Implementation Rules for Article 43, gazetted July 6, 2020. The rules cover content removal and data demands, interception on executive authorization and penalties for service providers who refuse. The Office for Safeguarding National Security opened on July 8, 2020. The police national security department dates from June 30, 2020.

**\[5\]** Apple Daily printed its final edition on June 24, 2021. Stand News closed on December 29, 2021, after a police raid and arrests. Citizen News announced its closure on January 2, 2022. Prosecutors charged the forty-seven on February 28, 2021, as the British government's six-monthly report for that half year records. Public libraries began withdrawing books for review in July 2020.

**\[6\]** Britain and the People's Republic signed the Joint Declaration on December 19, 1984, and registered it with the United Nations. The National People's Congress adopted the Basic Law in April 1990, and it took effect on July 1, 1997.

**\[7\]** Decision of the National People's Congress of May 28, 2020, on the legal system and enforcement mechanisms for safeguarding national security in Hong Kong. Decision of the National People's Congress of March 11, 2021, on improving the city's electoral system. The Standing Committee then amended Annexes I and II of the Basic Law. **Corrected.** The first edition attributed the March 2021 decision to the Standing Committee alone.

**\[8\]** The Security Bureau froze HK$18 million of assets held by three Apple Daily companies on June 17, 2021. The sum equals about US$2.3 million. Company filings and Security Bureau statements carry the figure. **Verified.**

**\[9\]** Police arrested Jimmy Lai and searched Apple Daily on August 10, 2020. Police put the number of officers at about two hundred. Apple Daily streamed the search live on its Facebook page, as Global News and Nieman Lab reported.

**\[10\]** The Legislative Council passed the Safeguarding National Security Ordinance in March 2024.

**\[11\]** The Professional Teachers' Union and the Civil Human Rights Front dissolved in August 2021. The Hong Kong Alliance dissolved in September 2021, and its June 4th Museum had closed that June. The government's statement on the slogan dates from July 2, 2020. National security education began in schools in February 2021. The Court of Appeal granted the injunction against the protest anthem in May 2024.

**\[12\]** *New.* National security police demanded the 612 Humanitarian Relief Fund's operational records in 2021. The fund suspended donations in September 2021 after its trustee advised that the bank would no longer process payments from the fund's account.

**\[13\]** Article 38 of the law. The Hong Kong Police Force announced HK$1 million bounties in July and December 2023.

**\[14\]** *New.* Basic Law Articles 18 and 158 and Annex III. The Standing Committee added the National Security Law to Annex III on June 30, 2020, and the Chief Executive promulgated it the same day.

**\[15\]** *New.* The Foreign Secretary's six-monthly reports to Parliament on Hong Kong. The report for January to June 2021 records the declaration of March 13, 2021. Parliamentary briefing material and published scholarship on the treaty note its lack of an enforcement mechanism.

**\[16\]** *New.* Congress passed the Hong Kong Policy Act in 1992. The Human Rights and Democracy Act became law on November 27, 2019. The Autonomy Act became law on July 14, 2020. The Secretary of State's certification to Congress dates from May 27, 2020. Joshua Wong and Denise Ho testified before the Congressional-Executive Commission on China in September 2019.

**\[17\]** *New.* Lu Kang spoke at the ministry's regular press conference on June 30, 2017. Reuters and the South China Morning Post reported his remarks and the Foreign Office reply the same day.

**\[18\]** *New.* The platforms announced their pauses on July 6 and 7, 2020. Contemporary reports in Forbes, Fortune and CNBC name them. TikTok withdrew from Hong Kong in the same week.

**\[19\]** *New.* The President signed Executive Order 13936 on July 14, 2020. OFAC designated eleven individuals under it on August 7, 2020. The State Department filed its Section 5(a) report under the Autonomy Act on October 14, 2020. Treasury filed its first Section 5(b) report on December 11, 2020. Its second followed in May 2021 and its third in February 2022. None of the three identified a foreign financial institution.

**\[20\]** *New.* HKSAR v Lai Chee Ying, \[2021\] HKCFA 3. The Court of Final Appeal gave judgment on February 9, 2021.

**\[21\]** *New.* The Appeal Committee of the Court of Final Appeal refused the Secretary for Justice leave to appeal on November 28, 2022. The Chief Executive reported to the central government that day. The Standing Committee interpreted Articles 14 and 47 of the law on December 30, 2022. The city's security committee issued its decision on the barrister on January 11, 2023.

**\[22\]** *New.* Statement of the Group of Seven foreign ministers on Hong Kong, June 17, 2020. Joint statement of the Five Eyes foreign ministers, November 18, 2020.

**\[23\]** *New.* The Prime Minister's article ran in The Times and the South China Morning Post on June 3, 2020, and the government republished it. The government announced the route on July 1, 2020. Interim leave at the border began on July 15, 2020. The full route opened on January 31, 2021.

**\[24\]** *New.* HSBC disclosed Peter Wong's signature and its own statement on WeChat on June 3, 2020. Standard Chartered issued its statement the same week. Fortune and Al Jazeera reported both, and Al Jazeera reported the banks' dependence on Asian revenue.

**\[25\]** *New.* Police obtained the freezes on Ted Hui's family accounts in December 2020. The South China Morning Post named HSBC, Hang Seng Bank and Bank of China.

**\[26\]** *New.* Prosecutors first charged Lai under Article 29 in December 2020. The High Court convicted him on December 15, 2025. It sentenced him on February 9, 2026. The UN High Commissioner for Human Rights commented on the verdict the day of sentencing.

**\[27\]** *New.* Canada and Australia suspended their extradition treaties with Hong Kong in early July 2020. Britain suspended its treaty and extended its arms embargo on July 20, 2020.

**\[28\]** *New.* The University of Hong Kong's council voted to dismiss Benny Tai on July 28, 2020.

**\[29\]** *New.* The citation is to *The Prince*, chapter 18, in the public-domain Marriott translation of 1908. The quoted words come from the chapter's opening paragraph, with an internal omission marked. **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case as the broken guarantee.

**Corrections to the first edition.** Five claims in the first edition did not survive a check against the record, and one framing needs narrowing.

*First arrests.* The first edition put them within the hour. The first arrest came shortly after midday on July 1, about thirteen hours after commencement. \[3\]

*Bank freezes.* The first edition said banks began freezing the accounts of designated organizations within four hours. No record supports the claim. The freezes documented here run from December 2020 through June 2021. \[8\] \[25\]

*App removals.* The first edition said protest apps left the local app store within eight hours. No record supports the claim. The record shows the rules of July 6, the platforms' pauses and TikTok's exit within the first week. \[4\] \[18\]

*Academic dismissals.* The first edition placed them in the first day. The University of Hong Kong's council dismissed Benny Tai on July 28, 2020, four weeks after commencement. \[28\]

*The forty-seven.* The first edition said the Party arrested them. Police arrested a larger group in January 2021, and prosecutors charged forty-seven of them in February. \[5\]

*One night.* The law's powers arrived in one night, and the dismantling took eighteen months. The Hong Kong section of *The Officer's Burden* repeats the first-arrest line and a forty-eight-hour framing, and both need the same correction.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The coordination.* The record documents every step. The timeline supports one design behind them, and no document states it.

*The wider aim.* The Architecture Vision argues it from what the Party did next.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The platform analogy.* Gap 6 reads documented articles of two laws as a platform with administrator rights. The articles are fact and the analogy is the architect's.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

*The guarantee line.* The claim that a guarantee its guarantor alone can revoke is a timing decision stands as a judgment.

*The Taiwan connection.* The historical record documents Deng Xiaoping's design of One Country, Two Systems as a model for Taiwan. The operation's effect on Taipei follows from events. Whether the Party intended that signal remains open.

**Narrowed or removed.**

The first edition called the operation the most efficient dismantling of a free society in the modern record. The comparison is gone, because the timeline makes the point without it. The ten-dimension walk became the principles audit in the Gap Analysis, and all ten dimensions survive there. The Huawei comparison is gone, because the defenders' clock supplies Hong Kong's own evidence of sequential defense.

### Technology IP

*This chapter's Documented and Argued labels stay inline in the chapter text. Only the citations move here.*

**Primary documents.** Notes marked *New* enter in this edition. The rest carry forward from the first edition.

**\[1\]** Justice Department releases describe Fujian Jinhua as a state-owned enterprise of the People's Republic. **Verified.** The Commerce Department's Entity List notice of October 29, 2018 states that Jinhua was completing a $5.7 billion production facility, the source of the funding figure. Reuters reporting from the same week gives the same figure.

**\[2\]** *New.* The Justice Department release of October 28, 2020 describes the two engineers and the Micron file used to adjust UMC's design rules.

**\[3\]** *New.* UMC's own filings with the SEC record the Taichung indictment of August 31, 2017 and Micron's civil suit of December 5, 2017. The Taichung District Court ruled on June 12, 2020. It fined UMC NT$100 million and sentenced three engineers to terms of four and a half to six and a half years. The retrial in January 2022 cut the fine to NT$20 million and acquitted one engineer. It reduced the other two sentences to six months and one year with probation. UMC's statement of October 2020 gives its account of the district court's findings. Micron's counsel took part in that trial, by UMC's account.

**\[4\]** *New.* UMC's SEC filings record that it filed its Fuzhou suits on January 12, 2018. Jinhua filed its own. The court granted the injunction on July 3, 2018. Micron's statement of July 5, 2018 records its objection that the court ruled before hearing its defense. UMC withdrew its Fuzhou complaints on November 26, 2021 under its settlement with Micron.

**\[5\]** Commerce Department Entity List addition for Fujian Jinhua, announced October 29, 2018. *New.* The Bureau of Industry and Security issued its semiconductor rules on October 7, 2022.

**\[6\]** Prosecutors filed the indictment in *United States v. United Microelectronics Corporation* under seal on September 27, 2018. The court unsealed it on November 1, 2018. *New.* The Attorney General announced the China Initiative that day. Assistant Attorney General Matthew Olsen ended it on February 23, 2022. Reporting at the time counted more than seventy prosecutions under it.

**\[7\]** UMC pleaded guilty on October 28, 2020 to one count under 18 U.S.C. § 1832(a)(3). It paid a $60 million fine and agreed to cooperate. **Corrected.** The first edition called the plea one to trade secret theft generally.

**\[8\]** Judge Maxine M. Chesney acquitted Fujian Jinhua after a bench trial on February 27, 2024. **Verified.** Micron and Jinhua settled in December 2023, and Micron and UMC settled in November 2021.

**\[9\]** *New.* The Cyberspace Administration of China opened its review on March 31, 2023 and announced its decision on May 21, 2023. Reuters reported on October 17, 2025 that Micron would stop supplying server chips to data centers on the mainland. The same report gave mainland revenue of $3.4 billion, or 12 percent of the total, and called Micron the first American chipmaker targeted. Contemporary reporting dates Micron's first plant on the mainland to 2007.

**\[10\]** *New.* State Council, National Medium- and Long-Term Program for Science and Technology Development, 2006 to 2020. State Council, Made in China 2025, issued in May 2015. National Intelligence Law of June 27, 2017, Article 7.

**\[11\]** *New.* The Trade Representative initiated the Section 301 investigation in August 2017 and published its report on March 22, 2018. The four-year review appeared on May 14, 2024. The Federal Register of September 18, 2024 summarizes its findings.

**\[12\]** Bell Labs announced the silicon solar cell in 1954. IEA, *Special Report on Solar PV Global Supply Chains* (2022). **Scoped** as in the first edition, with dominance stated and no point figure.

**\[13\]** *New.* The National Intellectual Property Administration issued its notice on patent application behavior on January 27, 2021. The Patent and Trademark Office published its study of non-market factors in mainland patent and trademark filings on January 13, 2021.

**\[14\]** *New.* The Justice Department indicted five officers of People's Liberation Army Unit 61398 in the Western District of Pennsylvania on May 19, 2014. The Thousand Talents Plan began in 2008.

**\[15\]** *New.* The grand jury indicted Ding in March 2024 and returned a superseding indictment in February 2025. The jury convicted him on January 29, 2026 of seven counts of economic espionage and seven of trade secret theft. The Justice Department called it the first conviction on AI-related economic espionage charges.

**\[16\]** Pat Choate prepared *A Great Wall of Patents* as a working paper for the U.S.-China Economic and Security Review Commission on November 7, 2005. The electronics maker's account and the Japanese inquiry count are Choate's, drawn from Japanese press reporting. Both are secondary.

**\[17\]** *New.* American Inventors Protection Act of 1999. The non-publication request appears at 35 U.S.C. § 122(b)(2)(B)(i). The Invention Secrecy Act of 1951 appears at 35 U.S.C. §§ 181 to 188. The America Invents Act of 2011 made the United States first-inventor-to-file for applications filed from March 16, 2013.

**\[18\]** *New.* Holding Foreign Companies Accountable Act, signed December 18, 2020. The Public Company Accounting Oversight Board announced complete inspection access on December 15, 2022.

**\[19\]** *New.* The citation is to *The Art of War*, chapter 13, in the public-domain Giles translation of 1910. Sun Tzu titled the chapter The Use of Spies. **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case for the recruitment channel.

**\[20\]** *New.* The citation is to Franklin's *Autobiography*, written between 1771 and 1790 and first published in full in 1868, now in the public domain. The passage concerns Franklin's refusal of a patent for the Pennsylvania Fireplace, offered by Governor George Thomas around 1744. **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case for the Library With No Librarian gap.

**Corrections to the first edition.** Four claims did not survive a check against the record, and one omission changed the case.

*The Fuzhou injunction.* The first edition placed it during the American prosecution and credited Jinhua alone. UMC and Jinhua filed in January 2018, and the court ruled on July 3, 2018. That was four months before any American government action. \[4\]

*The plea.* UMC pleaded guilty to receiving and possessing a stolen trade secret, one count. \[7\]

*The AI channel.* The first edition's note dropped the replication intervals, but its text kept them. It also claimed that model weights are often reconstructable from a paper, with no source. Both claims are gone. The Ding conviction carries the AI channel instead. \[15\]

*The case length.* The first edition's five and a half years ran from the sealed indictment of September 2018. This edition says more than five years from unsealing, which the record supports either way.

*The Taiwan prosecution.* The first edition omitted it. Taiwan indicted UMC first, convicted it first and cut the sentences on retrial. UMC's account of that ruling agrees with the American acquittal on the point that matters. Neither court found that the secret reached Jinhua. \[3\]

**A cross-case correction for *The Borrowed Knife*.** Its patent section says the eighteen-month rule came from implementing the World Trade Organization agreement. The rule came from the American Inventors Protection Act of 1999. Its remedies ask Congress to restore the publication exemption, and the exemption already exists for inventors who file only at home. Choate's patent material now lives here, and that section can shrink to a cross-reference when the case is rebuilt. \[17\]

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The coordination.* The record documents every channel. One authority running them as one campaign is the framework's argument.

*The wider aim.* The Architecture Vision argues it from the published plans and from what the channels took.

*The map.* The precision of the targets supports a working model of Western technology. No document describes one.

*The retaliation.* Micron called the Fuzhou suits retaliation. Reuters reported that observers saw the 2023 ban as retaliation for American chip controls. Treating both as retaliation is the framework's reading.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**Narrowed or removed.**

The SK Hynix paragraph is gone, because its dates and pay multiples had no citable source. The GPT-2, ResNet and AlphaGo replication claims are gone for the same reason. The ten-dimension walk became the principles audit, and all ten dimensions survive there. The variable interest entity material now points to *The Borrowed Knife*, which carries it in full.

**\[10\]** Rhodium Group, *De-risking U.S. Securities Investment in China*. It estimates American holdings of about $1.4 trillion in 2020, falling below $700 billion in 2023.

The Hesai sources are the company's Form 20-F, the district court opinion of July 2025 and the FY2026 defense act's sanctions-harmonization reporting provision.

**\[13\]** National Intelligence Law of June 27, 2017, Article 7.

**\[14\]** DiDi Global's offering closed on June 30, 2021. The Cyberspace Administration announced its review on July 2 and ordered the app's removal on July 4. DiDi completed its New York delisting in June 2022. The Party's decree on private tutoring dates from July 2021.

**\[15\]** *New.* The White House issued the America First Investment Policy memorandum on February 21, 2025. Section 2(l) carries the commitments cited.

**\[16\]** *New.* The five state-owned enterprises announced their delisting on August 12, 2022. Contemporary reporting and the Commission's quarterly table record the exits.

**\[17\]** *New.* Executive Order 14105 dates from August 9, 2023. Treasury's implementing rules took effect on January 2, 2025.

**\[18\]** *New.* The House Select Committee on the Strategic Competition between the United States and the Chinese Communist Party released its bipartisan report on American financial institutions on April 18, 2024. The report uses the word facilitated.

**\[20\]** *New.* The Financial Times reported the Alibaba unit stake completed on January 4, 2023. Reuters reported the Tencent unit stake of October 18, 2023 from the Qichacha registry, at a price of 600,000 yuan. Reuters reported the ByteDance stake and its board seat in 2021. The owning fund traces to the China Internet Investment Fund, set up by the cyberspace regulator and the finance ministry.

**\[21\]** *New.* The Company Law of the People's Republic requires companies to host Party organizations and to provide the conditions for their activities.

**\[22\]** *New.* The citation is to Julius Caesar's *Commentarii de Bello Gallico*, Book I, sections 31 to 32 and section 40. Diviciacus's appeal and Caesar's response to Ariovistus. Quotation from the McDevitte and Bohn translation (New York, 1869), public domain. The Aedui's later revolt under Vercingetorix and defeat at Alesia in 52 BC appear in Book VII.

**Corrections and changes from the first edition.**

*The patent channel.* The first edition ran two channels in one case. The patent channel and Choate's paper now live in *Technology IP*, and this case carries the capital channel alone. The move also retires the old remedy to restore a publication exemption the law already provides.

*The two lists.* The first edition said the 1260H designation carried no investment restriction until 2026. The fuller record shows two lists. Treasury's list bars investment, and Defense's list binds only procurement. Gap 3 now carries that distinction.

*The voice.* The first edition spoke in the first person plural and used Beijing as the actor. This edition names the Party and the collaborators and speaks of neither as "we."

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The design.* The record documents the Party's wall, its approval regime and its secrecy law. That the Party used the American capital market as one designed instrument is the framework's argument.

*The plum tree.* The record documents the timing of the state-owned exits. Reading them as a deliberate sacrifice is the framework's argument.

*The slope.* The record documents the fee structure. The claim that it, rather than any agent, carried the operation is the framework's argument.

*The collaborators.* The word carries its plain sense of working on the same deals. The record documents the work and the fees. It shows no one working for the Party.

*The flywheel and the gift.* The record documents the compensation, the special management shares and the committee's findings. The flywheel and the reading of the capital as a gift in control terms are the framework's arguments.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument, and every open question is a ground for inquiry rather than a finding.

**Narrowed or removed.**

The first edition compared the VIE flow to the CHIPS Act and to venture funding for American AI, and it claimed a flat return on the China benchmark from 2010 to 2024. Those comparisons had no citable basis in the chapter and are gone. The estimate that share-based compensation across the cohort passed one hundred billion dollars is gone too. The cited filings carry the point without it. The typical underwriting spread of four to seven percent is gone, because the largest offerings paid far less. The ten-dimension walk became the principles audit, and all ten dimensions survive there.

**\[15\]** Executive Order 14238 of March 14, 2025. Judge Royce Lamberth ruled in April 2025 and blocked a reduction in force of 532 jobs in September 2025. An appeals court stayed parts of his order in May 2025. The record found for this edition shows the dispute unresolved.

**\[17\]** *New.* The citation is to Timothy L. Thomas's "Russia's Reflexive Control Theory and the Military," in the *Journal of Slavic Military Studies*, volume 17 (2004). **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case for the target audience note.

**\[18\]** *New.* The Otrar caravan massacre of 1218 and Genghis Khan's subsequent ultimatums to Khwarezmian cities are documented across *The Secret History of the Mongols* and Juvaini's *History of the World Conqueror* (13th century), and corroborated by independent secondary accounts of the Mongol invasion of Khwarezmia. No specific wording of any ultimatum letter is quoted here, since no version found for this edition could be traced cleanly to a primary translation. **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case for the target audience note.

**\[23\]** *New numbering.* The Global Engagement Center's statutory authorization lapsed in December 2024.

**\[24\]** Microsoft Threat Analysis Center published an East Asia threat report in April 2024 on AI-generated material around Taiwan's January 2024 election.

**Corrections and changes from the first edition.**

*The limits.* The first edition said the export version carries no limits. TikTok set a sixty-minute default for minors in March 2023. The contrast now rests on enforcement, a hard limit tied to real-name registration against a soft default.

*The intent language.* The first edition's opening said ByteDance engineered the export app to hold attention at any cost, and it called the contrast a confession. The sourcing note had already disclaimed that intent. The opening now matches the note.

*The deal.* The first edition described the divestiture. This edition adds the Party's export-control veto, the licensing of the algorithm and the Party's approval.

*The domain list.* The header now uses the fixed domain names in their fixed order.

**Removed and kept removed.** The first edition removed a widely shared comparison of Chinese and Western children's career ambitions, which traced to a single commercial survey with mismatched samples. It stays out.

**The merge.** This edition merges two first-edition chapters, *The Cognitive-Industrial Complex* and *Psychological Operations*, into one chapter for this edition. Both signature gaps survive the merge. No owner of the objective function, and the home audience has no owner. Both guardrails survive it too. Labels rather than removals, and nothing polices the lawful speech of the defenders' own citizens. Citations from both source files are renumbered into one sequence above. Footnote numbers in this appendix do not match either source file's own numbering.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*Design intent.* No public document shows engineers tuning either the platform or a specific campaign to degrade a rival population, as distinct from tuning for engagement or running a lawful operation with degradation as a byproduct. The strategic usefulness of the effect is the framework's argument.

*The Digital Twin.* The predictive model of a population's divisions follows from how recommendation systems work. Its use by the Party's networks, and by its target audience analysts, is the framework's argument.

*The objective function as governance.* The record documents the registry and the regulations. The reading of that record as a governance decision is the framework's.

*Whether one office runs both halves.* No public document shows whether the platform's objective function and the practitioner's target audience analysis sit under one office or separate arms of the same authority. This chapter's argument does not depend on which is true.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**The guardrails that must survive any edit.** Nothing in the target polices the lawful speech of the defenders' own citizens. Every element of the target architecture discloses, audits or labels. None of them removes lawful speech.

"Our principle is that the Party commands the gun, and the gun must never be allowed to command the Party." **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case for the management plane.

**Corrections and changes from the first edition.**

*The rail cars.* The first edition said the procurement triggered no national-security review. That held at the time of the award. Congress barred federal transit funds for such rail makers in December 2019.

*The management plane.* This edition adds the commissar system, the Party's reach into private firms, the four certificates and the defenders' lists.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The coordination.* The record documents the statutes and each channel. One engine running them is the framework's argument.

*The management plane.* The record documents the commissar system, the Party units and the certificates. Reading them as an out-of-band management plane is the framework's reading.

*The banality.* Arendt's term describes Eichmann's Germany. Applying it to the Party's bureaucracy is an analogy, and the chapter claims no equivalence of crimes.

*The lineage.* The judgment that the Party stands outside Sun Yat-sen's republican lineage is normative and historical.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**The guardrail that must survive any edit.** The subject is a program and a legal compulsion, never a community. The chapter opens and closes on the 442nd because the thought experiment carries the argument's ethical spine.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.
