### Protocols and Ports

*This chapter's Documented and Argued labels stay inline in the chapter text. Only the citations move here. This chapter merges the Rulebook and Ports and Cranes source files from the first edition, and its notes are renumbered from both into one sequence.*

**Primary documents.** Notes marked *New* enter in this edition. The rest carry forward from the first edition, renumbered.

**\[1\]** *Verified for this edition.* The 3GPP TSG RAN1 #87 meeting convened in Reno, Nevada. It ran November 14 to 18, 2016, and selected polar codes as the control-channel coding scheme for 5G enhanced mobile broadband. The selection is documented across multiple 3GPP working papers and patent filings referencing the meeting by number and location.

**\[2\]** The House Committee on Homeland Security and the Select Committee on the CCP released their joint investigative report on September 12, 2024. The Wall Street Journal first reported the modem findings in March 2024. The nearly 80 percent crane-share figure and the ZPMC, COSCO and China Merchants ties come from the same report.

**\[3\]** The Central Committee and the State Council issued the National Standardization Development Outline on October 10, 2021.

**\[4\]** The European Telecommunications Standards Institute's register of declared essential patents, and industry counts of 5G declarations.

**\[5\]** *Verified for this edition.* The Wuhan Intermediate People's Court's order in the Xiaomi and InterDigital dispute, September 2020. The Chongqing court's rate ruling in the OPPO and Nokia dispute, December 2023. The European Union filed its WTO request for consultations on February 18, 2022, over China's anti-suit injunctions in standard-essential patent disputes. The EU requested a WTO panel on December 7, 2022 after consultations failed.

**\[6\]** *Verified for this edition.* Zhao Houlin served as ITU Secretary-General from 2015 to 2022. The ITU elected Doreen Bogdan-Martin at its Plenipotentiary Conference in Bucharest on September 29, 2022. She won 139 votes to 25 for Russia's Rashid Ismailov, out of 172 member states present and voting.

**\[7\]** *Verified for this edition.* Huawei and state-owned partners submitted the New IP proposal to the ITU-T's Telecommunications Standardization Advisory Group in September 2019. Reporting on the proposal and on facial-recognition standards proposals followed in 2019 and 2020. The ITU-T's Study Group 13 and Study Group 11 plenary declined to accept New IP as a new work item in December 2020.

**\[8\]** The Trade Representative issued its Section 301 finding on January 16, 2025. Its fees took effect on October 14, 2025, the same day as the Party's Ministry of Transport fees on American-linked ships.

**\[9\]** The U.S.-China Economic and Security Review Commission published its staff brief on LOGINK on September 20, 2022.

**\[10\]** Commerce issued its general advisory opinion on August 19, 2019. It issued interim final rules in June 2020, September 2022 and July 2024. The 2022 rule's notice records the effect of the uncertainty on American participation.

**\[11\]** The defense act for fiscal 2024 carries the LOGINK prohibitions, effective six months after enactment.

**\[12\]** The White House fact sheet of November 1, 2025 announced the suspension of three instruments. The Section 301 port fees, the Commerce affiliates rule and the Party's rare-earth export rule all became effective November 10, 2025. All three are due to expire around November 10, 2026 absent further action.

**\[13\]** *New, updated through this edition's research.* CK Hutchison announced its agreement to sell its global port portfolio on March 4, 2025, naming the Panama Ports Company among the assets and valuing the transaction at $22.8 billion. China's State Administration for Market Regulation opened an antitrust review. Chinese state media and officials pressed for COSCO Shipping to take a stake, later reported as a demand for a controlling rather than minority position. Panama's Supreme Court ruled CK Hutchison's underlying concession for the Balboa and Cristóbal terminals unconstitutional, in a decision reported from late February 2026, removing the Panama terminals from the transaction. MSC's Terminal Investment Limited took over interim operation of the Cristóbal terminal under an eighteen-month contract. Reporting on the remaining forty-one ports shows negotiations continuing without a signed agreement, unresolved over Chinese state-carrier participation. A Financial Times report from early March 2026 and a trade-press report from September 7, 2026 both confirm the standoff. **This item should be checked again at final proof**, per this brief's own instruction on any claim stated as current.

**\[14\]** The White House issued the United States Government National Standards Strategy for Critical and Emerging Technology on May 4, 2023. Its implementation roadmap followed in July 2024.

**\[15\]** *New.* The citation is to Executive Order 14028, signed May 12, 2021. Its formal title is "Improving the Nation's Cybersecurity." Section 4(e) directs the NIST guidance that led to the software bill of materials requirement for federal software suppliers.

**Corrections and changes from the first edition.**

*The domain list.* Both source chapters' headers now use the fixed domain names in their fixed order, merged into one list.

*The merge.* This edition merges two first-edition chapters, *The Rulebook* and *Ports and Cranes*, into one Technology Architecture case for this edition. It reads standards as the logical layer and ports and cranes as the physical layer. Both signature gaps are kept. One sits at Architecture Principles, where export controls kept American engineers out of standards meetings for five years. The other sits at Technology, where the cranes carried a channel nobody requested. Both guardrails are kept too. No contribution is suspect because of its source, and crane workers are not agents.

*The Panama item.* The first edition's Ports and Cranes chapter described the CK Hutchison sale as pending Panama's approval. This edition updates the record through the Panama Supreme Court ruling and the ongoing renegotiation of the non-Panama portfolio, and flags the item as needing a final check before print given how quickly it continues to move.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The coordination, at both layers.* The record documents the national outline and the state enterprises' ownership. Reading a single coordinated design behind the standards delegations and the port enterprises alike is the framework's argument.

*The chain across altitudes.* That the same six gaps recur at the logical and physical layers is this chapter's organizing claim, not a documented finding.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**The guardrails that must survive any edit.** No contribution to a standards body is suspect because of its source, and a good standard is good engineering wherever it comes from. Crane workers, welders and engineers build excellent cranes at low prices, and none of them are agents. The case concerns the enterprises and the Party's strategy for them, never the people who staff them.

### Extract (COVID-19)

*This chapter's Documented and Argued labels stay inline in the chapter text. Only the citations move here.*

**Primary documents.** Notes marked *New* enter in this edition. The rest carry forward from the first edition.

**\[1\]** Wuhan police reprimanded Li Wenliang on January 3, 2020. He died on February 7, 2020. Reporting at the time and the Party's own later inquiry acknowledge both events.

**\[2\]** Zhang Yongzhen's release of the genome on January 11, 2020.

**\[3\]** Wuhan health authorities' statement of December 31, 2019. Taiwan's Centers for Disease Control notice to the WHO the same day.

**\[4\]** WHO statement of January 14, 2020. The director-general's 2017 election.

**\[5\]** The WHO declared a public health emergency of international concern on January 30, 2020. The director-general had met the Party's leader in Beijing on January 28.

**\[6\]** *New.* Foreign ministry spokesman Zhao Lijian's post of March 12, 2020.

**\[7\]** Associated Press investigation of WHO internal recordings, 2020.

**\[8\]** WHO-convened joint study, 2021, and the reported refusal of raw data.

**\[9\]** *New.* *Missouri ex rel. Bailey v. People's Republic of China* in the Eastern District of Missouri. Missouri filed on April 21, 2020, and the Eighth Circuit revived the hoarding claim in January 2024. Judge Stephen N. Limbaugh Jr. entered judgment on March 7, 2025. The Missouri Independent reported the quoted finding and the damages. In December 2025 the Wuhan government and co-plaintiffs filed suit in the Wuhan Intermediate People's Court, and the Missouri Attorney General published the diplomatic service packet on December 16, 2025. **Default record.** The court's findings rest on evidence no defendant contested. In 2026 two defendants appeared to challenge the judgment.

**\[10\]** *New.* Justice Department indictment of two hackers working with the Ministry of State Security, July 2020.

**\[11\]** The Party's trade measures against Australian barley and wine, 2020.

**\[12\]** *New.* Shanghai's lockdown began on March 28, 2022. The Shanghai Municipal Health Commission defended child separation on April 4, 2022. More than thirty governments protested in writing through the French consulate and the British embassy on March 31, 2022.

**\[13\]** *New.* CNN and Radio Free Asia reported the Henan health codes in June 2022. Zhengzhou's discipline inspection and supervision authorities announced the punishments and the count of 1,317 on June 22, 2022, as Sixth Tone and Reuters reported. Yicai reported the renewed red codes on July 8, 2022.

**\[14\]** *New.* Bian Zhongyun's death at the Girls' Middle School attached to Beijing Normal University, August 5, 1966. The death toll follows the treatment in *The Stakes*.

**\[15\]** *New.* The Urumqi fire of November 24, 2022 and the Guizhou quarantine bus crash of September 18, 2022. The National Health Commission eased the policy on December 7, 2022.

**\[16\]** *New.* National Assessment of Educational Progress, 2022 mathematics results. NIH Director Francis Collins's email of October 8, 2020, released under the Freedom of Information Act. Jay Bhattacharya's confirmation as NIH director in March 2025.

**\[17\]** *New.* Canada invoked the Emergencies Act on February 14, 2022. Justice Richard Mosley of the Federal Court ruled on January 23, 2024. The Federal Court of Appeal affirmed on January 16, 2026.

**\[18\]** *New.* *Roman Catholic Diocese of Brooklyn v. Cuomo*, November 25, 2020. *National Federation of Independent Business v. OSHA*, January 13, 2022.

**\[19\]** *New.* Zhang Hongbing's public apology in 2013 for denouncing his mother in 1970.

**\[20\]** *New.* Watson and colleagues estimated the deaths averted by vaccination in its first year in *The Lancet Infectious Diseases* in 2022.

**\[21\]** *New.* Mulligan and Arnott estimated non-COVID excess deaths at 97,000 a year from April 2020 through 2021 in *Inquiry* in 2022. A 2024 preprint on medRxiv estimated 114,230 in 2020 and 127,597 in 2021. Both warn that some deaths assigned to other causes involved COVID. Rates use a population of about 332 million.

**\[22\]** *New.* Xiao, Unger and colleagues published the Chinese estimate in *JAMA Network Open* in August 2023. The Party's official count of about 60,000 hospital deaths dates from January 2023.

**\[23\]** *New.* Walder and Su published the rural death estimates and their timing in *The China Quarterly* in 2003. The population base averages about 840 million across the decade. The sent-down youth numbered in the millions by official counts.

**\[24\]** *New.* The Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services restricted nursing home visits on March 13, 2020 and advised postponing elective procedures on March 18, 2020. The CDC's *Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report* of September 11, 2020 reported the 41 percent figure.

**\[25\]** *New.* A 2023 comparison of 42 European countries published through PubMed Central gives Sweden's cumulative excess. Sweden's Corona Commission reported on elder care on December 15, 2020. A 2024 study in the *Scandinavian Journal of Public Health* disputes how readers should interpret the three-year figure.

**\[26\]** *New.* Robert Fairlie measured the fall in active business owners in a National Bureau of Economic Research working paper in 2020. The military discharges ended when Congress repealed the vaccine mandate in the defense act for fiscal 2023.

**\[27\]** *New.* The CDC required negative tests for air travelers from January 26, 2021. A presidential proclamation required vaccination for most foreign air travelers from November 8, 2021. Title 42 governed the land border from March 2020 to May 2023.

**\[28\]** *New.* CDC National Center for Health Statistics data recorded 630,505 U.S. divorces in 2020, a rate of 2.3 per 1,000 people and a decline of nearly 16 percent from 2019, the largest single-year drop since at least 2000. The rate rose slightly to 2.5 per 1,000 in 2021, still below pre-pandemic years. Figures draw on Census Bureau population estimates.

**Corrections and changes from the first edition.**

*The domain list.* The first edition placed the capture of the WHO under Agency, a dimension, in its list of domains. This edition places it in the Association domain, where alliances and institutions belong.

*The Missouri judgment.* The first edition predates the judgment and the countersuit. Both now carry the Legal record.

*The comparison.* This edition adds the Cultural Revolution comparison and the Western column, with the differences stated.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The death rates.* The rates come from published estimates and census populations. Setting them side by side across regimes, and any share of the American deaths assigned to policy, is the framework's reading.

*The coordination.* The record documents each channel. One design running them is the framework's argument.

*The court's findings.* The Missouri findings on September 2019 knowledge and on deliberate suppression rest on an uncontested default record.

*The fourth age.* The claim that the health code is the class label's successor is the framework's reading.

*The comparison.* The rows record documented events. The reading of likeness and difference is the framework's argument, and the comparison claims no equivalence of scale.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**Narrowed or removed.**

The first edition described AI-driven sentiment analysis and bot networks without a primary source for either. This edition keeps the documented spokesman's post and describes the platform steering in general terms. The ten-dimension walk became the principles audit, and all ten dimensions survive there.

### Consume (Taiwan)

*This chapter's Documented and Argued labels stay inline in the chapter text. Only the citations move here.*

**Primary documents.** Notes marked *New* enter in this edition. The rest carry forward from the first edition.

**\[1\]** The Republic of China's Ministry of National Defense published daily reports on the air-defense identification zone for October 1 to 4, 2021. **Verified.** The ministry counted 149 sorties across the four days, a record at the time.

**\[2\]** The PLA oath of enlistment and the Party's doctrine of absolute leadership over the armed forces. The preamble of the People's Republic's constitution and successive Party congress reports state the doctrine.

**\[3\]** Economic Cooperation Framework Agreement, signed June 29, 2010. **Scoped** as in the first edition, with the magnitude of trade stated and no point figure.

**\[4\]** The National People's Congress adopted the Anti-Secession Law on March 14, 2005. Article 8 holds the triggers. *New.* Report to the Twentieth Party Congress, October 2022.

**\[5\]** Ministry of National Defense daily incursion series, 2023 annual compilation.

**\[6\]** *New.* Joint Sword-2024A ran on May 23 and 24, 2024. Joint Sword-2024B ran on October 14 and 15, 2024. Strait Thunder-2025A ran on April 1 and 2, 2025. Justice Mission-2025 ran on December 29 and 30, 2025. The Australian Strategic Policy Institute's tracker records the coast guard's first participation in May 2024. The Global Taiwan Institute's analyses record the stated triggers and the encirclement theme.

**\[7\]** *New.* The State Council's tariff commission announced the first suspension on December 21, 2023. It took effect on January 1, 2024. The commission announced the second on May 31, 2024, and it took effect on June 15. Taiwan's Mainland Affairs Council protested the second the same day.

**\[8\]** *New.* United Nations General Assembly Resolution 2758, October 25, 1971. The resolution does not mention Taiwan.

**\[9\]** *New, updated through this edition's research.* Taiwan's prosecutors brought the charges on June 10, 2025. The four defendants were Huang Chu-jung, Ho Jen-chieh, Chiu Shih-yuan and Wu Shang-yu. The Taipei District Court convicted and sentenced all four on September 25, 2025. Terms ran from four to ten years, the heaviest against Huang for espionage, organizing a criminal network for China and money laundering. The Taiwan High Court ruled on appeal on June 25, 2026. It reduced Huang's sentence to six years, Chiu's to five and Wu's to three, and acquitted Ho outright. The court did not immediately publish its reasoning. The United States Indo-Pacific Command's legal update of June 13, 2025 recorded the original charges.

**\[10\]** *New.* The Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency issued its joint advisory on Volt Typhoon on February 7, 2024.

**\[11\]** Taiwan FactCheck Center and Doublethink Lab documentation of synthetic media around the January 2024 presidential election.

**\[12\]** The Party's customs administration suspended Taiwanese pineapple imports in March 2021 and grouper imports in June 2022.

**\[13\]** Republic of China Ministry of Foreign Affairs record of diplomatic allies. **Updated.** Taiwan's foreign ministry confirmed twelve allies in December 2025.

**\[14\]** *New.* Taiwan Relations Act, April 10, 1979.

**\[15\]** *New.* Taiwan Security Monitor at George Mason University, arms sale backlog updates from June 2025 to January 2026. Its January 2026 update records the stalled special budget. Taiwan's defense ministry reports to the legislature supply the status of each case.

**\[16\]** *New.* Hesai's annual report on Form 20-F records its February 2023 offering and its designation of January 31, 2024. Hesai challenged the listing on May 13, 2024. Judge Paul L. Friedman of the District Court for the District of Columbia upheld it in July 2025. His opinion describes the bar on Defense contracting from 2026. Senators Rick Scott, Marsha Blackburn and Bernie Moreno wrote to the SEC and Treasury in September 2025. **Corrected.** The first edition implied the listing and the designation ran at the same time.

**\[17\]** TSMC financial disclosures and industry reporting. **Scoped** as in the first edition. The share refers to leading-edge logic, not to semiconductors generally. *New.* TSMC's first Arizona plant began volume production in late 2024.

**\[18\]** *New.* The administration notified Congress of more than $11 billion in sales on December 17, 2025, the largest package on record. The guided-bomb sale dates from 2017 and the F-16 Block 70/72 sale from 2019. Taiwan Security Monitor records the delays.

**\[19\]** Taiwan National Communications Commission reports on the Matsu cable damage, February 2023. **Attribution note** carried forward. Formal attribution in the individual incidents remains unresolved.

**\[20\]** *New.* The Hong Tai 58 cut the Taiwan and Penghu No. 3 cable on February 25, 2025. The court convicted its captain on June 12, 2025 and sentenced him to three years. The appeal failed. A later civil ruling awarded Chunghwa Telecom about NT$18.2 million, roughly $570,000.

**Corrections to the first edition.**

*Hesai.* The first edition said Defense designated Hesai while the SEC let it raise capital. The offering came a year before the designation. The designation's defect is that it binds one owner, and Gap 3 now says so. \[16\]

*The trade lever.* The first edition said the Party held the trade agreement over Taiwan without ever speaking the threat. The Party suspended concessions openly in December 2023 and May 2024. \[7\]

*The allies.* The first edition said roughly a dozen. The count is twelve. \[13\]

*The espionage case.* The prior draft carried only the June 2025 charges, flagged as resting on one secondary source. This edition traces the case through conviction in September 2025 and the High Court's appeal in June 2026, which acquitted one of the four defendants. \[9\]

*The domain names.* The header now uses the fixed names AI and Cognitive and Data and Surveillance.

*The cross-reference.* The mapping's group placement now points to *The Code*, which replaces the old part number.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The coordination.* The record documents every component. One design running them is the framework's argument.

*The regime-survival thesis.* The claim that Taiwan threatens the Party's legitimacy rather than its territory is the framework's reading.

*The manufactured enmity.* Wide reporting documents the Party's policing of the Republic of China's name. The inference that the hostility belongs to the state and not the nation is the framework's argument.

*The triggers.* The Party's statements name the trigger for each exercise, and the record documents the dates of all six answers. Reading the tariff timing as political and the exercises as packages built in advance is the framework's argument.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**Narrowed or removed.**

The first edition gave Resonance a roughly threefold amplification. The figure belongs to the model in *The Physics* and no longer appears in this case. The ten-dimension walk became the principles audit, and all ten dimensions survive there. The complete mapping survives unchanged.

### Financial Control

*This chapter's Documented and Argued labels stay inline in the chapter text. Only the citations move here.*

**Primary documents.** Notes marked *New* enter in this edition. The rest carry forward from the first edition.

**\[1\]** The Shanghai Stock Exchange suspended Ant's listing on November 3, 2020, with trading due on November 5. **Corrected in the first edition** from hours to two days. The State Administration for Market Regulation fined Alibaba in April 2021. *New.* The People's Bank of China fined Ant 7.123 billion yuan on July 7, 2023.

**\[2\]** *New.* The Financial Times reported the credit-data order in 2021. Ant disclosed Ma's loss of control in January 2023.

**\[3\]** People's Bank of China rate schedules and the academic literature on Chinese household savings.

**\[4\]** Joint memoranda of the National Development and Reform Commission and the Supreme People's Court on blacklists. **Scoped.** The case rejects the single-score caricature.

**\[5\]** People's Bank of China Digital Currency Research Institute publications on the digital yuan. **Scoped.** Full integration of rail and record does not yet run at national scale.

**\[6\]** The Bank of England published *Money Creation in the Modern Economy* in its Quarterly Bulletin for the first quarter of 2014.

**\[7\]** *New.* The Treasury, the Federal Reserve and the FDIC announced the systemic-risk exception for Silicon Valley Bank on March 12, 2023.

**\[8\]** Operation Choke Point, 2013 to 2017, with House Oversight findings and FDIC policy reversals.

**\[9\]** Canada invoked the Emergencies Act in February 2022. *New.* The Federal Court ruled in January 2024, and the Federal Court of Appeal affirmed in January 2026.

**\[10\]** The British debanking episode of July 2023, the chief executive's resignation and the Financial Conduct Authority's review.

**\[11\]** *New.* Executive Order 14282 of April 23, 2025. The Education Department's report of October 2020 on Section 117 compliance. The DETERRENT Act passed the House on a vote of 241 to 169 in March 2025.

**\[12\]** *New.* Institute of International Education, *Open Doors* 2024, for the 2023 and 2024 academic year. **Verified.** The report gives 277,398 students from the mainland, second to India's 331,602.

**\[13\]** *New.* CHIPS and Science Act of 2022, section 10632. America First Investment Policy memorandum, February 21, 2025.

**\[14\]** *New.* The Comptroller of the Currency ended reputation-risk examinations on March 20, 2025. The Federal Reserve followed in June 2025. Executive Order 14331 of August 7, 2025. Contemporary legal analyses record both the support and the criticism.

**\[15\]** *New.* Executive Order 14178 of January 23, 2025.

**\[16\]** *New.* The GENIUS Act became law on July 18, 2025.

**\[17\]** *New.* The citation is to Napoleon's Berlin Decree of November 21, 1806, in the standard English translation collected by the George Mason University Center for History and New Media. **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case for economic war by closing a continent's ports.

**Corrections and changes from the first edition.**

*The Ant sequel.* The first edition ended with the suspension and the fine on Alibaba. This edition adds the credit-data order, Ma's loss of control, the 2023 fine and his appearance at the 2025 symposium.

*The free world's switch.* The first edition warned of a Western central-bank digital currency. The United States has since barred one by order and built a freeze capability into private stablecoins instead. The warning now follows the switch to where it went.

*Education capture.* This edition adds the university material, built on funding flows only.

*The domain list.* The header now uses the fixed domain names in their fixed order.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The integration.* The record documents each of the Party's instruments. Reading them as one machine is the framework's argument.

*The free world's drift.* The forecast that the free world could build the same system one convenience at a time is the framework's argument. Readers who trust design safeguards disagree with a forecast rather than a fact.

*The university gradient.* The record documents the funding flows. The claim that dependence shapes institutional choices without instruction is the framework's reading.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**The guardrail that must survive any edit.** The symmetry is in the mechanism. The difference is in the accountability, and the accountability is the only thing. Both halves of the case carry weight. Cutting the free world's half would turn the case into propaganda, and cutting the accountability would turn it into false equivalence.

### The Pen From the Inside

*This chapter's Documented and Argued labels stay inline in the chapter text. Only the citations move here.*

**Primary documents.** Notes marked *New* enter in this edition. The rest carry forward from the first edition.

**\[1\]** The Thousand Talents Plan dates from 2008. The FBI's headquarters took control of the response in 2018.

**\[2\]** *New.* The NBA episode of October 2019 and HSBC's disclosure of June 3, 2020, as recorded in the *Hong Kong* case.

**\[3\]** National Intelligence Law of 2017. *New.* The Organization Department's figure for foreign-invested firms, October 2017. The special management shares appear in *The Borrowed Knife*.

**\[4\]** *New.* The Micron record appears in *Technology IP*. The VIE figures appear in *The Borrowed Knife*.

**\[5\]** *New.* The PLA officers' indictment of May 19, 2014 names SolarWorld. **Verified.** The German parent company SolarWorld AG filed for insolvency on May 10, 2017, citing price erosion and no positive forecast. TikTok's user figure comes from its statements to Congress.

**\[6\]** *New.* **Verified.** Marriott fired customer-engagement employee Roy Jones in January 2018 after his account liked a Friends of Tibet tweet. Chinese authorities ordered Marriott's website and app suspended in China for one week over the same episode. Paramount's trailer for the *Top Gun* sequel in July 2019 replaced the Taiwanese and Japanese flags on the hero's jacket with unmarked patches. The film's May 2022 release restored them after Tencent withdrew as an investor in late 2019. Apple removed HKmap.live from its App Store on October 10, 2019, one day after the Party's *People's Daily* published a commentary attacking the app.

**\[7\]** *New.* Karl Marx wrote the passage in chapter 10 of the first volume of *Capital*. The Moore and Aveling translation of 1887 supplies the wording. The work is in the public domain.

**\[8\]** National Security Decision Directive 189, September 21, 1985. Senate Permanent Subcommittee on Investigations, *Threats to the U.S. Research Enterprise*, November 2019.

**\[9\]** *New.* Executive Order 14282 of April 23, 2025, recording the investigations of nineteen campuses from 2019 to 2021.

**\[10\]** *New.* The audit record appears in *The Borrowed Knife*.

**\[11\]** The growth figures for the Party's forces from 1937 to 1945. **Scoped.** The widely repeated allocation of seventy, twenty and ten percent attributed to Mao has contested provenance and carries no weight here.

**\[12\]** The House Select Subcommittee on the Coronavirus Pandemic issued its final report on December 4, 2024. The case relies only on the narrower findings neither the majority nor the minority report disputes.

**\[13\]** Douglas Pike published *Viet Cong* with MIT Press in 1966.

**\[14\]** Andrew Mertha published *Brothers in Arms* with Cornell University Press in 2014.

**\[15\]** *New.* The Office of the Director of National Intelligence counts eighteen organizations in the Intelligence Community since 2021. Presidential Policy Directive 21 names sixteen critical infrastructure sectors.

**\[16\]** *New.* The Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency's joint advisory on Volt Typhoon, February 7, 2024. The balloon episode of February 2023.

**\[17\]** Americans for Public Trust's FOIA release of CDC and union correspondence on the February 2021 guidance, and later congressional testimony. Both institutions' responses appear in the text.

**\[18\]** Secretary of Defense memorandum of August 24, 2021. Section 525 of the defense act for fiscal 2023, signed December 23, 2022. Rescission memorandum of January 10, 2023. Executive Order 14184 of January 27, 2025. *New.* The Department of Homeland Security announced the reinstatement of 56 Coast Guard members in 2025.

**\[19\]** Recruiting analysis from the Department of Defense and the service recruiting commands. **This case declines the monocausal claim, and that refusal carries weight.**

**\[20\]** *New.* The citation is to Matthew 4:9, King James Version, public domain. The parallel account appears at Luke 4:5 to 8. **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case as the lifecycle's opener.

**\[21\]** *New.* The citation is to the Byzantine-Venetian treaty of 1082, an imperial chrysobull of Alexios I Komnenos. Peter Frankopan's 2004 article in the *Journal of Medieval History* argues for a date of 1092 instead, a minority position among specialists. Manuel I Komnenos ordered the arrest of Venetians throughout the empire in March 1171. The Fourth Crusade sacked Constantinople in April 1204. **Classical anchor**, mapped to this case for the full lifecycle across generations.

**Corrections and changes from the first edition.**

*The count of agencies.* The first edition said seventeen intelligence agencies. The Intelligence Community has counted eighteen organizations since 2021.

*The lifecycle and the vampire model.* This edition adds both models and maps them to the security lifecycle.

*The reinstatements.* This edition adds the 2025 order's Coast Guard results and notes the absence of a Defense-wide total.

*The Phase contrast.* The first edition compared the discharge sequence with a Hong Kong operation of five domains in forty-eight hours. The *Hong Kong* case narrowed that framing, and this edition drops the comparison.

**Held for a later volume.** A case on sole-source accreditation as a monopoly gate remains reserved by authorial decision.

**Stated as inference, not finding.**

*The lifecycle.* The record documents each example at each stage. Reading them as one lifecycle is the framework's argument.

*The vampire model.* The six traits are an analogy for a method and an architecture. They describe no people, and the Chinese people were the method's first host.

*The node.* The FOIA record documents the exchange. Its reading as coherence beating mass is the framework's argument.

*The domestic cases.* The record documents the dates and counts. The reading of self-consumption is the framework's argument, and it takes no position on the vaccine or on the causes of the recruiting shortfall.

*The gap readings.* Every paragraph marked Argued in the Gap Analysis belongs here, and so does the principles audit.

*The target.* Every element of the target architecture is argument.

**The discipline that must survive any edit.** Case One sets aside the medical question. Case Two states its weakness first. An edit that strengthened either case by taking a side would turn a structural finding into a partisan claim and lose the readers the chapter most needs.

## A Note on What Comes Next {.unnumbered}

This book closed with a chapter for the generation that builds the fix. It closes the back matter with the same offer extended to every other reader.

The roadmap in Appendix F is not this book's property. It is a list of jobs nobody is currently doing, addressed to whichever reader has the standing to do one of them. A reader in government can raise Appendix F's Now column with the office that already holds the authority to act on it, this quarter, without waiting for a bill to pass. A reader in industry, in a university, or in a newsroom can find the gap nearest their own seat and start closing it before anyone asks them to.
